# Ask C4S — Chrome extension

Right-click any selected text, use slash commands, or click the floating button on SAP C4C pages to ask C4S. Side panel + popup, markdown-rendered streaming replies, one-click write-back to SAP, draft customer emails, DOM scraping, Google SSO. Connects to your local or deployed Ask C4S shim.

**v0.4** — adds Chrome side panel mode (persistent UX, no more popup-dismiss-on-blur), one-click *"Apply to ticket"* / *"Save as note"* writebacks to SAP, *"Draft customer reply"* (LLM-drafted email opened in your mail client), markdown rendering of replies, and keyboard shortcuts (Ctrl+Shift+Y → popup, Ctrl+Shift+U → side panel).

## Features

- **Side panel + popup**: click the toolbar icon for the transient popup, hit `Ctrl+Shift+U` (`Cmd+Shift+U` on Mac) — or the *"↗ Side panel"* footer link — to pop out into Chrome's persistent side panel. Same UI, fills the panel height, doesn't dismiss when you click back into SAP.
- **Keyboard shortcut**: `Ctrl+Shift+Y` (`Cmd+Shift+Y`) opens the popup with the active ticket pre-loaded. Remap at `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`.
- **Markdown rendering**: bold, italic, code, fenced blocks, headers, bullets, numbered lists, links — all rendered as HTML in the reply box. Dependency-free, XSS-safe (HTML-escapes first, then re-introduces only the allowed tags).
- **One-click writeback to SAP**: when a `/triage` reply parses out a Priority or Next-action, an *"Apply"* button row appears. One click POSTs to `update_ticket_priority` / `add_service_request_text` via the Ask C4S shim → SAP OData. Maps LLM strings (Low/Medium/High/Urgent) to SAP `ServicePriorityCode` (7/3/2/1). Status reported inline.
- **Draft customer reply**: button appears whenever a ticket context is detected. Kicks off a second LLM call to translate the internal triage into a polished customer-facing email, then opens `mailto:` with the body prefilled (and copies to clipboard as a fallback for long emails).
- **Right-click context menu**: *"Ask C4S about &lt;selection&gt;"* on any page → popup pre-filled with that text + source URL
- **In-page floating button**: appears on SAP C4C ticket pages → one-click access with the live page DOM scraped and shipped as LLM context
- **DOM scraping**: when opened from a SAP tab, the popup automatically attaches: page title, main heading, up to 30 labelled fields, and 2 KB of visible body text. A 📄 badge below the prompt shows it's attached; click to remove for this call.
- **Auto-detected ticket ID**: parses the active tab URL for SAP ObjectIDs / ticket numbers and pre-fills `Summarize ticket <ID>`
- **Slash commands**: `/triage`, `/similar`, `/parts`, `/history`, `/notes`, `/help` — each maps directly to a shim tool
- **Streaming responses**: replies render word-by-word as Claude generates them via `POST /api/chat/stream` (Server-Sent Events). Falls back to plain JSON when the stream endpoint is unavailable.
- **Prompt history**: last 10 questions stored in `chrome.storage.local`; click any to re-ask
- **Google SSO**: server-validated; session token persisted in the extension. Replaces manual Bearer-token paste for SSO-enabled deployments.
- **Configurable endpoint**: defaults to `http://127.0.0.1:3031`; point at `askc4s.example.com` or your prod URL via the options page
- **Branded icons**: 16/48/128 PNGs generated from `icons/generate.mjs` (no design tool needed)

## Load it locally (unpacked)

1. Open `chrome://extensions/`
2. Toggle **Developer mode** (top-right)
3. Click **Load unpacked**
4. Select this `chrome-extension/` directory
5. Click the extension's puzzle-piece icon in the toolbar → pin "Ask C4S" if you want it permanently visible

## Setting up Google SSO

The extension ships with a placeholder OAuth client ID. To enable real Sign-in:

### 1. Create a Google OAuth client

1. Go to https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials
2. Pick your project (or create one)
3. Click **Create credentials → OAuth client ID**
4. Application type: **Chrome Extension** ← important
5. Name: `Ask C4S`
6. Item ID: paste your **extension ID** (find it in `chrome://extensions/` — the long string under "Ask C4S" after you've loaded the extension)
7. Save → copy the generated **Client ID**

### 2. Wire it into the extension

Edit `chrome-extension/manifest.json`:

```json
"oauth2": {
  "client_id": "PASTE_YOUR_CLIENT_ID_HERE.apps.googleusercontent.com",
  "scopes": ["openid", "email", "profile"]
}
```

Reload the extension in `chrome://extensions/`.

### 3. Configure the server allowlist (recommended)

In `.env`:

```
ASKC4S_AUTH_ALLOWED_DOMAINS=example.com,acmecorp.com
```

This is a comma-separated allowlist of email domains. Only signed-in users whose email belongs to one of these domains get a session token. Leave the var unset to allow any verified Google account (fine for solo dev; not recommended for prod).

Restart the shim: `node dist/uiServer.js`

### 4. Sign in

1. Open the extension options (right-click toolbar icon → Options, or ⚙ in the popup)
2. Click **Sign in with Google** → Google consent screen → your account
3. The server validates against `/api/auth/google` and issues a session token
4. The popup header now shows your name in a small pill

The session token is stored in `chrome.storage.sync` (cross-device with your Chrome profile) and auto-refreshes by re-running the flow when it expires (24h default).

## Slash commands

Type any of these at the start of your prompt:

| Command | What it does | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `/help` | List available commands | `/help` |
| `/triage [ticket]` | LLM-triage a ticket → priority + category + next action + SLA risk | `/triage 3553044` (or just `/triage` when on a C4C tab) |
| `/similar [query]` | Vector-similarity search across the local embedding index | `/similar pump bearing replacement` |
| `/parts [product]` | Top stocked parts for a product hint | `/parts centrifugal pump` |
| `/history [ticket]` | Service-history group summary | `/history 3553044` |
| `/notes [ticket]` | All ServiceRequestText rows on a ticket | `/notes 3553044` |

If you don't pass a ticket arg and the active tab is a C4C ticket page, the extension auto-substitutes the detected ticket ID.

## DOM scraping

When you click the floating "✨ Ask C4S" button on a SAP C4C page OR open the popup from a SAP tab via the toolbar icon, the extension:

1. Walks the page DOM looking for SAP UI5 labels (`.sapMLabel`, etc.) and pairs each with its sibling value
2. Captures up to 30 labelled fields, the page title, the main heading, and ~2 KB of visible body text
3. Skips `<script>`, `<style>`, hidden elements
4. Ships the snapshot as `context` on the `/api/chat` call

A 📄 badge appears below the textarea: *"Page context attached (N fields · ~K KB)"*. Click it to remove the context for this call (useful for ad-hoc questions where the page isn't relevant).

The LLM sees a prompt like:
```
Page context (scraped from SAP UI):
URL: https://my339699.crm.ondemand.com/...
Tab title: ServiceRequest 3553044
Heading: Pump head leaking — flow out of spec

Visible fields:
  • Status: In Process
  • Priority: High
  • Customer: ACME Industrial
  • ...

Visible body text (truncated):
[~2 KB of deduped visible text]

User question: <your prompt>
```

## Streaming responses

The popup calls `POST /api/chat/stream` first. Server proxies Anthropic's stream and emits Server-Sent Events:

```
data: {"type":"delta","text":"Hi"}\n\n
data: {"type":"delta","text":" there"}\n\n
data: {"type":"done","model":"claude-sonnet-4-5","usage":{...}}\n\n
data: [DONE]\n\n
```

Each delta appends to the reply box as it arrives. If the server returns 404 / 405 (older shim without the stream endpoint), the popup falls back to plain `/api/chat`.

## In-page button

When you visit any of these hosts, a floating "✨ Ask C4S" badge appears in the bottom-right:

- `*.crm.ondemand.com` (SAP C4C / Service Cloud)
- `*.crm.sap.com`
- `*.c4c.sap.com`
- `*.cloudfoundry.io`

Click → popup opens with the URL parsed for a ticket ID + full DOM snapshot attached. A small **×** hides the badge for the current tab.

## Icons

Generated by `icons/generate.mjs` — a zero-dependency Node script that builds 16/48/128px PNGs directly via raw byte construction + `node:zlib`. To regenerate:

```bash
node chrome-extension/icons/generate.mjs
```

**v0.9.9**: replaced the legacy V mark with a 4-point sparkle ✨ — the universal AI symbol used by Anthropic, Notion AI, Microsoft Copilot, etc. Same dark rounded-square tile and cyan→blue→violet→pink gradient palette. At 48px+, two smaller satellite sparkles sit in opposite corners (cyan top-left, pink bottom-right) for visual rhythm; at 16px the satellites are suppressed to keep the toolbar icon clean.

## Side panel mode

Chrome 114+ ships a built-in **side panel** that pins to the right edge of the browser window. Same UI as the popup, but it stays open while you click around SAP. Three ways to open it:

1. Press `Ctrl+Shift+U` (`Cmd+Shift+U` on Mac) anywhere in Chrome
2. Click *"↗ Side panel"* in the popup footer (closes the popup, opens the panel)
3. Click *"↗ Side panel"* on any reply (in the action button row)

The same `popup.html` is reused via `?mode=sidepanel` query string — body CSS just expands to fill the panel height. No separate file to maintain.

## One-click writeback

When `/triage` returns a structured reply (Priority / Category / Next action / SLA risk), the popup parses it and surfaces *Apply* buttons:

- **✓ Apply priority: High** → `POST /api/tools/update_ticket_priority` with `servicePriorityCode=2` (mapping: `Urgent→1, High→2, Medium→3, Low→7`)
- **✓ Save as next-action note** → `POST /api/tools/add_service_request_text` with `typeCode=10022` (Service Response Report)
- **✉ Draft customer reply** → second LLM call → `mailto:` with the body prefilled

All writebacks send `dryRun: false` and `acknowledgeProduction: true`, so they commit immediately on the live SAP tenant. Status (success / failure / error message) renders inline below the buttons.

## Permissions

| Permission | Used for |
|---|---|
| `contextMenus` | Right-click "Ask C4S" item |
| `storage` | Endpoint + manual token + SSO session token + history |
| `activeTab` + `tabs` | Read the active tab's URL for ticket ID detection + send `scrape-page` message to content script |
| `scripting` | Reserved for future "scrape any tab on demand" |
| `identity` | Google SSO via `chrome.identity.getAuthToken` |
| `sidePanel` | Open Chrome's persistent side panel via `chrome.sidePanel.open()` |
| `clipboardWrite` | Copy reply / draft email to clipboard (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`) |
| `host_permissions` | Allowed server endpoints + Google APIs |

## Cost attribution

Every chat from the extension lands in `ai_usage` tagged with:
- `actor = "chrome-extension"` (or the SSO email once authenticated — coming in v0.4)
- `module = "service"`

Visible in `/admin/ai-spend`.

## Cross-origin notes

The manifest's `host_permissions` array lists the local shim. For any other server URL the Options page asks Chrome for permission when you save it (`optional_host_permissions`), so nothing needs editing.

## What's NOT yet built (v0.5+)

- **Voice input**: `webkitSpeechRecognition` mic button in the textarea
- **Screenshot-as-context**: `chrome.tabs.captureVisibleTab` → ship the bitmap as a vision input on the Anthropic call (huge for field-service tickets with photo attachments)
- **Auto-detect ticket fields beyond ID**: scrape the customer / product / serial from the SAP DOM as separate fields (today these come from the OData lookup, not the page)
- **Saved prompts / templates**: pin frequently-used prompts; share an org library via `chrome.storage.sync`
- **Token budget badge**: lift cost from `ai_usage` into the popup footer (`1.2k in · 480 out · $0.018`)
- **Per-user actor attribution**: the extension sends `X-Admin-Actor: chrome-extension` but should send the SSO user's email when signed in
- **Token refresh** before expiry rather than at 401
- **Multi-IdP SSO**: only Google today; Microsoft Entra / Okta / WorkOS for enterprise customers
- **Streaming on the main `/internal` web UI**: only the extension uses the new endpoint so far
- **Background SLA alerts**: service worker poll → `chrome.notifications` when a ticket crosses 80% of SLA
- **Omnibox**: `c4s <ticket>` in the address bar → triage without opening the popup

## Versions

- **v0.1** — context menu + popup + manual Bearer token + local endpoint only
- **v0.2** — branded icons, in-page button overlay, prompt history, auto-detected ticket ID from URL, six slash commands
- **v0.3** — DOM scraping for full page context, SSE streaming replies, Google SSO with server-side validation + domain allowlist
- **v0.3.1** — placeholder OAuth client_id is now detected and the *"Sign in with Google"* button is greyed out with a one-line wire-up nudge instead of crashing into Chrome's internal OAuth page (which would otherwise surface a confusing CSP *"inline script"* violation that appeared to come from `options.html` itself). Also catches the *"Extension context invalidated"* state that fires when you reload the extension while the options page is open, and shows a reload-this-page banner instead of failing silently.
- **v0.4** — Chrome side-panel mode (persistent UX, opens via `Ctrl+Shift+U` or popup footer link), markdown rendering of replies (bold/italic/code/lists/headers via a small XSS-safe parser), one-click *"Apply priority"* and *"Save as note"* writebacks to SAP from `/triage` replies, *"Draft customer reply"* button that LLM-generates an email body and opens `mailto:`, *"Copy"* button for the reply text, `Ctrl+Shift+Y` keyboard shortcut for the popup. Bumps minimum Chrome version to 114.
- **v0.4.1** — fixes *"`sidePanel.open()` may only be called in response to a user gesture"* — the popup now calls `chrome.sidePanel.open()` directly (was previously forwarding through `chrome.runtime.sendMessage`, which crosses processes and strips the gesture token). Pre-caches the underlying normal-window id at popup load so the click handler can fire synchronously. The Ctrl+Shift+U keyboard handler in `background.js` switched from `async/await` to the callback form for the same reason.
- **v0.4.2** — fixes the *"Ticket ID or ObjectID:"* prompt that fired when the URL didn't expose a ticket number cleanly. Modern SAP C4C HTML5 client URLs encode the ticket inside an opaque base64 fragment (e.g. `#Nav/1/eyJ0...`), so URL-based detection silently failed. The extension now falls back to scanning the scraped DOM snapshot — heading first ("3553044 - MSD BPO ..."), then `document.title`, then labelled fields, then a first-1-KB body-text scan — before resorting to the prompt dialog. `/triage`, `/history`, and `/notes` all share the same resolver so they auto-detect from any of those sources.
- **v0.4.3** — significantly expands ticket-ID detection. `content.js` now exposes a `titles[]` array containing every likely page header (h1/h2/h3, `.sapMObjectHeaderTitle`, `.sapMObjectHeaderTitleText`, `.sapUshellShellHeader-tabContainer-tab-active`, `.sapMITBFilterText`, `[data-sap-ui*='title']`, etc.) rather than just the first `<h1>` — the first `<h1>` in C4C is often "Home" or a breadcrumb, hiding the actual ticket title. The extractor scans all of them. New `decodeFragmentTicket()` base64-decodes the `#Nav/1/eyJ0...` JSON fragment and pulls out an ObjectID or numeric ticket ID directly from the URL. `scrapeActiveTab()` now tries three tab-query forms (`currentWindow → lastFocusedWindow → any active tab`) so the side panel reliably reaches the underlying SAP tab on every Chrome version. Adds `console.debug("[askc4s] ...")` logs at each detection step — open DevTools → Console on the side panel to see which path matched (or why none did).
- **v0.4.4** — fixes the "*Detected ObjectID: 12DB4D4737BB1FD0AABAECB14D6999A9*" eyesore. The new URL-fragment decoder in v0.4.3 was returning the 32-hex ObjectID first (because it's the strongest signal), which short-circuited the snapshot extractor before it could find the human "3553044". `init()` now ALWAYS runs both URL and snapshot detection in parallel and picks the most readable form — preference order is `snapshot ticketId → URL ticketId → snapshot ObjectID → URL ObjectID`. Inside the URL-fragment decoder, the numeric ticket ID also now wins over the 32-hex ObjectID. Either form resolves to the same ticket server-side via `/api/lookup`, so we lose nothing by preferring the friendly one for display — and the `/triage` lookup response still hands back the canonical ObjectID for Apply-to-SAP writebacks.
- **v0.4.5** — three big improvements when the previous version was still falling back to the ObjectID. (1) `content.js` now scans into **same-origin iframes**, two levels deep — SAP C4C's HTML5 shell often renders the ticket detail in a nested iframe that's invisible from the top document, so all previous extractor work was hitting only the breadcrumb chrome. (2) New body-text extractor pattern: `^<digits> - <subject>` matches the ticket title even when the surrounding "Ticket" keyword isn't on the same line (which it usually isn't — the breadcrumb separates them). (3) `init()` now dumps the **full raw scrape** (titles[], headings, body-text start, field keys) to `console.debug("[askc4s] init: detection summary", ...)` — open the side panel's DevTools and you can see exactly what came back. If detection still misses, the log line is enough to add the missing selector.
- **v0.4.6** — removes the hardcoded vendor-name sign-off from the LLM email draft prompt — generated drafts now end with just *"Best regards, [Your name]"*.
- **v0.5.0** — Tier 1 features. **Auto-triage on ticket open** (settings toggle: off / once-per-ticket / always) — `/triage` fires automatically when a ticket is detected, no need to type the command. **Tone dial** on Customer Reply drafts — three buttons (🤝 Empathetic · 💼 Formal · ⚡ Concise) regenerate the email with the same context but a different voice; warns before discarding hand-edits. **Inline reply editor** — replaces the immediate `mailto:` open with an editable textarea + *"Copy & open mail"* / *"Just copy"* / *"Close"* buttons. Switches to "subject only + clipboard body" for drafts >1800 chars (Outlook's `mailto:` length limit). **Voice input** via `webkitSpeechRecognition` — mic button in the textarea, click to start/stop, final segments append automatically. **Saved prompts / templates** — six built-ins (🎯 Triage, 📋 Summarize visits, 🔍 Check warranty, 🚨 Draft escalation, 📞 Customer update, 🔁 Find similar); ⭐ Save button on the popup adds new templates from the current prompt; cross-device sync via `chrome.storage.sync`; manage at Options ▸ Saved templates (hide built-ins, delete custom).
- **v0.5.1** — fixes "*Detected ObjectID instead of ticket ID*" + "*LLM thinks it's on the Home dashboard, not the ticket*" — both caused by the SAP C4C HTML5 client rendering ticket details inside a **cross-origin iframe** that `iframe.contentDocument` is blocked from reading. The v0.4.5 iframe traversal silently skipped that frame, so the scrape only saw the outer shell (breadcrumb / "Home" chrome). Switched the scrape path from `chrome.tabs.sendMessage` (top-frame only) to `chrome.scripting.executeScript({ allFrames: true })` (every frame, cross-origin included — Chrome trusts the extension under `activeTab` + user-gesture). Each frame returns its own snapshot; the popup merges them into one. Falls back to the old message-based scrape when scripting fails. Adds a `__frames: { total, withData }` field to the merged snapshot so the DevTools `[askc4s] scrapeActiveTab: all-frames scrape OK` log shows how many frames contributed.
- **v0.5.2** — fixes "*I don't have access to query the customer's tickets*" — the *📋 Summarize visits* and *🔍 Check warranty* built-in templates were plain-text prompts, so the LLM had to refuse them (no tool-use in the chat path). Added two new slash commands: **`/visits [N]`** pre-fetches the customer's last N tickets via `get_account_open_tickets` (using the active ticket's `BuyerPartyID`), then hands them to the LLM for a terse summary; **`/warranty`** pulls the active ticket's `SerialID` and queries `get_serial_history`, then summarises warranty + failure patterns. Both templates now map to their respective slash commands. The slash-chip row now includes `/visits` and `/warranty`; `/help` and the textarea placeholder are updated.
- **v0.5.3–v0.5.9** — iterative fixes to `/visits` for tenant schema variance: widened the party-ID fallback chain (`pickBuyerPartyId` tries 13+ field names + regex catch-all); fixed the OData single-entity unwrap (`d.results` is an object, not an array, for `ServiceRequestCollection('id')` queries); switched the customer search from `get_account_open_tickets` (which filters only by `BuyerPartyID`) to `search_tickets` with a custom OData filter; added schema-intersection to skip fields that don't exist on the tenant's `ServiceRequest` schema (real failure: *"Property AccountPartyID not found in type ServiceRequest"*); switched from one giant OR-filter to **sequential single-clause queries** because SAP C4C's OData v2 parser chokes on long OR chains with *"Operands of logical operator '' are not valid"*. End state: every search attempt is a single `<Field> eq '<value>'` clause, tried sequentially, stop at the first match. Includes leading-zero variants (SAP party IDs are zero-padded to ~60 chars in some fields, unpadded in others).
- **v0.6.0** — **screen-aware chips**. Detects the current SAP screen type from the scraped DOM (ticket / account / opportunity / lead / activity / list / dashboard / unknown) using a 4-stage classifier: scraped titles starting with `<digits> -` → ticket; labelled-field hints (e.g., `"Service Priority"` → ticket, `"Industry"` → account); title/heading keywords; list-view fallback. Slash commands and built-in templates carry a `screenTypes: [...]` tag and only render when the current screen matches; commands tagged `["all"]` show everywhere; user-created templates default to "always show" unless tagged. Added new account-screen templates (*🎫 Account's open tickets*, *💚 Account health*), a list-view template (*📊 Summarize this list*), and a universal one (*❓ Explain this page*). New "Screen: 🎫 Ticket" indicator strip above the chips with a *Show all* toggle that bypasses filtering when the detection misses or you want a non-default command.
- **v0.6.1** — **sub-tab awareness + auto-refresh**. The scrape now captures the active SAP UI5 sub-tab (Overview / Notes / Activities / Attachments / Sub-Tickets / Solution Center / Assign Technician / etc.) via `.sapMITBSelected` and `[aria-selected="true"]` markers. Templates carry an optional `subTabs: [...]` tag that narrows display further — `📝 Summarize notes` shows only on the Notes tab, `📞 Summarize activities` only on Activities, etc. The screen indicator now shows the breadcrumb `Screen: 🎫 Ticket › Notes`. **Auto-refresh** on `window.focus` and `visibilitychange` — every time the operator clicks back into the side panel (typically after switching SAP sub-tabs), the panel re-scrapes and re-renders chips. A manual `↻` button in the indicator strip also forces a refresh. De-duped so rapid-fire focus events don't pile up. Adds 8 new sub-tab-specific templates: 📝 Summarize notes / ✍️ Draft a note (Notes) · 📞 Summarize activities / 📅 Plan follow-up (Activities) · 📎 Summarize attachments (Attachments) · 🔗 Sub-ticket status (Sub-Tickets) · 🔎 Search solutions (Solution Center) · 👤 Recommend tech (Assign Technician).
- **v0.6.2** — stale-context guards in the popup/panel (mirrors v0.3.1's options.html fix). When the extension is reloaded while the panel is open, the panel now shows a one-line amber banner *"Extension was reloaded. Click here to reload it"* instead of throwing chained errors. The Ask button is auto-disabled until reload. `init()` and `ask()` bail out early when `chrome.runtime.id` is gone. Removes the leftover `claude-sonnet-4-5` model name from the reply-meta footer for both slash and streaming paths — operators see only token counts now.
- **v0.6.3** — **clears the reply when context actually changes**. `refreshContext()` now tracks not just screen + sub-tab changes but also detected ticket-ID changes, and on any of those events clears the stale reply box, action buttons, draft editor, action status, and meta footer. Reply persists across simple focus events (so you can finish reading) but resets when you navigate to a different ticket or sub-tab. Also updates the cached `detectedTicket` value when SAP navigates to a new ticket.
- **v0.7.0** — **In-page annotation overlays** (the demo-wow feature). A new **🪄 Annotate** button in the panel sends the page context to the LLM with a strict JSON-output prompt requesting per-field suggestions (Priority should be Higher, Status should be Escalated, etc.). The reply is injected via `chrome.scripting.executeScript({ allFrames: true })` so every same-origin AND cross-origin SAP frame gets the overlays. For each annotation, a floating yellow card anchors to the matching SAP field (label matching via case-insensitive text), showing `current → suggested` + reason + an **Apply** button. Clicking Apply routes through `background.js` (which has auth + a tool allowlist) to the shim's write tools — `update_ticket_priority`, `update_ticket_lifecycle`, `escalate_ticket`, `add_service_request_text`. On success the card shows ✓ and fades. A risk-colored HUD pins top-right with the summary and an ✕ to dismiss everything. **✕ Clear ann.** button in the panel removes all overlays on demand.
- **v0.27.79** — **ECC pricing: real mutual-TLS SOAP transport wired (endpoint = "Request Sales Document Data from SAP Business Suite").** The pricing call is the CPI/iflmap SOAP service `…/cxf/COD/ERP/CUSTOMERQUOTEREQUESTEXTERNALSALESDOCUMENTDATAOUT?sap-client=100`, authenticated by **client certificate (mTLS)**. `eccPricing.ts` now builds a real `node:https` agent carrying the client cert (PEM pair or PKCS#12) and POSTs the SOAP body (`postSoap()`), with server-cert trust from the system CA (`--use-system-ca`). New env: `ECC_PRICING_URL`, `ECC_PRICING_SOAP_ACTION`, `ECC_PRICING_CLIENT_CERT`/`_KEY` or `ECC_PRICING_PFX` + `ECC_PRICING_CERT_PASSPHRASE`, `ECC_PRICING_SERVER_CA`, plus sales-area defaults. Verified: with the endpoint enabled the route reports `configured:true` and the precise remaining gap — `soap-request-template-not-wired`. **Two content drop-ins remain (need a sample message):** `buildSoapRequest()` (the CustomerQuoteRequestExternalSalesDocumentData request envelope) and `parseSoapPrice()` (net-price/currency XPath in the response). Transport, config, cache, batch endpoint, and the Price column are all done. **Shim rebuilt + restarted.**
- **v0.27.78** — **Prep-for-PM Price column (ECC pricing scaffold).** Investigated where part prices live: the C4C OData API exposes **no per-product price** on this tenant (Product master has only a cost *category*; ServiceRequestItem has no amount fields; the internal price list is empty; `CustomerOrderItem` has `NetPriceAmount` but ProductID isn't filterable; `ContractItem` is filterable but the PM spares have 0 lines). Prices live in ECC and C4S gets them via a pricing *simulation* — which isn't a read-only OData call. So this lands the **scaffold** for an ECC pricing connector: new `src/eccPricing.ts` with a single isolated drop-in `callEccPricing()` (the only place the real ECC HTTP call goes), env config (`ECC_PRICING_*` in `.env`), a 6-hour cache, and a batch helper; a shim route **`POST /api/get_product_prices`** (best-effort, always 200); and a **Price column** in the Prep-for-PM parts table that hydrates lazily via that route. Until the ECC endpoint is wired the route returns `configured:false` and the column shows `—` with a one-time footnote (*"Prices come from ECC — configure the ECC pricing endpoint…"*). Zero impact on anything else — purely additive. **Shim rebuild + restart + extension reload; the actual ECC call is the remaining one-function drop-in once the endpoint contract is provided.**
- **v0.27.77** — **Generic "Draft customer reply" now personalizes like the /interactions one — contact name greeting, "Jane Doe" sign-off, To: pre-filled.** The reply-actions **✉ Draft customer reply** button (under a note/triage reply, with the Empathetic/Formal/Concise tone dial) was the one customer-email path still emitting "Hi there," with placeholder/no signature and an empty To. It now converges on the same personalization the /interactions "Respond to customer" path got earlier: `draftCustomerReply` resolves the ticket contact's **name** (`BuyerMainContactPartyName → ReportedPartyName → ContactPartyName`) + **email** (`pickEmailFromEntity` over `/api/lookup`) and the operator's **display name** (`resolveDisplayName`), stashes them on `currentDraftCtx`; `generateDraftWithTone` then greets by the contact's first name and signs with the sender's exact name (real values in the prompt, `personalizeDraft` as the bracket-placeholder safety net), and tone re-rolls keep the personalization. The mailto **To:** is populated from the resolved contact email (`ctx.customerEmail`). Net: **both** customer-email generators (`generateDraftWithTone` + `generateEmailReplyDraft`) now behave identically. All lookups are best-effort — a miss falls back to the old generic greeting, never blocks the draft. **Reload extension only.**
- **v0.27.76** — **"AI Companion" tagline next to the "Ask C4S" header.** Added a subtle pill-style tagline (`header .title-tag`) immediately after the product name in the gradient header bar, so it reads **Ask C4S · AI Companion**. Hidden in detached-popup mode along with `.title` (the OS title bar already carries the name there). Pure header markup + CSS; no behaviour change. **Reload extension only.**
- **v0.27.75** — **Order Parts item type is division-aware: MSD → SRP3, everyone else → ZRP3.** Per operator rule: if the ticket's header field `KUTDivision_KUT` is `MSD`, the committed line item must use processing type **SRP3**; all other divisions keep the **ZRP3** default. Implemented shim-side in `logServiceItem` so it's authoritative and centralized: a new `fetchTicketDivision` reads `KUTDivision_KUT` off `ServiceRequestCollection('<id>')` (60s cache, so a multi-part commit fetches once), and the override fires **only when the incoming code is ZRP3** — Book Time (`ZALT`), consumption (`SRP1`), and any explicit Options override are left completely untouched ("do not break anything else"). The switch sets `opts.processingTypeCode` before the body is built, so the dry-run preview, the SAP POST, and the failure diagnostics all reflect the effective code; the decision is logged (`KUTDivision_KUT=MSD → item type ZRP3 → SRP3`). Any division-lookup failure silently keeps ZRP3 (best-effort, never blocks the commit). Verified by dry-run: MSD ticket 4387049 → SRP3, GSS ticket 688142 → ZRP3, and an MSD ticket via Book Time → still ZALT. **Shim rebuild + restart done in dev; extension reload optional (no extension code changed).** Note: the Order-parts confirm dialog still prints the configured base code (ZRP3) for MSD tickets — cosmetic only, the actual write + dry-run preview are SRP3.
- **v0.27.74** — **Book Time actuals actually land now: read the created line's ObjectID from the `Location` header.** Live test showed the new ZALT rows with Actual End blank and Actual Quantity 0 — the shim log read `SUCCESS … Echoed back: {}`, i.e. SAP returns an **empty POST body** on this tenant, so `result.d.ObjectID` was null and the v0.27.72 post-create Actual-date PATCH was being **silently skipped**. (The Actual Start that *did* show was SAP auto-stamping creation time, not our write.) Two root-cause facts captured: (1) on this tenant the **hours/quantity are DERIVED from `ActualEnd − ActualStart`**, not from `ActualQuantity` — so both timestamps MUST be set or the grid shows 0h (matches existing rows: 11:30→12:00 = 0.5h, 9:00→11:00 = 2h); the "Finish Work" tick is just the grid's cue that Actual End is filled (no such field exists on the entity). (2) the created ObjectID is only in the **`Location` response header**. Fix: new `c4cPostWithMeta` in `connectors/c4c/client.ts` returns `{ data, location, status }` from the shared write driver (zero behaviour change for every other caller — `c4cPost`/`c4cPatch`/`c4cDelete` still return just the body); `logServiceItem` resolves the created key via **POST echo → Location header → re-query** (`ParentObjectID eq … and ProductID eq …`, newest first — labour/travel have distinct ProductIDs), then PATCHes `ActualStartDateTime`/`ActualEndDateTime`. Only if all three resolution paths fail does it soft-warn. **Shim rebuild + restart done in dev; re-test the Add to Ticket — Actual Start, Actual End, and the derived hours should all populate.** Extension reload optional (no extension code changed).
- **v0.27.73** — **Detected-ticket chip merged onto the Screen line (one context row, not two).** Operator request ("Move this"): the **🎫 Ticket NNN · detected** pill and the **Screen: …** indicator were two stacked rows above the textarea. The pill now renders inline at the left of the Screen row, so they read as one. A dedicated `#detected-ticket-pill` span lives inside `#screen-indicator` (with its own `.detected-ticket-pill` style); the three detected-pill writer sites (init + refreshContext) target it instead of the shared `#selection` div. `#selection` stays reserved for the **full-width SLA-warning and right-click-selection banners** (block treatment) — they no longer get clobbered by the detected pill, so a right-click selection on a ticket page now shows both. `.screen-indicator` gains `flex-wrap: wrap` so the longer combined row degrades gracefully (wraps the Search/icons to a second line) on narrow panels. Dashboard-visibility gate is unaffected (it keys off the `detectedTicket` dataset, not `#selection`). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.72** — **Book Time: booked hours now land in Actual Start/End (not Requested).** Per operator: *"It must go to Actual start and Actual End not Requested."* SAP forbids `ActualStartDateTime`/`ActualEndDateTime` on **create** (they're in `buildBodyFromProbe`'s `SKIP_EXACT` — "Create not allowed for properties"), so `logServiceItem` now **creates the line, then PATCHes the created item's actuals** (the same write `update_service_item_actuals` does) — reading the new item's `ObjectID` from the POST echo. New options `actualStartMs`/`actualEndMs`; end derived = start + booked hours (or explicit `actualEndMs` when later than start). The create body mirrors the probe's own (valid) `RequestedStart/EndDateTime`, so the booked time no longer leaks into Requested and the create can't trip the v0.27.71 "End date must be after start date" error. Best-effort: if SAP rejects the date PATCH (e.g. an item lifecycle state that locks `Actual*`), the line still exists and the panel shows a soft amber warning (*"Line created, but SAP rejected setting Actual Start/End: …"*) rather than a hard failure (which would tempt a duplicate re-add). Lock-aware single retry on the PATCH. Dry-run verified on ticket 12DDDABA…: Actual dates appear as the annotated `(post-create PATCH) ActualStartDateTime/EndDateTime` (+150 min for 2.5h, end > start), absent from the real create body, and the booked start is **not** present in `RequestedStart/EndDateTime`. The live PATCH is operator-initiated (the gated Add-to-Ticket click). **Shim rebuild + extension reload required.**
- **v0.27.71** — **Book Time fix: SAP 500 "End date must be after start date."** First live Book Time write failed on both rows — the body set `RequestedStartDateTime` to the chosen (future) start but left `RequestedEndDateTime` as the mirrored *probe* value (an old date), so end < start. Fix in `buildBodyFromProbe`: whenever the START is overridden, also set END = start + the booked hours (Book Time's `quantity` IS hours), or the caller's explicit `requestedEndMs` when it's valid and later than start; floor the gap at 1 minute. Extension also now passes `requestedEndMs = startMs + hours·3,600,000` explicitly (belt-and-suspenders). Dry-run verified: explicit end (+30 min for 0.5h) and shim-derived end (+90 min for 1.5h) both land after start. **Shim rebuild + extension reload required** (shim already rebuilt + restarted in dev).
- **v0.27.70** — **Phase taxonomy re-mapped to 4 columns (operator table): Closure restored + chips reassigned.** Re-added the **④ Closure** column (merged into Resolution in v0.27.60) and re-bucketed chips per the supplied layout: **① Discovery** = Customer Pulse · Interactions · Account's open tickets · Map view (+ Triage / Check warranty / Skill match); **② Troubleshooting** = Ticket Summary · KB Resolution · Service History (+ Search KB / Find similar / the comms drafts); **③ Resolution** = Parts check · Order parts · Prep for PM (+ PM kit gaps / Sub-ticket status); **④ Closure** = Draft a note · Book time · Plan follow-up · Nearby PMs · Close-out. Net moves: Ticket Summary, KB Resolution, Service History → Troubleshooting; Parts check, Prep for PM → Resolution; Map view, Account's open tickets → Discovery; Draft a note, Plan follow-up, Nearby PMs, Book time, Close-out → Closure. `PHASE_GROUPS` gains the closure entry between resolution and tools; `PHASE_BY_ID` re-mapped. Pure grouping change on the column layout. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.69** — **NEW: ⏱ Book time — log labour + travel hours to a ticket as ZALT line items.** Reporter spec: a "Book Time" action that asks Travel hours + start, Labour hours + start, then on "Add to Ticket" creates the ZALT (Actual Labor / Travel) rows — ACTUAL_LABOR / ACTUAL_TRAVEL. **Reuses the proven `log_service_item` write path** (probe → build-from-probe → POST → self-heal → lock-retry), the exact one /order-parts commits through, via background.js apply-annotation — no new SAP write code. New `/book-time` slash command + ⏱ chip (Resolution phase) opens a form panel: Labour and Travel each get an hours field + a `datetime-local` start; **Add to Ticket** commits each filled row SEQUENTIALLY (SAP locks the parent per write) with `processingTypeCode: "ZALT"`, the labour/travel ProductID, `quantity` = hours, `uomCode: "HUR"`, and the start as epoch ms. The two products are configurable (`c4s.book-time-labor-product` / `…-travel-product`), defaulting to this tenant's `11266683` (ACTUAL_LABOR / ONSITE LABOR) and `11266684` (ACTUAL_TRAVEL / TRAVEL TIME) — SAP derives the external ID + description from the ProductID. **Shim changes:** `LogServiceItemOptions` gains `requestedStartMs`/`requestedEndMs` → written to `RequestedStartDateTime`/`RequestedEndDateTime` (the settable schedule fields; `ActualStartDateTime` stays SAP-managed/excluded-on-create); and a new `floatArg` helper lets `log_service_item` accept fractional `quantity` (hours like 0.5 / 1.5 — `numberArg` rejected non-integers). **Dry-run verified** on ticket 4387049: body = `{ProductID:11266683, UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode:ZALT, ActualQuantity:1.50…, PlannedQuantity:0, QuantityMeasureUnitCode:HUR, RequestedStartDateTime:/Date(…)/}`, ActualStartDateTime correctly absent. The live write is operator-initiated (the gated Add-to-Ticket click) and requires `feature.auto_writeback_enabled` — like all SAP writes; the actual POST shape for ZALT-on-create may need the same first-live-write confirmation /order-parts went through, and the self-heal/lock-retry already in `logServiceItem` covers the known failure modes. **Shim rebuild + extension reload required** (shim already rebuilt + restarted in dev).
- **v0.27.68** — **Hide 🔎 Parts lookup + 🗂 Knowledge chips (operator request).** Same reversible `hidden: true` flag. `tpl-builtin-parts` (Parts lookup — manual catalog search; /parts-check + /order-parts cover the ticket-driven flows) and `tpl-builtin-knowledge` (Knowledge — now redundant with v0.27.67's /kb-resolve, which covers KB articles + same-product closures). Both still work if typed (`/parts`, `/knowledge`); only the chips are hidden. Remove the flag to restore. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.67** — **/kb-resolve: bring in "same product, fixed elsewhere" (additive).** Reporter wanted the closure-history section from /knowledge — how OTHER units of the same product were resolved — inside KB resolution, *without breaking existing KB-resolve*. The handler now, after resolving the ticket, makes a guarded (try/catch, never throws into the KB path) `search_tickets` call (`ProductID eq '<id>' and status eq '4'`, newest 8), drops the current ticket, and pulls resolution notes for the top closed ones (`get_ticket_notes`, capped at 4 — preferring Service-Response-Report/resolution/root-cause notes, else the longest). Result carries a new `productClosures[]`; `renderKbResolutionsPanel` appends a "🔧 Same product, fixed elsewhere" section below the KB cards — clickable **#ticketID** (opens in C4C), date · customer, and the inline resolution note. **The existing deep-KB-resolution flow is untouched**: same `runDeepKbResolution` call, same cards; closures are purely appended. Bonus — the panel now renders even when KB matched 0 articles *if* product closures exist (previously bailed to an "info" message), and the closures are folded into the Copy/Draft plain-text snapshot. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed** (reuses existing search_tickets + get_ticket_notes tools).
- **v0.27.66** — **/history (service history) — structured panel with clickable tickets instead of plain text.** Reporter: *"Service history looks like plain text?"* It did — the `/history` handler returned `kind: "reply"` with a flat text blob, so ticket IDs weren't clickable and the layout was a bare list. The handler now returns a new `kind: "serial-history-panel"` with a structured `tickets[]` (ticketId, **objectId**, subject, status, isClosed, date, customer), and a new `renderSerialHistoryPanel()` renders it as cards: clickable **#ticketID** (opens in SAP C4C via `openTicketInC4C`, same path as My Day / map markers), a green/grey status pill (open vs closed/completed), and a date · customer meta line, under a "Serial · Equipment" subheader and "N past tickets" count. A 📋 Copy button (in reply-actions) preserves the plain-text export. `.svc-hist-*` CSS namespace. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.65** — **/ticket-summary EFFORT showed 0h despite logged labour/travel — fixed ZALT detection.** Reporter on ticket 4387049: 6 line items, EFFORT = 0h Labour / 0h Travel. Root cause: this tenant logs BOTH labour and travel under one processing code **`ZALT`** ("Actual Labor / Travel"), distinguished only by the `Description` ("ONSITE LABOR" vs "TRAVEL"). The shim's `detectKind` only recognized the canonical `ACTLAB`/`ACTRVL` codes, so every ZALT line fell through to "other" → 0h. Rewrote `detectKind` (both copies in `uiServer.ts`): match canonical codes in the code field, then keyword in the Description (`\bTRAVEL\b` → travel, `\bLABO(U)?R\b` → labour), then a ZALT fallback that reads the code text (TRAVEL-and-not-LABOR → travel, else labour). Travel is tested before labour because the ZALT code text literally reads "Actual Labor / Travel". Canonical-code tenants are unaffected. **Live-verified on 4387049**: now Labour 2.5h · Travel 1h · Total 3.5h (kindCounts labour 2 / travel 1 / other 3 — the 3 "other" are genuinely non-effort lines). Note: the panel's "(ACTLAB)/(ACTRVL)" sub-labels are cosmetic canonical names; the data is summed from ZALT on this tenant. **Shim rebuild required; extension unchanged** (shim already rebuilt + restarted in dev).
- **v0.27.64** — **Move the screen-indicator row above the textarea (operator request).** The "Screen: … · ⌘K Search · 📎 · 🕘 · ↻ · 📊 · Show all" strip (plus its 🕘 recent-prompts dropdown) was below the prompt textarea; it's now directly under the detected-ticket pill and above the textarea — it's context/toolbar, so it reads better at the top. Pure DOM reorder in popup.html; all IDs and JS wiring unchanged (`#screen-indicator`, `#history`, the toolbar icons). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.63** — **Hide six more chips (operator request).** Flagged `hidden: true` (the reversible v0.27.61 switch — remove the flag to restore): 💚 Account health (`tpl-builtin-account-health`), 📊 Summarize this list (`tpl-builtin-list-summary`), ❓ All commands (`tpl-builtin-help` — /help still works typed, and ⌘K covers discovery), 📝 Summarize notes (`tpl-builtin-notes-summarize`), 📎 Summarize attachments (`tpl-builtin-attachments-summarize`), 📞 Summarize activities (`tpl-builtin-activities-summarize`). Trims the Resolution column (the three sub-tab "Summarize …" chips) and the Tools column (Account health, Summarize this list, All commands). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.62** — **Toolbar declutter: page-context + recent-prompts → icons next to Search; footer Endpoint removed; Annotate promoted to a bottom button.** Reporter requests on the main panel. **(1) 📎 Page context** — the full-width "Page context attached (N fields · ~X KB)" banner under the chips is now a compact 📎 icon in the indicator row next to ⌘K Search; the field/KB detail moved to its tooltip, and clicking now *toggles* attach/detach (was one-way clear) with an active tint when attached (`lastPageContext` stashes the block so it can be re-attached). **(2) 🕘 Recent prompts** — the bottom "Recent prompts (N)" list is relocated to a 🕘 icon next to Search; clicking it drops the list down as a bordered card under the icon row (populated but collapsed by default; the icon only appears when there's history). **(3) Footer "Endpoint" link removed** — the endpoint is changed via ⚙ Settings now (the shim-status dot + its tooltip remain; ↗ Side panel remains). **(4) Bottom action row** now shows the three primary actions **Ask · Clear · 🪄 Annotate** (Annotate promoted out of the ⋯ overflow); ⋯ keeps Save-template + Clear-annotations, ⚙ stays. All element IDs unchanged so existing wiring (annotate flow, clear-annotations toggle, options) keeps working; the removed `#open-options-link` is now guarded with `?.`. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.61** — **Hide "👤 Match technician" chip (operator request).** It's redundant with "👤 Skill match" (both run `/skills`) and was only meant for the Assign-Technician sub-tab but leaked onto the general ticket view. New code-level `hidden: true` flag on a built-in template — `getTemplates()` now filters `!t.hidden` in addition to the per-user runtime hidden list (the chip ✕). The flag is the reversible "off for now" switch (remove it to restore); distinct from a user hiding it via ✕. Applied to `tpl-builtin-assign-recommend`. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.60** — **Phase taxonomy relabel + merge (operator-defined).** Reporter: rename Initiation → **Discovery**, Communication → **Troubleshooting**, and merge Resolution + Closure into one. `PHASE_GROUPS` is now 3 lifecycle columns + Tools + Saved: **① Discovery** (the former Initiation set — Pulse, Interactions, Ticket Summary, Triage, warranty, Parts check, PM kit gaps, Prep for PM, Service history, Skill match) · **② Troubleshooting** (former Communication — escalation, customer update, draft note, summarize activities, plan follow-up) · **③ Resolution** (former Resolution **plus Close-out**, which folded in — Knowledge, KB resolution, Search KB, Find similar, Order parts, Parts lookup, notes/attachment/sub-ticket summaries, **Close-out**) · **🧰 Tools** · **📌 Saved**. Internal phase keys renamed to match (`initiation`→`discovery`, `communication`→`troubleshooting`, `closure`→`resolution`); `tpl-builtin-closeout` remapped to `resolution`. The contract templates re-bucket the same way (Coverage/Renewal → Discovery, Renewal outreach → Troubleshooting, Linked tickets → Resolution). Pure label/grouping change on top of the v0.27.59 column layout. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.59** — **Chip tray phases rendered as a TABLE (columns), not stacked text.** Follow-up to v0.27.57: the lifecycle grouping stacked each phase header above its chips (vertical sections). Reporter wanted it laid out as a table with 4-5 columns. `renderTemplateChips()` now builds one `.chip-phase-col` per non-empty phase (header + chips stacked within), placed in a responsive CSS grid (`#template-chips.phase-grid`, `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(94px, 1fr))`). On a wide panel all phases sit side by side as columns — ① Initiation · ② Communication · ③ Resolution · ④ Closure · 🧰 Tools — reading left-to-right as the workflow; on a narrow side panel the columns wrap to additional rows instead of overflowing. Chips fill their column (full-width, left-aligned, long labels wrap); usage-rank order preserved within each column. Headers get a bottom rule for the table look. Pure layout change — phase assignment, click/auto-fire, delete, and screen-filtering all unchanged. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.58** — **Customer reply drafts: real contact name in the greeting, contact email in To, operator name in the sign-off — no more `[Customer name]` / `[Your name]` placeholders.** Reporter on ticket 4387049: *"Auto show the contact name, and contact email in the To, In Best regards, Jane Doe."* **Shim `/api/draft_customer_reply`** now extracts the customer CONTACT name (`BuyerMainContactPartyName` → `ReportedPartyName` → `ContactPartyName` — the addressee, NOT `BuyerPartyName` which is the company) and accepts a `senderName` param; the LLM prompt is rewritten to greet by the contact's first name (*"Hi Yunfei,"*) and sign off with the exact sender name (*"Best regards, Jane Doe"*) instead of bracket placeholders, and the response now returns `recipientName`. **Extension**: the ✉️ Respond-to-customer flow resolves the operator's name via v0.27.55 `resolveDisplayName()` (SAP-session display name), passes it as `senderName`, and stores `recipientName`/`senderName` in `currentDraftCtx` so **tone-dial regeneration** (`generateEmailReplyDraft`) re-personalizes too instead of reverting to placeholders. New shared `personalizeDraft()` is a belt-and-suspenders net — distinct sender vs recipient token sets (`[Your name]`/`[Sender]`/`[Agent]` → sender; `[Customer name]`/`[first name]`/`[Contact]` → recipient first name) — applied on the server draft, the local fallback draft, and every tone regeneration, so no placeholder ever survives even on older shims or the local path. The recipient email already flows into the mailto **To** via `currentDraftCtx.emailRecipient` (now reliably populated from the server's `recipient`). **Live-verified on 4387049**: *"Hi Yunfei,"* · To = `sam.roe@example.com` · *"Best regards, Jane Doe"* · parts-on-order facts intact. **Shim rebuild + extension reload required** (shim already rebuilt + restarted in dev).
- **v0.27.57** — **Chip tray grouped by service lifecycle: ① Initiation → ② Communication → ③ Resolution → ④ Closure (+ 🧰 Tools · 📌 Saved).** Reporter asked to categorize the commands into the ticket lifecycle phases. The flat usage-ranked tray (with the "+N more" collapse) is replaced by labelled phase sections, so the tray reads as a workflow instead of a wall of buttons. New `PHASE_GROUPS` (ordered headers) + `PHASE_BY_ID` (per-built-in phase) + `phaseOf(tpl)` (explicit `phase` field wins → per-ID map → user-templates land in 📌 Saved → else 🧰 Tools). `renderTemplateChips()` now: screen-filters + usage-ranks as before, buckets the result into phases (rank order preserved *within* each phase, so heavy-use chips still rise to the top of their group), then renders each non-empty bucket under a full-width uppercase header (`.chip-phase-header`, forces a flex line-break). Mapping highlights: pre-visit prep (Parts check, PM kit gaps, Prep for PM, Service history, Skill match, warranty) + the triage reads (Pulse, Interactions, Ticket Summary, Triage) sit in **Initiation**; drafting/escalation/notes/activities + the new ✉️ Respond-to-customer-adjacent commands in **Communication**; KB + Order parts + Parts lookup + notes/attachment/sub-ticket summaries in **Resolution**; Close-out in **Closure**; Map/Nearby/Explain/All-commands/account+list in **Tools**. The four contract templates carry phases too (Coverage/Renewal → Initiation, Renewal outreach → Communication, Linked tickets → Resolution), so the same lifecycle reads on the Contract screen. Removed the now-obsolete top-6 collapse + "+N more / Show less" toggle (`TOP_CHIPS_VISIBLE` / `templateChipsExpanded` deleted) — the phase sections are the organization now. No behavior change to chip clicks, auto-fire, delete/hide, or screen-filtering. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.56** — **Contract commands now HARD-scoped — never shown on a Ticket, even in "Show all".** Reporter, on a Ticket with the screen-filter off, saw the four contract templates (📋 Coverage analysis · 📅 Renewal check · ✉️ Draft renewal outreach · 🔗 Linked tickets) and asked to make them *"available only on Contract not on Ticket."* They were already `screenTypes: ["contract"]`, but the v0.27.39 "Show all" toggle bypasses ALL screen filtering, so they appeared on the ticket as discovery clutter. New optional `strictScreen: true` flag on a command/template means it opts OUT of the show-all bypass: `matchesCurrentScreen()` now takes a third `strict` arg and only short-circuits on show-all when `!strict`, so strict commands fall through to the real `screenTypes.includes(currentScreen)` check and stay hidden on unrelated *detected* screens. The four contract templates are marked `strictScreen: true`; the flag is threaded through all four filter call sites (slash chips, template chips, and both command-palette lists). Deliberate carve-out: strict commands STILL show on `"unknown"` screens, so a detection miss on a real contract page never makes them unreachable. Everything else is unchanged — non-strict commands keep the lenient show-all behaviour. Built-ins read the flag straight from the in-code registry (only hidden-IDs are persisted, not copies), so no migration needed. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.55** — **Dashboard greeting now personalised: "Good afternoon, Jane Doe".** Reporter: *"can you show Good afternoon Jane Doe"*. The greeting was time-of-day only. New `resolveDisplayName()` resolves the operator's name in priority order — (1) `chrome.storage.sync["my-display-name"]` (manual, set in Options), (2) the auto-detected employee label with its "· Employee NNN" suffix stripped, (3) **live SAP-shell harvest** (the name in the SAP user menu — works even for admin/service-agent logins that have NO EmployeeCollection row, which is exactly this reporter's case: manually-entered party ID 10041366, no employee match), (4) Google SSO profile name. `hydrateDashboardGreeting()` paints the base greeting instantly, then appends `, <name>` once resolved (async, non-blocking) on both dashboard-show paths. The resolved name is cached to `my-display-name` (source `auto`) so subsequent loads are instant; `autoDetectSapIdentity()` also persists the harvested shell name regardless of whether the employee lookup succeeds. **New Options field** "Display name (dashboard greeting)" under My SAP identity as the manual override / escape hatch (source `manual` — never clobbered by the auto-harvest; clear it to re-enable auto). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.54** — **Respond to customer: NEW SHIM ENDPOINT — context-aware draft from the ticket's LATEST state.** Reporter: *"can we also add as new shim, to reply customer with latest information on the ticket context aware"*. **New `POST /api/draft_customer_reply`** (`{ticketId|objectId, tone}`): resolves the ticket, then fetches its CURRENT state fresh from SAP in parallel — full ticket entity (status, subject, dates, contact email via the KUT_CEMAILS_KUT-style *mail*-field scan), line items split into **parts ON ORDER (ZRP3, "not yet confirmed delivered")** vs consumed (SRP1/ZRP1), and the latest 12 notes (which on this tenant include emailed-in content) — and drafts the reply server-side via the existing `chat()` pipeline with hard grounding rules: facts ONLY from the assembled context, never invent dates/part numbers/promises, answer the customer's latest message first **in its language**, and say "the team is confirming" when asked-for info isn't on the ticket yet. Returns `{draft:{subject,body,language}, recipient, contextText, meta:{status, partsOrdered, partsConsumed, notesUsed}}`. **Extension (v0.27.54)**: the ✉️ Respond to customer button is now SERVER-FIRST — it calls the new endpoint and opens the editor with the returned draft directly (no second LLM call); `contextText` becomes the tone dial's regeneration context so 🤝/💼/⚡ re-drafts use the same fresh facts; the v0.27.52 panel-context draft remains as automatic fallback for older shims or endpoint errors. Why this matters vs v0.27.52: drafts now reflect what's true NOW (status changes, newly ordered parts, notes added after the panel was rendered — including cache-served v0.27.51 renders), and the endpoint is reusable by any client (companion UI, future MCP tool). **Live-verified on French ticket 4386459**: fully French draft, subject `RE: Demande de prix 2026 — Solution de calibration iCAP Q Tune 500 mL`, honestly states the material reference is being confirmed (it's genuinely not on the ticket), meta `{notesUsed:1, partsOrdered:0}`. **Shim rebuild + extension reload required** (shim already rebuilt + restarted in dev).
- **v0.27.53** — **Pop-out carries the session over — no more starting fresh.** Reporter popped the side panel out to a window (v0.26.31 ↗ button) while viewing an /interactions result and the new window booted empty: *"when you pop out it is starting fresh"*. Root cause: the detached popup is a SEPARATE JS context — module state (rendered panel, prompt, detected ticket, caches) doesn't follow. **Fix — state handoff via `chrome.storage.session`** (in-memory, extension-only, survives nothing beyond the browser session): the pop-out click writes `{prompt, detectedTicket, lastResult, cacheMeta, ts}`; the new window's detached-popup init reads it (one-shot — key removed on read; 30s freshness window so a stale handoff never resurrects), restores the prompt text + detected-ticket dataset + OS window title, and **re-renders the last panel through the normal `renderResult` dispatcher** — so every button in the restored panel (translate, Respond to customer, Prep, retry) is live, not a dead HTML copy. New module-level `lastRenderedResult` captured inside `renderResult` for serializable, renderer-pure panels: interactions, ticket-summary, pm-prep, knowledge, kb-resolutions, parts-check, pulse, nearby, close-out, emails. **Deliberately excluded** (re-run instead of restore): map-panel (Leaflet re-init), order-parts-panel (live cart state outside the result object), debug-panel (huge), errors. Bonus: an interactions handoff also seeds the new window's v0.27.51 per-ticket cache with the ORIGINAL fetchedAt/LastChangeDateTime metadata, so /interactions in the popped window is instant and correctly revalidated. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.52** — **/interactions: ✉️ Respond to customer.** Reporter: *"can we add respond to customer"*. New green button in the timeline header — drafts a customer-facing reply straight from the context the panel already holds, then opens the EXISTING reply-editor pipeline (editable textarea, 🤝/💼/⚡ tone dial, "Copy & open mail" mailto routing) — no new editor surface. **Context fed to the LLM**: the executive summary + current status + open questions + action items the panel already computed, PLUS the customer's most recent email verbatim (first email-shaped entry in the newest-first timeline; capped 1800 chars) with an explicit instruction that the reply must answer THAT message **in ITS language** — a French thread gets a French draft (also added a language-matching format rule to the shared `generateEmailReplyDraft` prompt, which upgrades the /emails thread-reply flow too). **Subject**: `Re: <latest email subject>` (Re:/Fwd:/AW:/TR: chain stripped so exactly one prefix), else `Ticket NNN — update from your service team`. **To: recipient chain**: ticket contact email via `/api/lookup` + `pickEmailFromEntity` (KUT_CEMAILS_KUT et al — the v0.27.41 authoritative source, NOT the latest sender which is often an internal CSR address) → sender-shaped field on the latest email → page-context scrape → blank for the operator. **Tone default** maps from the summary's customer sentiment: frustrated/escalated → 🤝 empathetic, urgent → ⚡ concise, else 💼 formal — switchable on the dial as always. Editor scrolls into view when the draft lands. Works on cache-served renders too (v0.27.51 — the context is the cached result itself, so the draft starts instantly). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.51** — **/interactions: per-ticket result cache — command-hopping no longer refetches + re-runs the LLM.** Reporter: *"If I click on interactions one ticket it fetched all the details but when I go to order parts and come back to interactions it searches again — how to make it memorize for that ticket?"* **New in-memory `interactionsResultCache`** (Map keyed by ticket ObjectID) stores the fully-computed panel result (notes + emails + activities + LLM summary) after each successful run. On the next `/interactions` for the same ticket the handler still does its single cheap `/api/lookup` (which it needed anyway for ID resolution) and uses it as the **freshness probe**: if the entry is under 15 min old AND the ticket's `LastChangeDateTime` matches what we saw at fetch time (new notes/emails/status changes bump it), the stored result renders instantly — zero SAP collection fetches, zero LLM calls. Stale or missing → normal full pipeline. **Cache-served renders show a chip**: *"⚡ Loaded from memory (fetched N min ago, ticket unchanged since) · ↻ Refresh"* — Refresh drops the entry and re-runs the full pipeline for that ticket. **Translations survive re-renders**: v0.27.50's in-place swaps live on DOM datasets (wiped by re-render), so both translate paths now ALSO write `_translated`/`_translatedTo` back onto the note objects (same references the cache holds) and the translated summary onto `result._summaryTranslated`; `renderInteractionsPanel` restores them at the end of every render (summary-card apply factored into `applySummaryCardTranslation()`). **Targeted invalidation**: Mark-as-resolution (`/kb-resolve`) writes a note = new timeline entry → it now deletes that ticket's cache entry after the successful write. Order-parts commits don't invalidate (line items don't appear in the interactions timeline). In-memory only — resets when the side panel closes; intentionally NOT persisted to chrome.storage (email bodies are chunky; the pain point is command-hopping within a session). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.50** — **/interactions translate: swap the body text IN PLACE — fixes "Translate to English but still shows French".** Reporter translated a French ticket, status said *"✓ Translated 2 entries + summary"*, yet the timeline still showed French. Root cause was PLACEMENT, not translation: since v0.26.105 the translation rendered into an append-below container (`#ix-tl-translation-<idx>`) that sits UNDER the full-length original body — on long emails (like the forwarded Demande de prix thread) that block landed off-screen, and the original French body stayed fully visible above it. Also the ✓ count tallied the LLM response keys, not what actually landed in the DOM (a missing row silently `continue`d). **Fix**: new shared `applyEntryTranslation()` — stashes the original on `dataset`, REPLACES the `.ix-tl-body` text with the translation, swaps the collapsed `.ix-tl-preview` line too (unexpanded rows visibly switch language), and inserts a small amber tag above the body: *"🌐 Translated to English · show original"* with a working toggle (`toggleEntryTranslationView()` keeps the tag link + per-entry button labels in sync). Both paths use it: **Translate all** (status now counts only applied rows — `✓ Translated 2/2 entries + summary`, warns `⚠ no timeline rows matched` + console dump when zero land) and the **per-entry 🌐 button** (first click translates + swaps; later clicks toggle translated ↔ original from the cached dataset — no repeat LLM calls). Error display still uses the old container; summary-card translation unchanged. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.49** — **Dashboard: 🕒 Recent section removed.** Reporter: *"remove the recent from dashboard"*. The top-5 recent-prompts cards (v0.26.131) were eating vertical space the My Day briefing now uses better — and one of the surfaced entries was an off-topic prompt ("What are you built on?"), which looks bad in demos. Removed the HTML block (`#dashboard-recent-title` + `#empty-state-recent`), the `.dashboard-recent*` CSS, `hydrateDashboardRecent()` and both its call sites (auto empty-state + 📊 toggle). Prompt history is untouched — still recorded and reachable via the **⌘K command palette** (Recent group) and the reply-box 📌 Recent strip. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.48** — **My Day: SAMPLE PREVIEW + zero-workload repair actions.** Reporter's panel showed *"You have 0 open tickets assigned — 0, 0, 0"* (a stale party ID saved from the v0.27.46 setup card — no 👤 label line = it wasn't auto-detected) and asked *"Its not working can you show a mock up for now"*. Two fixes: **(1) Zero-workload state** — instead of the dead-end 0-line, the briefing now shows WHO was checked (the 👤 label, or the raw saved party ID so a bad value is immediately visible), states that all three assignment places were checked, and offers **🔍 Re-detect from SAP** (clears the saved ID + the 30-min detection cache, re-runs v0.27.47 auto-detection) and **⚙ Options** buttons. **(2) Sample preview** — an amber-dashed, **SAMPLE PREVIEW**-badged card renders a fully-populated briefing (7 open across the three assignment places, 1 escalated, 3 visits due/1 overdue, 2 with ZRP3 ordered parts, 4 focus rows with plausible instrument subjects — TSQ Altis, Vanquish HPLC PM, Orbitrap Exploris PM, Dionex ICS-6000 — and obviously-not-real ticket IDs) so the operator/demo always sees what My Day is FOR. Appended to BOTH empty paths: the zero-workload state and the identity setup card. Sample rows are inert — #IDs don't open SAP and 🔧 Prep reveals *"That's a sample row — connect your SAP identity above and I'll prep your real visits"*. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.47** — **My Day: auto-detect the logged-in SAP user's EMPLOYEE identity — no more typing your party ID.** Reporter: *"Can you get id from logged in user - Employee id (not user id)"*. The v0.27.46 setup card asked the operator to find + type their party ID; now the panel reads WHO IS SIGNED IN to SAP from the open C4C tab and resolves them to their employee record automatically. **Harvest** (`harvestSapIdentityInline`, injected into every frame of the SAP tab in the **MAIN world** so it can see `sap.*` globals): ① Fiori shell user API (`sap.ushell.Container.getUser()` → full name / id / email), ② C4C HTML5 (Oberon) client globals (`sap.client.user` / `.identity` / `.getUser()` — shape varies by patch level, all probed), ③ DOM sweep of avatar/user-menu/profile elements (`title` + `aria-label` always, `textContent` for leaf nodes only) with a person-name shape filter that rejects UI labels like "User Settings", plus `FIRST.LAST` logon-shape and email-shape captures. **Resolve** (new shim endpoint `POST /api/resolve_employee`): tries candidates against `EmployeeCollection` in priority order — `UserID eq` (exact logon) → `Email eq` (+lowercase variant) → `BusinessPartnerFormattedName eq` (exact name) → **derived `FIRST.LAST` logon from the name** (this tenant's convention, e.g. MAROUANE.NAJAR) → two-token `substringof` on the name — every strategy requiring an UNAMBIGUOUS single row (`$top=2`, exactly 1 hit; guards common surnames). Returns `partyId` (**BusinessPartnerID** — what ticket assignments actually use), `EmployeeID` (the human-facing employee number, per the request), UserID, name, email + a full `tried` trail for console diagnosis. On match the panel saves `my-party-id` + a `my-party-label` ("Name · Employee NNN") + `my-party-source: "auto"`, and the briefing header shows **👤 Name · Employee NNN** *(detected from your SAP session — change in ⚙ Options)*. **Fallbacks are first-class**: detection failure (no SAP tab / nothing harvested / no employee record) renders the setup card with the SPECIFIC reason — e.g. live-verified on the reporter's own login: *"You're signed in to SAP as 'Jane Doe', but there's no matching employee record — admin accounts often aren't employees"* (confirmed: admin/service-agent logins aren't in EmployeeCollection; `ServiceAgentCollection` is RBAM-403 for the integration user) — plus a **🔍 Detect from SAP** button that re-runs detection on demand (bypasses the 30-min session negative-cache). Manual entry (setup card or Options) now sets `my-party-source: "manual"` and clears the stale auto label. **Shim rebuild + extension reload required.**
- **v0.27.46** — **Dashboard "MY DAY" — the AI-companion briefing (real value, not a launcher).** Reporter spec: *"Dashboard can be meaningful — No of tickets assigned to you (three places assignments can happen: Service Agent, Service Technician on header, Service Technician at item level), out of these your AI companion can search knowledge and give recommendations, focus on customer visits, make sure you have parts in van stock and customer received parts, I can see there are escalated tickets needing attention — show real value of AI companion."* **New shim endpoint `/api/get_my_day`**: resolves the operator (party ID param, or sign-in email → `EmployeeCollection.BusinessPartnerID` lookup — readable since the RBAM grant), then unions open tickets across ALL THREE assignment places — `ProcessorPartyID` (service agent, header) + `ServicePerformerPartyID` (technician, header) + `ServiceRequestItem.ServicePerformerPartyID` (technician, item level; parents fetched in OR-chunks of 12) — with per-ticket role tags, escalation flags, visit-due-within-7-days/overdue computation, and ZRP3 ordered-parts counts for the focus subset (escalated + due-soon, max 12, items fetched at concurrency 6). **Two calibration bugs caught by live testing against technician 8000010249**: (1) the naive `/escalat/i` text fallback matched **"Not Escalated"** → 137/137 false-escalations; now requires code `"2"` or text matching escalat without a leading "not" — same latent bug FIXED in v0.27.4's get_ticket_summary too; (2) "open" filter `lt '4'` included status-3 *"Sys.Completed"* rows years old → now `lt '3'` (Open + In Process only). Calibrated result: 85 real open tickets, 0 escalated, 84 overdue PMs. **Panel**: new "🧑‍🔧 My day — what I can do for you" section between the stats and Quick Actions, hydrated async on both dashboard-show paths (auto empty-state + 📊 toggle). Conversational briefing: *"You have **N open tickets** assigned — A as service agent, B as technician (ticket header), C via line items"* · 🚨 escalated alert line · 📅 visits-due line (*"I can prep each one: parts for your van stock, whether the customer received ordered parts, and KB fixes"*) · 📦 ordered-parts attention. Top-6 focus tickets render as rows: clickable #ID (opens in C4C) · subject · ESCALATED/OVERDUE/DUE SOON/📦 badges · **🔧 Prep button** that fires `/prep-pm <id>` directly (hides the dashboard to make room). Capability footer states what the companion can do (/kb-resolve · /parts-check · /prep-pm · customer replies). **Identity setup**: one-time inline card in the dashboard (party-ID input + Save → `chrome.storage.sync["my-party-id"]`) + a matching "My SAP identity" field in Options; email auto-resolution kicks in when blank. **Reload extension + shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.27.45** — **📊 manual dashboard toggle in the screen-indicator strip.** Reporter: *"where is dashboard icon"* — there wasn't one. The dashboard (greeting + AI-impact stats + quick actions + recent prompts, v0.26.131/132) is the EMPTY-STATE card: it auto-shows only when the panel has no context (no detected ticket, no reply/pulse on screen) and hides the moment a ticket is detected — which on ticket-heavy workflows means operators effectively never saw it and had no way to open it. **New 📊 button** next to ↻ / Show all in the indicator strip: opens the dashboard on demand from ANY screen (hydrates greeting + recent prompts + async stat tiles); clicking it again — or the dashboard's own ✕ — hides it and re-marks it session-dismissed so the focus-driven refreshContext doesn't resurrect it. Reuses the existing hydrate/hide plumbing; no new state. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.44** — **`/order-parts` search: full PRODUCT MASTER fallback (attempt 4) — unlocked by the user granting the integration user Products read access.** Follow-up to v0.27.43: the reporter granted the integration user full access in SAP (*"I logged in with my user, I have full auth"* → clarified the UI user ≠ API user → grant applied). Verified live: `ProductCollection` now readable (plain scan + filtered), **key field is `ProductID`** (`InternalID`/`ID` → 400 "Property not found in type Product"), and part 10013549 resolves: *"OPAMP,PA142,+/-160V OUT,SIP10"*, BaseUOM EA, Active. **Bonus: `InstallationPointCollection` is now readable too** — the /map city/state backfill (v0.27.21) automatically upgrades from 🌐 reverse-geocode to 🏢 InstallationPoint with zero code changes. **New attempt 4 in `searchParts`**: when stock catalog + line-item attempts all miss, query the product master — exact `ProductID` first, then `substringof` on `Description` for free text (≥4 chars). Hits feed the existing stockless-seed pipeline → synthetic rows with `onHand: null` → the UI's existing "⚪ ATP / no stock data" badge — zero renderer changes. Master seeds carry the product's REAL `BaseUOM` (e.g. ZDP consumables) instead of the hardcoded EA; non-active products get a ` [status]` suffix on the description so operators see they're picking an obsoleted material. On tenants WITHOUT the Products grant the 403 is caught and attempt 4 is a silent no-op (v0.27.43's escape hatch + message remain the fallback). `matchedBy: "ProductMaster (full product list — no stock/usage records)"` surfaces in the results banner (which v0.27.43 fixed). **Shim rebuild required; extension unchanged.**
- **v0.27.43** — **`/order-parts` search: manual add-by-ID escape hatch + honest no-matches message + banner-wipe bug fix.** Reporter searched `10013549` (OPAMP, clearly visible in SAP's own Products dialog with 167,646 entries) and got *"No matches"*. **Root cause — a coverage gap, not a search bug**: the parts search reads TWO sources — the stock catalog (`StockLocationItemCollection`) and past line items (`ServiceRequestItemCollection`, the v0.26.64 "stockless seeds" fallback). Part 10013549 has **zero stock rows AND zero usage history** (both verified by probe), and the third source — SAP's full product master — is unreachable: **`ProductCollection` returns HTTP 403 RBAM Not Authorized for the integration user** (`MaterialCollection` 404s — doesn't exist on this tenant). Same permission family as the InstallationPoint 403. Lasting fix = SAP admin grants the integration user read access to Products. **What shipped**: (1) the no-matches message now states the real coverage (*"…in the stock catalog or past line items — the two sources this search can read"*) + the 403 explanation + the admin hint, replacing the old generic "try a different keyword"; (2) **manual add-by-ID escape hatch** — when the query is material-ID-shaped (`/^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{5,18}$/`), a blue *"➕ Add '10013549' to cart anyway"* button adds it with no catalog data (UoM defaults EA; the SAP commit only needs the ProductID) + a verify-the-ID warning since a typo would create a line for the wrong part; (3) **BUG FIX** — a stray duplicate assignment in the results renderer (`innerHTML = parts…` immediately after `innerHTML += parts…`) was REPLACING the v0.26.59 "matched on X" / token-fallback banners, so they never actually rendered; append now preserves them. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.42** — **`/order-parts`: self-heal for SAP's "Schedule line type is not valid" on PM-contract tickets.** Reporter's commit of part 11083446 to PM ticket 4431605 failed with HTTP 500 `"Schedule line type is not valid"`. Diagnostic embed showed the cause: this PM ticket has NO prior parts rows, so `probeExistingServiceItem`'s v0.26.85 fallback mirrored the most-recent line — the **PM contract SERVICE line** — contaminating the parts POST with `SchedulingRelevant_KUT: "101"` (a schedule-line type valid for service visits, not parts), service UoM `ZDP` (not EA), and `ContractID`/`ContractItemID: "21"` linkage. **Fix — additive, error-driven two-stage heal in `logServiceItem`'s existing retry loop** (fires ONLY on that exact error message, so all currently-working flows are untouched): stage 1 drops `SchedulingRelevant_KUT` + `KUT_PlannedDurationHours_KUT` and normalizes `QuantityMeasureUnitCode` to the cart's UoM (or `EA`); if SAP still complains, stage 2 additionally drops `ContractID`/`ContractItemID` + `RequestedStart/EndDateTime`. Fits within the existing 3-attempt loop (original → stage 1 → stage 2). Documented as a new layer in docs/engineering-notes.md's LOCKED /order-parts section with an explicit warning NOT to make the strips unconditional (contract linkage may be legitimate when the probe found a real parts row). **Iteration 2 — the heal regex never fired on the first live retry**: SAP's actual message is `"Schedule line type␣␣is not valid"` with a DOUBLE space (an empty type placeholder interpolated into the message template); the literal single-space regex silently missed it. Now `/Schedule line type[\s\S]{0,30}?is not valid/i` — tolerates the empty-placeholder double space AND named variants ("Schedule line type XYZ is not valid"). **Iteration 3 — sharpened the lock-collision hint**: the operator's next retry hit `"object locked by JANE.DOE"` (their OWN SAP tab in Edit mode — SAP checks the lock before validating the schedule line, so the heal couldn't run). `commitErrorHint`'s lock branch now extracts the lock OWNER from the message and leads with the self-lock fix (*"If that's you: your SAP tab has the ticket open in Edit mode — switch to Display, retry"*) before the rarer other-user/integration cases. **Iteration 4 — ROOT CAUSE found + stage-3 product-history heal**: with the lock cleared, stages 1-2 fired correctly (diag showed the contamination gone) but the error persisted. Read-only probes revealed why: ALL 5 sampled ZRP3 rows tenant-wide are UoM `EA`, while product 11083446 (ZDP-unit DIBA tubing consumable) has been ordered **10/10 times as `SRP1` with `Planned = Actual = qty`** — it is NOT ZRP3-orderable; the blank in SAP's `"Schedule line type ␣ is not valid"` is a failed schedule-line-type customizing lookup for (product class × ZRP3).
