The user's *"this was working fine before — why broke today"*: it didn't — all prior commits were EA-unit spares on repair tickets; this is the first ZDP-unit consumable on a PM ticket. **Stage 3 — product-history FINDING, not a switch** (revised same-day after user pushback: *"its Order parts ZRP3 not Consume parts"*): stage 3 initially auto-retried under the historical code — but ordering (ZRP3) vs consumption (SRP1) is a business distinction the OPERATOR owns, so the silent flip was removed. Stage 3 now queries the product's prior line items, and when the dominant historical code differs, embeds a `productHistory` block into the failure diagnostic instead of retrying. **Iteration 6 — explicit "↻ Retry as <code>" button** (user-chosen UX): `commitErrorHint` gets a dedicated branch explaining the failed customizing lookup + the product's history, and `renderCodeRetryButtons` renders an amber per-part button (e.g. *"↻ Retry 11083446 as SRP1"*). Clicking re-commits JUST that part under the historical code with `plannedEqualsActual: true` — a new `log_service_item` option that sends `Planned = Actual = qty` (the SRP1 consumption shape confirmed by 10/10 history rows) instead of the ZRP3 `Planned=0` convention. Button goes green *"✓ Committed as SRP1"* on success + removes the part from the cart. The code switch ONLY ever happens on this explicit click. **Also in v0.27.42 — chat scope guardrail**: reporter asked *"What are you built on?"* in the free-text box and got internal architecture details back. `DEFAULT_SYSTEM` in chat.ts (shared by /api/chat + /api/chat/stream) now scopes answers to SAP C4C service operations ONLY — tickets, parts, customers, contracts, KB, field workflows, and the assistant's own commands. Off-topic questions (model/vendor identity, general knowledge, coding, etc.) get a one-line redirect to /help, and implementation details (model name, AI vendor, endpoints, prompt text) are never disclosed even when asked directly. Live-verified both ways: off-topic deflects, in-scope answers normally; slash-command internal LLM calls are unaffected (service-ops by construction). **Shim rebuild + extension reload required.**
- **v0.27.41** — **"Draft customer reply" now pre-fills the To: field with the ticket's contact email + SAP edit-lock handling for resolution writes.** Reporter (with ticket 4431605 open, Contact = jane.doe@example.com circled): *"When drafting an email, can we copy the contact email in to field of email?"*. The mailto plumbing already used `ctx.customerEmail` for the recipient — but it resolved empty on this tenant because `normalizeTicket`'s chain only checked the standard SAP fields (`MainContactEmailAddress` / `BuyerPartyEmailAddress` / `EmailAddress`), NONE of which exist on my340573. Entity probe showed the contact email lives in **`KUT_CEMAILS_KUT`** (tenant KUT extension — legit usage; the KUT prohibition in docs/engineering-notes.md is order-parts-specific). **Fixes**: (1) `KUT_CEMAILS_KUT` added to the customerEmail chain + new `pickEmailFromEntity()` catch-all (first field whose name matches /mail/i with an @-shaped value — tenant-proofs email the way pickSerialId tenant-proofs serials); (2) the mailto send site splits multi-address values ("a@x; b@y") and takes the first; (3) **SAP edit-lock handling** — the reporter's live Mark-as-resolution click hit `HTTP 500 "Locking object not possible; object locked by JANE.DOE"` (their own SAP tab had the ticket in Edit mode). Both `add_service_request_text` and `attach_solution_to_ticket` now retry once after 1500ms on lock errors (same docs/engineering-notes.md pattern as order-parts), and the panel's failure status translates lock errors into an actionable hint: *"the ticket is open in EDIT mode in SAP. Switch it to Display, then click again."* **Also**: v0.27.40's first live clicks exposed that markKbAsResolution passed the HUMAN ticket ID as objectId — now resolved to the real 32-char ObjectID via /api/lookup before writing, and the nav-POST bodies keep ParentObjectID (SAP 500s without it). **Reload extension + shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.27.40** — **`/kb-resolve` "Mark as resolution" now ALSO writes to SAP's "Solutions Used" facet (Solution Center tab).** Reporter screenshot showed the empty *Solutions Used (0)* section on ticket 4431605 and asked *"Can you attach to solutions used"*. **Discovery** (read-only, via `/api/debug/query`): the facet is backed by `ServiceRequestSolutionProposal` — the ONLY solution-shaped nav property on the ticket (11 navs total) — and the flat `ServiceRequestSolutionProposalCollection` carries dedicated fields for EXTERNAL knowledge bases: `ExternalKnowledgeBaseArticleID` / `ExternalKnowledgeBaseArticleURI` / `ExternalKnowledgeBaseArticleDescription` (+LanguageCode) / `Note` (+NoteLanguageCode) / `ParentObjectID`. SAP designed this entity for exactly the MindTouch case. **New shim tool `attach_solution_to_ticket`** (serviceOps.ts + uiServer.ts case + toolRegistry entry): POSTs `{ParentObjectID, ExternalKnowledgeBaseArticleID (≤40), ExternalKnowledgeBaseArticleURI (≤1000), ExternalKnowledgeBaseArticleDescription (≤255) + "EN", Note (≤2000) + "EN"}` through `runGatedWrite` (same three-gate model as every write). Self-healing strip-retry for SAP's *"Create not allowed for properties"* annotation errors — same v0.26.81 pattern as log_service_item, one retry with the rejected fields removed. **popup.js `markKbAsResolution`**: after the TypeCode-10022 note write succeeds, a second call attaches the article to Solutions Used — articleId extracted from the title via doc-number regex (`MAN0010249`-style, `[A-Z]{2,6}-?\d{5,9}`), URI + title from the card, Note = the LLM-extracted fix steps (≤1500 chars, bullets cleaned). **Non-fatal by design**: if the facet write fails (permissions, tenant variance), the note is already saved — status reads *"note saved; Solutions Used attach failed: <why>"* instead of failing the whole mark. Success status reads *"Resolution saved to ticket + Solutions Used"*. **Net effect**: clicking ✅ Mark as resolution now populates BOTH the Notes timeline (audit trail with full fix steps) AND the structured Solutions Used facet the SAP UI shows in Solution Center — the article appears in the exact place SAP reporting + other integrations expect solutions to live. **BONUS FIX — `add_service_request_text` was broken on this tenant ALL ALONG.** The user's first live click surfaced `POST /ServiceRequestTextCollection → HTTP 404 "Resource not found for the segment"`: the flat entity set is NOT exposed at the service root on my340573 — texts are only reachable through the navigation property on the parent ServiceRequest (which the READ path in getTicketNotes always used). Every note write — /close-out save, triage "Save as note", KB resolution note — had been 404ing whenever actually invoked. This is the MIRROR of the docs/engineering-notes.md line-item quirk (line items: nav POST 404s, flat works; texts: flat POST 404s, nav works). **Fix**: both `add_service_request_text` and `attach_solution_to_ticket` now try flat first and fall back to the navigation POST (`/ServiceRequestCollection('<id>')/ServiceRequestTextCollection` resp. `/ServiceRequestSolutionProposal`) on segment-not-found, with `ParentObjectID` dropped from the nav body (implied by the URL). **Reload extension + shim rebuild required (new tool endpoint).**
- **v0.27.39** — **Panel UX overhaul: 9 clutter fixes (reporter: *"its clumsy for end user"*).** The main panel had grown to 16 multi-colored chips in jittery rows, a 6-control action bar, a full-width detected-ticket banner, and dev plumbing in the footer. Nine coordinated changes: **(1) Top-6 chips + "+N more ▾" toggle** — chips beyond the first 6 collapse behind a dashed meta-chip; expanded state is session-only so every panel open starts compact. Doesn't engage unless ≥2 chips would hide. **(2) Compact textarea + self-clearing prefill** — the free-text box collapses to a single line (38px) when empty/unfocused and expands on focus or content (`:not(:placeholder-shown)`); chip auto-fire now clears its own prefill after the run completes (only if the operator didn't edit it mid-run — `ta.value === filled` guard), returning the box to compact. The prefill stays visible DURING the run as a "what's running" indicator. **(3) Detected-ticket pill** — `🎫 Ticket 4386849 · detected` inline pill (`.selection-pill`, display:inline-flex) replaces the full-width banner; the banner treatment remains for SLA warnings + right-click selections (multi-line content), which explicitly strip the pill class. `maybeShowEmptyState`'s presence check updated from `display === "block"` to `!== "none"` so the pill counts. **(4) Action-bar overflow** — Save / Annotate / Clear-annotations moved into a `⋯` dropdown; bar now reads Ask · Clear · ⋯ · ⚙. Button IDs unchanged so all existing wiring (annotate flow toggling clear-annotations visibility, init listeners) works untouched; menu closes on inner click + outside click. **(5) Category = color, 4 learnable families** — full background tints replace the 7 random-feeling left-border accents: green=triage, orange=parts, blue=field, violet=knowledge; comms/close/other/user stay neutral slate so the 4 families keep meaning. **(6) Adaptive chip ordering** — per-chip run counts in `chrome.storage.local` `c4s.chip-usage`, bumped only on real runs (not Shift-click edits); render sorts count-desc with stable tie-break to canonical order, so a fresh install looks exactly like the curated default until usage accumulates. The top-6 visible set thus self-personalizes. Reset via Options → "↺ Reset chip order". **(7) Shim status dot** — footer's "Connects to your C4S shim" sentence replaced by a live dot (green=reachable, red=down, slate=checking; 2.5s-timeout GET / ping, 10s throttle, re-pinged on focus via refreshContext). Tooltip carries endpoint URL + the start-c4s-dev.bat remediation hint. **(8) Stable chip grid** — `.template-chips` switched from flex-wrap to `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(118px, 1fr))`: every chip is cell-width, rows align into tidy columns, no more row-width jitter; long labels ellipsize. **(9) Empty-state polish** — the v0.26.131 dashboard already covers the no-ticket case; its tip is now bolded ("**Open a ticket in SAP** to unlock…") and mentions PM prep. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.38** — **NEW: `/prep-pm` — pre-visit PM briefing that consolidates last PM date + FSE, cumulative prior labour, corrective maintenance since last PM, parts replacement history, system configuration, open follow-ups, and LLM-inferred PM kit + gap analysis into one panel.** Reporter spec: *"A field service engineer is preparing for an upcoming preventive maintenance visit. Instead of manually searching service history, prior labor entries, installed components, corrective maintenance history, and historical parts usage across multiple tabs and systems, they open the ticket and click: 'Prep for PM.' Within seconds: 'Last PM completed March 12, 2025 by Tom Doe · total prior labor time: 3.5 hours · corrective maintenance completed twice since last PM (pressure fault, leak detection issue) · parts previously replaced: PM kit, O-rings, inline filter · system configuration includes Ultimate 3000 pump, autosampler, RS detector · no open follow-up issues detected.'"* — built as a new slash command + shim endpoint + dedicated renderer. **NEW shim endpoint `/api/get_pm_prep`** (uiServer.ts) — pulls in parallel: (1) current ticket entity for serial + product + InstallationPointID, (2) past tickets on the SAME serial via direct OData with $select including `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_Type` (the existing `getSerialHistory` tool doesn't $select KUT fields, so we use direct OData here), (3) per past ticket, ServiceRequestItemCollection filtered by ParentObjectID — concurrency 6, capped at 30 most-recent past tickets, sums labour (ACTLAB) + travel (ACTRVL) + collects parts (ZRP1/SRP1 — consumption allowlist per docs/engineering-notes.md), (4) InstallationPointCollection entity for system-config details (graceful 403 fallback — known limitation on Thermo Fisher integration user), (5) last PM ticket's notes (TypeCode 10022 Service Response Reports) for the client-side LLM kit-gap step. Aggregations computed server-side: **last PM** = newest closed ticket with `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_Type` ∈ {131 PM Contract, 141 IQ/OQ, 181 PM Billable}; date + FSE name resolved via 6-field chain (ServicePerformerPartyName → ProcessorPartyName), **cumulative labour** across all past tickets (separate labour + travel — FSE wants to see "X hours on equipment, Y hours driving"), **corrective maintenance since last PM** = NOT-a-PM tickets closed AFTER lastPM date, **parts history** deduped by ProductDescription with timesReplaced + lastReplacedAt, **open follow-ups** = past tickets not closed (the spec's "no open follow-up issues detected" check). Same `detectKind` / `detectQty` regex chain as `/api/get_ticket_summary` so labour detection works across the 8 tenant field-name variants. **NEW slash command `/prep-pm`** (popup.js) — calls the shim endpoint, then runs ONE LLM call client-side to infer PM kit contents + gaps from past PM notes + parts history (since `get_pm_kit` is "blocked" per toolRegistry.ts — no exposed BOM entity in this tenant's $metadata, mirroring how `/pm-kit-check` infers kit contents from history rather than a real lookup). Strict-JSON output prompt returns `inferredKitContents[]` + `gaps[]` + `shortNarrative`. **NEW renderer `renderPmPrepPanel`** with independent `.pmprep-*` CSS namespace so `/ticket-summary` (`.tsum-*`) and `/interactions` (`.intsum-*`) styles are unaffected. **Layout** (top to bottom): (1) Header strip — ticket ID + subject + customer/product/serial pills + current secondary-type, (2) **Headline card** — the spec-style one-liner with color-coding (green if PM within 12mo, amber 12-24mo, red if no PM or 24mo+) showing "Last PM completed [date] ([Nd] ago) by [FSE] · total prior labour: Xh · N correctives since last PM · parts previously replaced: A, B, C · N open follow-ups", (3) Last PM detail card — date + FSE + subject + type + clickable ticket link, (4) Cumulative effort tiles (3-up grid) — prior labour / prior travel / tickets with labour, (5) Corrective maintenance table — Ticket # · Closed · Type · Subject, (6) Parts replacement history table — Part · Times · Last replaced · Material ID, sorted by frequency, (7) PM kit + gap analysis with violet-tinted background — narrative + Inferred kit contents list + amber-highlighted Likely gaps list, (8) System configuration kv-grid — primary equipment + InstallationPoint + location (graceful empty fallback when SAP returns 403), (9) Open follow-ups table — amber-tinted with "Review BEFORE arriving" warning, (10) Footer with "↗ Open current ticket in SAP C4C" button + collapsible diagnostic. **Chip placement**: `🔧 Prep for PM` in the parts category, sits right after `/pm-kit-check` in the chip order so it pairs naturally with kit-gap workflow. **Other wiring**: added to `isSlowSlash` regex (progress strip activates for the multi-second fetch), added to `/help` text. **Net effect**: an FSE planning a PM visit clicks one chip and within 3-5 seconds sees the entire equipment briefing they would have had to manually assemble from Service History · Labor · Parts · Components tabs across 5+ minutes of SAP navigation. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required (new endpoint).**
- **v0.27.37** — **`/kb-resolve`: "✅ Mark as resolution" button per KB article — one-click writeback that links the helpful KB article to the ticket as a Service Response Report note.** Reporter: *"Can we assign the relevant resolution to Ticket by clicking a button whichever article helped the technician to fix the issue"*. With KB direct mode finally returning real articles (v0.27.31-35 chain), the panel was still read-only — operators had to manually retype/paste the article URL + fix steps into a SAP note. Now there's a green **✅ Mark as resolution** button on every card (next to *Open article ↗*). One click appends a structured note via the existing `add_service_request_text` writeback path (TypeCode `10022` — same code `/close-out` uses for Service Response Reports, so the note appears in the same SAP timeline category). **Note shape** (chosen via in-flight design Q to keep audit-trail full): `📚 KB Resolution Applied (via Ask C4S /kb-resolve)` header, then `Article: <title>`, `URL: <url>`, `Why this article: <oneLineSummary or matchReason>` (the LLM's relevance reasoning from /kb-resolve), `Fix steps:` followed by the LLM-extracted resolution as a bulleted list (`renderResolution`-equivalent cleanup — strips bullet markers, splits on newlines, drops empties), then `Marked: <local datetime>` footer. The `📚` prefix is intentional — `/interactions` can split *"KB resolution applied"* from other note categories in a future iteration without extra schema. **Multiple-mark semantics**: each click writes a NEW note. Real fixes often pull from 2-3 articles; rather than force-pick one resolution, every helpful article becomes its own timeline entry. To prevent accidental double-writes within the same panel render, the button transitions to a disabled grey `✓ Marked as resolution` state after a successful write — backed by an in-memory `Set<\`${ticketObjectId}::${articleUrl}\`>` (`kbResolutionMarkedSet`) so re-renders within the same popup session preserve the visual state. Re-opening the popup resets the session state; the underlying SAP note from the prior click remains on the ticket. **Wiring**: button click is event-delegated on `document` (mirrors the existing `.panel-refresh` pattern) so it works for both render call sites — the standalone `kb-resolutions-panel` AND the `KB RESOLUTIONS` section inside `/knowledge`. Each card carries `data-kbr-mark-key="<articleUrl-or-title>"`; the handler looks the article up in a module-level `Map` rebuilt on every render (`kbResolutionArticleIndex.clear()` at the top of `renderKbResolutionCards`). Button renders ONLY when (a) `$("prompt").dataset.detectedTicket` carries a valid ObjectID — no ticket on screen means no destination for the note, AND (b) the article has a stable identifier. **UI feedback**: green gradient (`#16a34a → #15803d`) for the active button; grey gradient (`#6b7280 → #4b5563`) once marked; brightness hover, no-pointer disabled state. `kbr-card-link` row converted to flex with `justify-content: flex-end` so Mark button + Open article link sit side-by-side neatly. Action status bar surfaces *"Saving resolution to ticket…"* during the write and *"Resolution saved to ticket — <article title>"* on success, or `Couldn't save resolution: <error>` on failure (button re-enables so operator can retry). Gated by the same `feature.auto_writeback_enabled` flag as `/order-parts` and `/close-out` — if writeback is off the SAP write returns blocked and the panel surfaces the error. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.30** — **`/history` REDEFINED: now actually does serial history (past tickets for this equipment's serial number).** Reporter: *"so is it supposed to be serial history?"* after v0.27.29 fixed the field-name typo and surfaced the truth — `/history` was hitting the SHIM tool `get_service_history` (navigation-property discovery on a single ticket) when the chip description had ALWAYS promised *"Past tickets for this equipment's serial number across customers and dates"*. The chip metadata (`📜 Service history`) was written for the right intent; the handler was wired to the wrong MCP tool. Naming collision between `get_service_history` (diagnostic) and `get_serial_history` (real serial history) caused it. **Now matches the chip**. The handler: (1) looks up the ticket via `/api/lookup` to get the entity, (2) extracts `SerialID` via the 10-field-name catch-all `pickSerialId()` (handles `SerialID` / `EquipmentID` / `InstallationPointID` / `KUT_*` variants — same helper `/warranty` and `/knowledge` use), (3) calls `/api/tools/get_serial_history` with `{serialId, top: 25, includeClosed: true, orderBy: "LastChangeDateTime desc"}` — the same shim tool `/warranty` already uses, (4) renders newest-first as `<rank>. <ID> — <subject>` followed by `<status> · <date> · <customer>` per row, (5) excludes the current ticket from the results so the operator only sees PRIOR tickets. **Header line** shows `Serial: <id> · Equipment: <product>` + `<N> past ticket(s)` so the operator sees the scope at a glance. **Error paths**: (a) no SerialID extractable → info message *"This ticket doesn't carry a serial we can extract"* with pointers to `/visits` and `/similar` as alternatives, (b) zero past tickets → info message explaining the SerialID might not be propagating consistently, (c) HTTP failure on the SAP call → error with the raw body. **`/help` text** updated: was *"Pull service history rows for a ticket"* (vague), now *"Past tickets for this equipment's serial number (across customers + dates)"*. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.29** — **`/history`: field-name regression — every call returned "No history rows" regardless of how much history existed.** Reporter screenshot: `/history 4316974` on a fully-populated ticket (Service Location, Contract, Service Level, Installation Point 2374092, etc.) returned the empty "No history rows" message. Bug: handler read `lookData.history.groups` and `g.rows.length` — neither field exists on the shim response. **Actual shape** from `/api/lookup` (shim's `cachedGetServiceHistory` → `getServiceHistory`): `lookData.history = {sourceTicketObjectId, history: [{navigationProperty, targetEntityType, data: {d: {results: [...]}}, ...}, ...], failures, discovery}`. So the array lives at `lookData.history.history` and per-entry row counts at `g.data.d.results.length`. **Fix**: `lookData?.history?.groups` → `lookData?.history?.history`; `g.rows.length` → `g?.data?.d?.results?.length ?? g?.data?.results?.length ?? 0` (handles both V1's `{d:{results}}` and the unwrapped shape some tenants return). **Bonus** — also surface the `failures` bucket so the operator sees which navigation properties SAP rejected (usually auth-scope or metadata mismatch), rather than silently dropping them: lines appear at the end of the summary as `Failed (N): • <navProperty>: <error snippet>`. **Why it went unnoticed**: `/history` is a v0.4-era diagnostic command rarely used directly — operators get the same data via `/interactions` (timeline view) and `/ticket-summary` (operational facts). The shim's response shape changed at some point and the handler wasn't updated to match. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.28** — **`/map`: stop v0.27.27's `/nearby` pre-fill from leaking into subsequent bare `/map` opens.** Reporter: *"Now MAP View also defaulting the Secondary ticket types, Only nearby should default Map view should work as usual like earlier"*. Bug: HTML input values persist while the map panel is hidden (the panel uses `display: none/flex`, not destroy/recreate). After `/nearby` set `map-filter-secondary-type` to `"131,141,181"` and `map-filter-city` to the operator's ZIP, opening `/map` directly later still showed those values because v0.27.27's `openMapPanel` only SET values when the caller provided them — it never CLEARED stale values from prior calls. So `/map` second-open inherited the `/nearby` filters. **Fix — `data-prefilled-by` attribute tracking**. When `openMapPanel` auto-fills `map-filter-city` or `map-filter-secondary-type`, it tags the element with `data-prefilled-by="command"`. On the NEXT `openMapPanel` call, if the caller doesn't pre-fill that same input AND the tag is still present, the value is cleared and the tag removed. A one-time `input` listener on each pre-filled element removes the tag the moment the operator types anything — at that point the value is treated as user-owned and never auto-cleared again. **Net effect**: user-typed values persist across map opens (matches pre-v0.27.27 behavior the reporter described as *"earlier"*); chip-pre-filled values clear themselves the next time `/map` opens without them. `/nearby` chip still pre-fills both fields as before; the only change is that opening `/map` afterwards starts fresh. Applied to both `city` and `secondaryTypeCodes` for symmetry (reporter only flagged secondary type, but the same leak existed for city). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.27** — **`/nearby` REDEFINED: now opens the map view filtered to PM-related tickets within radius of the operator's location (old same-customer bundling deleted).** User direction: *"replace /nearby with what i have mentioned earlier, do not add same-customer"*. The old `/nearby` was customer-bundling (other open tickets at the SAME customer for one-site-visit work-stacking) — misnamed since "Nearby" naturally implies geographic. Now it does what the label promised: opens `/map` filtered to **PM-related Secondary Types (codes 131 PM Contract, 141 IQ/OQ, 181 PM Billable)** within radius of the **operator's current location** (browser geolocation → reverse-geocoded ZIP). **New helper `resolveOperatorZip()`** — three-tier resolution: (1) `chrome.storage.local` saved preference (`c4s.operator-zip`, 7-day TTL) for instant subsequent clicks; (2) `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition()` with 8s timeout → `reverseGeocode(lat, lon)` (the v0.27.20 helper) → ZIP; (3) `window.prompt()` fallback if geolocation denied or yields no postal code. Successful resolutions save back to the storage key so the second click is instant. **Multi-code Secondary Type filter** (popup.js `applyMapFilters` extension): the input now detects comma-separated all-numeric values like `"131,141,181"` and builds a multi-OR clause `(KUT_Secondary_Ticket_Type eq '131' or eq '141' or eq '181')`. Single values keep existing substringof/eq behavior — fully backward compatible. **`openMapPanel` extended** to accept `initialFilters.city` (pre-fills the City/ZIP input) and `initialFilters.secondaryTypeCodes` (pre-fills the Secondary Type input with the joined codes). The standard `applyMapFilters()` auto-call at panel open then picks both up. **Chip update**: label stays `📍 Nearby PMs`, description rewritten to describe the new behavior, `screenTypes` widened from `["ticket"]` to `["all"]` since the new flow is geographic (works from any screen). **Manifest**: added `"geolocation"` to `permissions` — Chrome prompts the user once for location access. **Check-in auto-fire REMOVED**: the v0.7.0 check-in flow used to auto-fire `/nearby` to surface same-customer bundling immediately after check-in. Since `/nearby` no longer does customer bundling, the auto-fire is gone — check-in still records the customer + start time, operators can manually open `/map` for geographic context. **Old handler deleted entirely** — no `/same-customer` or `/bundle` slash kept as a backup (per explicit user instruction "do not add same-customer"). The `nearby-panel` renderer (line 13880) is now dead code but left in place to avoid touching the locked /map UI rendering paths. **Net effect**: clicking `📍 Nearby PMs` now opens the map view centered on your location with PM-related tickets pre-filtered — one click from anywhere to "what PM work is near me right now?". **Reload extension; the geolocation permission triggers a one-time browser prompt on first click.**
- **v0.27.26** — **`/map` table view: surface the city/state source per row (🏢 InstallationPoint / 🌐 reverse-geocode / 📋 SAP entity).** Reporter: *"Now can you check if city and state is from installation point or reverse geo coding in the table view?"*. The data was already on each row via `_cityStateSource` — set in v0.27.10 (SAP ticket entity path, implicit/undefined), v0.27.20 (`"reverse-geocode"`), v0.27.21 (`"installation-point"` or `"installation-point-cache"`). Just needed to surface it. **Per-row icon**: small dimmed marker appended to the City cell — `🏢` for SAP InstallationPoint FK lookup (authoritative — what SAP shows), `🌐` for Nominatim reverse-geocode (approximate — derived from lat/lon when SAP didn't have the address), `📋` for direct SAP ticket entity field (`ServiceLocationCity` etc., the v0.27.10 path), and no marker when the row has no city/state at all. Hover tooltip on the marker explains the source in plain language: *"From SAP InstallationPoint (cached lookup)"* / *"Derived from lat/lon via Nominatim reverse-geocode (approximate)"* / *"From SAP ticket entity directly (e.g., ServiceLocationCity field)"*. **Row tooltip extended** with a `City/State source:` line so hovering anywhere on the row also reveals it. **Table title coverage breakdown**: the v0.27.7 coverage line in the title bar now includes `City/State source: 🏢 12 · 🌐 8 · 📋 4 · — 2` so the operator can see at a glance how many of each source the current filtered set has. Helps catch when backfill is heavy-lifting (lots of 🌐 = SAP doesn't have addresses, we're deriving) vs. when the entity carries the data already (lots of 📋). **CSS** (`.map-table-city-source`): 0.78em, 0.65 opacity, `cursor: help` — readable but doesn't compete visually with the city name. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.25** — **`/map`: country-aware Radius default (100 mi US / 100 km elsewhere) + persist the operator's manual choice.** Reporter: *"Can you default the radius to 100 Miles if it is USA and equivalent KMs if other regions"*. Previously the radius dropdown opened on *"No radius filter"* and the unit defaulted to whatever was first in the radio list — operators had to manually pick both every time. **Fix**: new `applyMapRadiusDefault()` helper runs after `wireMapInteractions` on map open. Two-pass logic: **(1) Restore saved preference** — checks `chrome.storage.local` for `c4s.map-radius-pref` (set whenever the operator changes radius or unit AND radius > 0); if found, applies it. **(2) Country-aware default** for first-time use — reads `navigator.language`, matches `-([A-Z]{2})$`, and if the country code is `US` sets unit to `mi`, otherwise `km`. Value is `100` in both cases — same dropdown selection, just different unit. The reasoning: operators think in round numbers, 100 covers about a day's drive radius, and the dropdown already has `100` as a stocked option (no HTML changes needed). **Persistence wiring**: `saveMapRadiusPreference()` listens on the radius `<select>` change event AND the unit radio button change event. Saves both values to `chrome.storage.local` whenever radius > 0. *"No radius filter"* (value 0) is treated as a transient state — we don't save it as a sticky preference, so closing the panel mid-search with radius cleared still defaults back next time. **Double-wire guard** (`radiusSel._c4sRadiusPersistWired = true`) prevents stacking duplicate listeners if `openMapPanel` is called repeatedly. **DevTools log** on every map open: `[c4s] map: radius default applied — 100 mi (source: saved-preference)` or `(source: country-default)` so the source is traceable. **Net effect**: first-time US operators see `100 mi` selected when they open `/map`; first-time non-US operators see `100 km`. Once they manually adjust (say to `50 mi` or `250 km`), their choice sticks across sessions. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.24** — **`/map`: hide the Priority filter (always "Any" in real operator workflow).** Reporter: *"Now remove Priority as filter because its always Any"*. The dropdown was rarely-if-ever moved off the default — operators on this tenant don't filter by `ServicePriorityCode` at the map level (they look at all priorities within their geographic scope, then use the Priority column on individual tickets to triage). The filter was visual clutter. **Fix**: `<div class="map-filter-group" hidden>` wrapper. Element + `<select id="map-filter-priority">` stay in the DOM (still queryable via `$("map-filter-priority")?.value`) so the JS clauses-builder logic at popup.js line 16161 is COMPLETELY unchanged. `filters.priority` always resolves to `""` (the empty Any option), so the `clauses.push(\`ServicePriorityCode eq …\`)` branch is silently skipped. **Zero code changes in popup.js** — pure HTML toggle. Re-exposing the filter (e.g., on a tenant where Priority IS used for map-level narrowing) is a one-line revert: drop the `hidden` attribute from the wrapper. **Net effect**: filter sidebar is slightly tighter; nothing else changes. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.23** — **`/map`: "Max tickets to map" default raised 50 → 200; cap raised 100 → 200; silent radius-mode bump REMOVED.** Reporter (after the v0.27.22 lockdown) flagged the UX inconsistency: *"In that case we should always keep the default value in MAX tickets to 200 instead of 50?"*. They were right — the sidebar showed `50` but radius mode silently executed `200` via `Math.max(filters.top, 200)`. What the operator picked wasn't what ran. Since the FSM workflow is almost always radius-mode, the slider's value was essentially decorative. **Fix**: (1) HTML `<select>` adds a `200` option as the new selected default; old `25` / `50` / `100` options retained so operators can dial down for fast snapshots. (2) JS clamp raised `Math.min(100, ...)` → `Math.min(200, ...)`, default `"50"` → `"200"`. (3) The `effectiveTop = filters.radiusKm > 0 ? Math.max(filters.top, 200) : filters.top` silent bump eliminated — `effectiveTop = filters.top` now. The variable name kept so the existing diagnostic logs (`mapState._effectiveTop`, `console.log("[c4s] map: search outcome", { effectiveTop })`) still surface a meaningful value without rewiring downstream. **Tooltip updated**: was *"Default 50. In radius mode this is automatically bumped to 200 for a wider candidate pool"* — now reads *"Default 200 — matches what radius mode needs. Drop to 50 or 25 only if your tenant is slow or you want a quick snapshot. v0.27.23 removed the silent radius-mode bump (was '50 means 200'), so what you pick here is what runs."* **Tradeoff**: non-radius searches go from ~500ms to ~1.5s by default. Acceptable because (a) browsing without radius is uncommon for FSMs, (b) operators who need a fast snapshot can dial down explicitly, (c) the 200-row OData payload (~2 MB) is fine on modern connections. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.22** — **`/map`: two bugs hiding the city/ZIP filter — substring-dedup ate alternatives + client-side filter applied via InstallationPoint backfill.** Reporter trace: 4 failed SAP queries trying `ServiceLocationCity` → `ServiceLocationCityName` → `ServiceLocationCustomerCityName` → `IncidentLocationCity`. **Bug 1 — substring-dedup**: smart-strip's alternative-picker filtered via `c.clause.includes(a)` — too aggressive. After swap to `ServiceLocationCityName`, the clause TEXT now contains the substring `"CityName"` and `"City"` — so when looking at the next alternatives `CityName` and `City` (bare versions, which would have been the right ones on many tenants), the dedup said *"already used, skip"*. Only the alternatives that don't appear as substrings inside an already-used field name got tried. **Fix 1**: word-boundary regex match — `new RegExp(\`\\b${alt}\\b\`).test(clause)`. Now bare `City` only matches if there's a non-word character (or start/end) on either side, so `ServiceLocationCityName` no longer hides `City` or `CityName` as candidate alternatives. **Bug 2 — fields don't exist anyway**: even with all 5 alternatives tried, none of the city fields are exposed on `ServiceRequest` on this tenant. Address lives on `InstallationPoint` only (the FK the reporter pointed out in v0.27.21). Same situation as `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` from v0.27.19. **Fix 2**: client-side city/ZIP filter using the v0.27.21 InstallationPoint backfill. Operator's input ("92592" / "Temecula") gets stashed in `clientSideCityCriterion` when the city clause is added. After SAP returns rows AND after the InstallationPoint backfill populates `city` / `state` / `postalCode` / `country` on each row, if the city clause was dropped, the criterion is matched **case-insensitive against any of those fields**. So `92592` matches the ZIP, `Temecula` matches the city name, `California` matches the state — all work transparently. **`mapState.markers` array also narrowed** to match the filtered row set, then `renderMapTiles()` re-renders the map so the visual matches the table. **Status row** shows informational note: *"ℹ city/ZIP '92592' filter applied CLIENT-SIDE (kept 12 of 50; server-side ServiceLocationCity etc. don't exist on this tenant — matched against InstallationPoint-backfilled city/state/postal)"*. **Net effect**: combining secondary-type + city/ZIP now actually returns the right tickets even when SAP can't filter by city server-side. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.21** — **`/map`: city/state backfill now uses SAP `InstallationPoint` FK (was reverse-geocoding lat/lon).** Reporter screenshot of a real ticket: `InstallationPointID: 2921929` clearly visible on the ticket entity, also surfaced in the "Installation Point" field on the SAP UI. The InstallationPoint entity in SAP is the canonical owner of the service-location address — same data the SAP UI shows under "Service Location" but joined via a clean FK rather than denormalized onto every ticket. The reporter rightly called this out: *"Why can't you get the location from installation point id as you already have ticket"*. Beats the v0.27.20 reverse-geocode hack on every axis: (1) no external dependency (pure SAP), (2) no rate limits (parallel SAP calls vs serial 1.1s Nominatim queue), (3) authoritative source (matches what SAP UI shows under Service Location), (4) free, (5) works on tenants where Nominatim wouldn't recognize a private/industrial postcode anyway. **New helper** `fetchInstallationPointAddress(id, endpoint, headers)`: hits `/InstallationPointCollection?$filter=InstallationPointID eq 'NNN'&$top=1` via the existing `/api/debug/query` shim passthrough (no new SAP route needed), extracts `city` / `state` / `country` / `postalCode` from the response. Field-name guards: tries `AddressCity` / `City` / `CityName` / `ServiceLocationCity` etc. for city (SAP exposes this differently per tenant config), `AddressStateCodeText` / `AddressStateCode` / `StateCode` / `Region` for state, similar fallbacks for country and postal. Cached under `c4s.installation-point-cache` keyed by InstallationPointID — **90-day TTL** since installation addresses rarely change (longer than reverse-geocode's 30d since the data is authoritative). **New orchestrator** `backfillCityStateFromInstallationPoint(allRows)`: groups rows by unique `InstallationPointID` (or `InstallationPointIDV1` fallback) so multiple tickets at the same installation share ONE SAP call. Runs SAP fetches **in parallel** with concurrency cap of 8 — much faster than reverse-geocode's serial queue. Re-renders the table modal progressively after each batch. **Reverse-geocode demoted to ULTIMATE fallback** (still runs after the InstallationPoint backfill for any row that's STILL missing city/state — e.g., tickets without InstallationPointID set, or installations whose entity doesn't have address fields). The chain is now: SAP entity fields (v0.27.10) → InstallationPoint FK (v0.27.21, new primary) → Nominatim reverse-geocode (v0.27.20, last resort). **Row builder change**: each `base` row now stashes `_ticket: <original entity>` (marked with `_` prefix so it's recognized as metadata, not display data) so the InstallationPoint backfill can read `InstallationPointID` / `InstallationPointIDV1` without re-fetching. **Net effect**: for the reporter's 36 mappable tickets, expect ~5-10 unique installations to be looked up (1-3 seconds total in parallel — much faster than the 30-second reverse-geocode estimate). Cached aggressively so subsequent map opens are instant. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.20** — **`/map`: reverse-geocode lat/lon → city/state to backfill empty address columns.** Reporter screenshot: 36 mappable tickets in the table view, ALL rows showing City="—" and State="—" despite having visible markers on the map. v0.27.10 added ~30 SAP field-name fallbacks + a wildcard sniff for `City` / `State` / `Region` / etc. — and they STILL came up empty. Two possible reasons: (1) the tenant uses yet another field naming convention we haven't covered, OR (2) the SAP entity simply doesn't carry address fields on these tickets even though `KUT_RPLatitude_KUT` / `KUT_RPLongitude_KUT` are populated (common for Tech Support tickets with rooftop coordinates but no service-address record). Either way, chasing more field names is a dead end. **Real fix — derive city/state from coordinates we ALREADY have**. New `reverseGeocode(lat, lon)` helper hits Nominatim's `/reverse?lat=X&lon=Y&addressdetails=1` endpoint and parses the response: city pulled from `city || town || village || municipality || hamlet || suburb || county` (Nominatim varies by region), state from `ISO3166-2-lvl4` (ISO subdivision code, e.g., "NC") or `address.state`, country from `country_code`, postal from `postcode`. **Aggressive caching**: results stored under `c4s.reverse-geocode-cache` keyed by lat,lon rounded to 4 decimals (~11m resolution). Multiple tickets at the same site share one cache entry. 30-day TTL for positive matches, 5-min for failures (matching the v0.27.12 geocode cache pattern). **Background backfill**: new `backfillCityStateFromCoords(allRows)` runs AFTER `renderMapMarkersFromRows` builds the initial row set. Groups rows by rounded lat/lon (so 5 tickets at the same site = 1 Nominatim call). Iterates groups, reverse-geocodes (respecting Nominatim's 1.1s queue), stamps every row at each site with the discovered city/state/country/postal. Re-renders the table modal progressively if it's currently open — operator sees rows fill in one site at a time. Logs `[c4s] map: 30 unique sites need reverse-geocoding for city/state` at start, `[c4s] map: reverse-geocode filled city/state on 28 / 30 sites` at end. **Source tag**: filled rows get `_cityStateSource = "reverse-geocode"` so future code paths (e.g., download CSV) can mark them as derived rather than authoritative. **Net effect**: City + State columns now populate for every row that has lat/lon — works UNIVERSALLY regardless of how the SAP tenant names its address fields. Tradeoff: first table view open shows blank cells for ~30 seconds while Nominatim is queried for unique sites (1.1s per call); subsequent opens are instant from cache. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.19** — **`/map`: CLIENT-SIDE due-on-site filter as ultimate fallback — guarantees the filter works on every tenant regardless of OData support.** Reporter: *"Can you do something about this seriously its been back and forth multiple times but not fixed yet"*. The previous 5 versions (v0.27.13 → v0.27.17) tried to make `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` work server-side — primary field, literal-format swap, bitmask combo search, alternative field-name chain. Every attempt eventually 400'd on this tenant. The honest answer: **`OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` is not filterable in OData on this tenant**, none of the alternative SAP-standard date fields (`ServiceExecutionDueDateTime`, `OnSiteDueDateTime`, etc.) exist either, and no amount of literal-format gymnastics changes that. **Real fix**: when the server-side clause exhausts all alternatives and gets dropped, apply the filter CLIENT-SIDE post-fetch. The row builder already does field-chain resolution for the table column's "Due On Site" value (`OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` → `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` → `OnSiteDueDateTime` → `ScheduledStartDateTime` → `RequestedEndDateTime`) — we use that same chain on each returned row, parse the date with `parseSapDate`, and keep only rows whose due date falls inside the operator's [now, now+N days] window. **Three components**: (1) `clientSideDueDateRange = {fromMs, toMs}` is stashed when the operator sets a due-days filter. (2) After the SAP search returns, if `droppedKinds` includes `"due-on-site"`, the client-side filter is applied — `rows.filter(t => parseDateFromAnyField(t) in range)`. Rows without ANY parseable due date are excluded (matches OData `ge X and le Y` semantics — null dates don't satisfy the range). (3) Status row shows an INFORMATIONAL note (not a warning): *"ℹ due-on-site filter applied CLIENT-SIDE on this tenant — kept N of M tickets in the X-day window (server-side OData filter on the date field was rejected; we filtered post-fetch using the row's date value)"*. **`MAX_ATTEMPTS` bumped 8 → 16**: the smart-strip needs room to fully exhaust all alternatives before dropping the clause (with 2 date clauses × 2 formats × 6 alternatives = up to 24 server-side attempts in the worst case; the loop bails when clear no field/format works). **Net effect**: the operator's filter ALWAYS takes effect now. If SAP can do it server-side, great. If not, we filter client-side. Either way, the result set matches the operator's intent. **Performance note**: client-side filtering doesn't change the SAP fetch — we still pull the same `$top=200` candidate pool. We just narrow the displayed set after fetch. Negligible cost on a 200-row pool. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.18** — **`/map`: Secondary Type dropdown — hide the technical code suffix from the visual UI (keep it in DOM via `data-code`).** Reporter screenshot: every Secondary Type dropdown option rendered as TWO stacked lines — *"Pre Installation"* bold on top, *"Pre Installation — 101"* smaller and lighter underneath. Same pattern for all 17 options. *"Can you show only the first line text and hide the technicals — just hide don't delete"*. **Root cause**: Chrome's `<datalist>` UI shows TWO lines when an `<option>`'s `value` attribute differs from its inner text content — first line bold = `value`, second line lighter = inner text. v0.27.11's option template was `<option value="Pre Installation">Pre Installation — 101</option>` — value `"Pre Installation"` ≠ text `"Pre Installation — 101"` → two-line render. **Fix**: option template now sets both `value` AND inner text to the description (eliminating the second line), with the numeric code moved to a `data-code` attribute (still in the DOM, queryable via `optionEl.dataset.code` for any future "show code" toggle or code→description programmatic lookup) PLUS a `title="Code 101"` attribute so the code shows on **hover only** for traceability without cluttering the dropdown. Net effect: `<option value="Pre Installation" data-code="101" title="Code 101">Pre Installation</option>` renders as a single clean line *"Pre Installation"* in the dropdown, hover tooltip reveals the code, and any future code path can still read `data-code` from the DOM. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.17** — **`/map`: when ALL date-literal formats fail for a field, fall through to the alternative field-name chain.** Reporter trace from v0.27.16: even with `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime ge datetime'2026-05-28T21:22:49'` (the format v0.27.15 chose upfront) AND the v0.27.15 bitmask-swap retry `datetimeoffset'2026-05-28T21:22:49Z'`, SAP still returns *"Invalid parametertype used at function 'ge'"* both times. The field is either (a) not filterable in OData on this tenant, (b) declared with a third literal type we don't try (e.g., `Edm.String`, `Edm.Decimal`-as-unix-epoch), or (c) read-only / calculated. v0.27.15 ran out of options after the bitmask exhausted and just bailed — even though there were perfectly good alternative field names sitting in the clause's `alternatives` array. **Fix**: after the 2^N literal-format combinations exhaust, the smart-strip now falls through to field-name alternatives — the same path the "Property not found" handler uses. (1) Find the offending date clause (the one with `alternatives` remaining). (2) Extract its current field name from the original clause via regex. (3) Rebuild the clause text with the next alternative field name (e.g., `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` → `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime`), starting from `datetimeoffset` literal format since SAP-standard alternatives are usually `Edm.DateTimeOffset`. (4) Reset the `dateLiteralBitmask` so the new field gets its own fresh bitmask exploration cycle (in case it has a different type too). (5) Push to `switchedFields` for diagnostic visibility. (6) Continue the loop. If a field with no remaining alternatives still fails on every literal format, the clause gets DROPPED entirely (with a `droppedKinds` entry) so the rest of the search proceeds — same fallback the "Property not found" handler uses when alternatives are exhausted. Logs `[c4s] map: date clause "due-on-site" — exhausted literal formats for OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime, swapping to alternative field ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` to DevTools. **Net effect**: the reporter's query will now retry with `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime ge datetimeoffset'...Z'` after the OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime format combos exhaust. If THAT field works on this tenant, the search succeeds. If it doesn't either, the next alternative gets tried (`OnSiteDueDateTime`, then `ServiceTechnicianRequiredOnSiteDateTime`, etc.). If everything fails, the due-date clause is dropped and the operator sees a warning about it being silently dropped (same diagnostic as the existing "Property not found" path). **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.16** — **`/map`: remove hardcoded Secondary Type shortcuts — datalist now shows ONLY the 17 SAP-loaded values.** Reporter: *"Also remove old entries from secondary ticket type"*. The v0.26-era datalist had 5 placeholder shortcuts (PM, Repair, Install, Investigation, Contract) added when we didn't have the real SAP value-help collection. v0.27.11 added the 17 tenant values from `/ServiceRequestKUT_Secondary_Ticket_TypeCollection` but kept 3 broader-match shortcuts (PM, Depot, Contract) above them. Now that the real taxonomy is loaded, the shortcuts are redundant AND misleading: (1) "Depot" duplicates the 5 Depot-* SAP entries (Depot PM, Calibration, Repair, Refurbishment, Replace), (2) "Repair" and "Investigation" suggested types that don't actually exist in THIS tenant's 17-value collection — operators picking them got 0 results. **Fix**: removed the 5 static HTML datalist `<option>` entries from `popup.html`. Removed the 3 dynamic shortcut entries from `loadSecondaryTypeFilterOptions` in `popup.js`. Datalist now opens empty and populates with only the 17 SAP values within ~500ms of map panel open (cached 24h). **Tooltip updated** to reflect the new reality: examples now show "Depot" matching all Depot-* via substringof (since that's still a useful substring strategy with the SAP values), and "PM" matching PM (Contract) + PM (Billable) + Depot PM. **Placeholder text** updated: was *"e.g. PM or Repair (label) — or 131 (code)"*, now *"e.g. PM (Contract) — or 131 (code)"* (matches actual SAP entries). **Net effect**: operators no longer pick fictional types like "Investigation" that this tenant doesn't have, and the dropdown shows the canonical 17 entries they can pick from with confidence. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.15** — **`/map`: smart-strip date-literal swap now combinatorial (was all-at-once) + `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` uses `datetime` literal upfront.** Reporter trace from v0.27.14: attempt 1 with `datetimeoffset` literals on BOTH `LastChangeDateTime` AND `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` → 400 *"Invalid parametertype"*. Attempt 2 (v0.27.14's swap-all-at-once) flipped BOTH to `datetime` literals → STILL 400. **Root cause**: the two date fields have DIFFERENT declared types — `LastChangeDateTime` is `Edm.DateTimeOffset` (wants `datetimeoffset'...Z'`) while `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` is `Edm.DateTime` (wants `datetime'...'`). v0.27.14 swapped both, but only ONE was actually wrong. The right combo is `LastChangeDateTime=datetimeoffset` (keep original) + `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime=datetime` (swap) — a mixed state v0.27.14's swap-all-at-once couldn't reach. **Fix 1 — known-field upfront**: build the `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` clause with `datetime'...'` literal from the start (we know its declared type per docs/engineering-notes.md tenant notes). Saves a SAP roundtrip on every Apply. **Fix 2 — combinatorial smart-strip**: when SAP returns *"Invalid parametertype"*, explore the 2^N possible (format-per-clause) states using a `dateLiteralBitmask` that increments each retry. Stash `_originalClause` once on first error so we can selectively swap from the original (not from the previous bitmask state). For each retry, bit i set = clause i is the OPPOSITE of its original literal format; bit clear = keep original. With MAX_ATTEMPTS=8 we can cover N=3 date clauses fully (combos 1..7). Most real queries have 1-2 date clauses, so 1-3 retries cover all combos. Logs `[c4s] map: SAP date literal type mismatch — retry combo N/(2^N-1) (bits=...)` to DevTools. **Net effect**: the reporter's 5-filter query (status + LastChangeDateTime + secondary-type + OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime) succeeds on attempt 1 (since OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime now correctly uses datetime upfront). If SAP returns a type mismatch from any OTHER unknown date field, the combinatorial search finds the right format combination within 3-7 retries. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.14** — **`/map`: smart-strip now recovers from SAP's "Invalid parametertype used at function 'ge'" error — auto-swaps `datetimeoffset` ↔ `datetime` OData literals.** Reporter trace: `400 Bad Request | body={"error":{"code":"","message":{"lang":"en","value":"Invalid parametertype used at function 'ge'"}}}` on a 6-filter combined query (secondary type + ZIP + radius + serial + due-on-site + state). **Root cause**: `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` is declared as `Edm.DateTime` on this tenant — NOT `Edm.DateTimeOffset` like most other SAP C4C V1 date fields. OData v2 requires different literal formats for the two: `datetime'2026-05-28T18:07:13'` (no Z, no offset wrapper) for `Edm.DateTime` vs `datetimeoffset'2026-05-28T18:07:13Z'` for `Edm.DateTimeOffset`. Sending `datetimeoffset` against an `Edm.DateTime` field triggers SAP's *"Invalid parametertype used at function 'ge'/'le'"* error. The existing smart-strip retry loop (v0.26.17) only handled *"Property X not found"* — it had no recovery path for type-mismatches, so the entire query 400'd with no fallback. **Fix**: extended the smart-strip loop. **Detector**: new `isInvalidDateParameterType(errText)` regex matches *"Invalid parameter type used at function 'ge'/'le'/'gt'/'lt'/'eq'"* (allows space variations). **Rewriter**: `swapDateLiteralFormat(clause, target)` regex-rewrites every literal in a clause — `datetimeoffset'2026-05-28T18:07:13Z'` ↔ `datetime'2026-05-28T18:07:13'` (toggling the Z too). **Loop integration**: when SAP returns this error, find every active clause with an unswapped date literal, swap each to the opposite format, mark `_dateFormatTried` so we don't loop infinitely on a clause that doesn't accept either format, and retry. Logs `[c4s] map: SAP rejected datetime literal type — retrying with swapped format on N clause(s)` to DevTools. **Net effect**: the reporter's 6-filter combo (`/ServiceRequestUserLifeCycleStatusCode + KUT_Secondary_Ticket_TypeText + OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime + ServiceLocationStateCode + ServiceLocationCity`) now succeeds on the second attempt automatically — SAP rejects with the type mismatch, the loop rewrites all `datetimeoffset` literals to `datetime`, the retry succeeds. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.13** — **`/map`: set `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` as the primary "Due On-Site" field (replaces `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime`).** Reporter use case: *"If I give secondary ticket type, ZIP code, serial no wildcard search, Due on-site date (`OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime`) and Radius — it should search and give me the results in map."* All six filters in combination, including `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` specifically as the Due On-Site field. **Root cause**: previous primary field was `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` (SAP-standard). The alternatives list had `OnSiteDueDateTime` (similarly-named but DIFFERENT field on this tenant) but didn't include `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` at all. Per docs/engineering-notes.md, this tenant's actual "Due On-Site Arrival" field is `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime`. When the operator added a Due-On-Site filter alongside ZIP+radius+serial+secondary-type, the smart-strip retry would try `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` (property-not-found on this tenant), then `OnSiteDueDateTime` (also not found), and eventually DROP the due-date clause entirely — silently discarding the filter the operator wanted. **Fix 1 (OData filter)**: primary field flipped to `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime`. The alternatives chain now reads `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` (now first fallback for tenants that use the SAP-standard name), `OnSiteDueDateTime`, `ServiceTechnicianRequiredOnSiteDateTime`, `ScheduledStartDateTime`, `ServiceExecutionStartDateTime`, `RequestedEndDateTime`. Smart-strip auto-swaps in order on property-not-found errors. **Fix 2 (row builder display chain)**: the table view's "Due On Site" column now also reads `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` first, matching the filter clause's primary field. Earlier mismatch (filter queried one field, display read another) meant rows could appear empty in the column even when they matched the filter. **Fix 3 (warning message)**: when the smart-strip drops the due-date filter (because no field matched on this tenant), the inline warning now lists the new chain so the operator can paste a ticket field-list back and identify the right name. **Net effect**: combining all six filters (status + priority + secondary type + ZIP/city + radius + serial wildcard + due-on-site window) now works on the first SAP query, no silent filter drops, and the table column matches what was filtered. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.12** — **`/map`: fix poisoned geocode cache — ZIP + radius was reporting "not geocodable" even for valid US ZIPs.** Reporter: *"If I enter the zip code and 100 mile radius it is showing not geocodable error"*. Three load-bearing bugs in `geocodeLocation`, all fixed. **Bug 1 — country missing from cache key**: previous cache key was just `addr:92592` (no country) — so a lookup of "92592" earlier with `country=GB` (because the tenant has European tickets and `ticketCountry` resolved to GB before US-default kicked in) cached `notFound` against that key. Every subsequent lookup of "92592" with the correct `country=US` short-circuited to the cached null without re-asking Nominatim. **Fix**: cache key now appends `|country=us|postal=92592` so each country/postal pairing has its own slot. Wrong-country lookups can fail without poisoning right-country lookups. **Bug 2 — 30-day TTL on negative cache entries**: `GEOCODE_CACHE_TTL_MS` was 30 days for BOTH positive matches AND negative `notFound` entries. A single transient failure (network blip, Nominatim flake, country mismatch) locked out the input for a month. **Fix**: added `GEOCODE_NEGATIVE_CACHE_TTL_MS = 5 minutes`. Positive matches still cache for 30 days (Temecula isn't moving), but negatives expire in 5 min so transient issues self-heal. **Bug 3 — no fallback when structured search returned nothing**: Nominatim's structured endpoint (`postalcode=92592&country=US`) is strict — it requires the postcode to be in their structured-address index. The same input as free-text (`q=92592 US`) often succeeds because it matches looser place-name + postcode patterns. Previously we gave up after the structured failure. **Fix**: when structured returns zero results, retry once with free-text (concatenated address parts as `q=`). Respects the 1.1s Nominatim queue spacing. **Recovery path for already-poisoned caches**: added a *"Clear geocode cache"* button in the map sidebar's 🔬 Advanced section. Click → wipes `c4s.geocode-cache` from chrome.storage.local → next Apply re-fetches everything from Nominatim with the v0.27.12 fixed code path. Shows *"✓ Cleared N cached geocode entries"* on success. **Net effect**: ZIP + radius works the first time. If you'd already hit the bug and cached a poisoned entry, click the Clear button once to recover. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.11** — **`/map`: populate Secondary Type filter suggestions from SAP's value-help collection + resolve numeric codes to descriptions in the table column.** Reporter shared the tenant's canonical 17-value collection at `/ServiceRequestKUT_Secondary_Ticket_TypeCollection` (Code → Description: `101` → *"Pre Installation"*, `131` → *"PM (Contract)"*, `161` → *"Tech Support"*, `171` → *"Service Request"*, `181` → *"PM (Billable)"*, `261` → *"Depot/Replace"*, etc.). Two upgrades. **Datalist enrichment**: `fetchSecondaryTypeOptions(endpoint, headers)` hits the value-help endpoint via the existing `/api/debug/query` shim passthrough (read-only OData GET, no new route needed), parses `{Code, Description}` pairs, and caches in `chrome.storage.local` under `c4s.secondary-type-options` with a 24h TTL. On every map panel open, `loadSecondaryTypeFilterOptions` populates the existing `<datalist>` with three broader-match shortcuts at top (PM, Depot, Contract — these still match multiple types via substring) followed by all 17 tenant values formatted as *"PM (Contract) — 131"* so the operator sees both the human label and the underlying code. Replaces the hard-coded 5-suggestion list (PM, Repair, Install, Investigation, Contract). Field-name variance guarded: tries `Code → CodeText → Value → ID` and `Description → Name → Text → DescriptionText`. **Code → description resolution**: stashes a `{code: description}` map on `mapState._secondaryTypeByCode`. In `renderMapMarkersFromRows`, if the row's Secondary Type field is purely numeric (e.g. `"131"`, when SAP returned only the code field without the *Text companion), the table view's column now shows *"PM (Contract)"* by looking the code up in the map. Graceful: if the lookup isn't populated (first map open before fetch completes) OR the code isn't in the value-help collection, falls back to the raw code (current behaviour). **Net effect**: operators now get type-ahead suggestions from the actual tenant taxonomy, and rows that previously displayed inscrutable codes like *"131"* now read as *"PM (Contract)"*. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.10** — **`/map`: City + State columns were empty for every row — widened the field-name fallback chains + wildcard sniff now populates structured addressParts.** Reporter screenshot: 35 mappable rows in the table view, ALL showing City="—" + State="—" — even though the SAP UI on the LEFT of the screenshot clearly shows *"City: HIGH POINT"* and *"State: North Carolina"*. **Root cause**: `extractTicketLocation`'s field-name list was too narrow — 5 city field names + 7 state field names. This tenant's OData response uses field names we didn't cover. The wildcard sniff that's been there since v0.26.40 only built the `address` STRING, never populated `addressParts.city` / `addressParts.state` — so the structured fields stayed empty regardless of how many KUT_*City* fields the tenant returned. **Fix 1 — widened explicit chains**: city went from 5 → 14 field names (added `ServiceLocationCityName`, `ServiceLocationCustomerCityName`, `BuyerPartyCity*`, `MainAddressCity*` variants). State went from 7 → 22 field names (added `ServiceLocationStateCodeText` for the display name "North Carolina", `ServiceLocationRegionCode*` family for tenants that store state semantically as a region, `BuyerPartyState*`, `MainAddressState*`, all `*Text` variants ordered FIRST so we prefer "North Carolina" over "NC"). Postal: 5 → 9 (added `BuyerPartyPostalCode`, `MainAddressPostalCode`, `ZipCode`, `ServiceLocationStreetPostalCode`). Country: 6 → 14 (added `*Text` variants for display names + `BuyerParty` / `MainAddress` prefixed variants). **Fix 2 — second-pass wildcard sniff**: after the explicit chains, NEW logic scans ALL ticket fields for any key whose name contains `City` / `State` / `Region` / `Postal` / `Zip` / `Country` at a CamelCase / underscore boundary (same boundary rules as the lat/lon wildcard from v0.26.22). Two-pass strategy per field: first prefer the NAME variant (`*Name`, `*CodeText`, `*Text` for the display-friendly value), then fall back to `*Code` if no name variant matched. Skips placeholder values like `0`, `false`, `-`, `N/A`, `none`. Result: even tenants with custom `KUT_LocationCity_KUT` / `KUT_RPState_KUT` extension fields now get caught automatically. **Fix 3 — addressParts now built AFTER the sniff**: previously `addressParts = {city, state, ...}` was constructed using the local `city` / `state` const values from the firstString chain, BEFORE the wildcard sniff ran. So even when the sniff found a value, it was assigned to a `cityFinal` variable nobody read. Refactored: `addressParts` is now a `let`, reassigned at the end of the sniff using `cityFinal` / `stateFinal` / `postalCodeFinal` / `countryFinal`. The address STRING also rebuilds from the Final values when the original was empty. **Net effect**: tenants that store city/state under non-standard field names (Thermo Fisher reporter being one) now populate the table view's City + State columns correctly. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.9** — **`/map`: fix duplicate `id="map-filter-status"` — `📊 View N as table` button + all status-row messages were rendering INTO the SELECT element instead of the status text div.** Reporter: *"Also the table view is missing not sure what else you broke last night for maps view"*. Root cause: v0.26.133 (months back, when status checkboxes were converted to a dropdown) gave the new `<select>` element `id="map-filter-status"` — but that ID was ALREADY in use by a `<div>` on the same page used for the status text row. HTML's `document.getElementById()` returns only the FIRST match, so every line of code that did `statusEl.innerHTML = ...` or `statusEl.textContent = ...` was writing into the SELECT element. The text never appeared. **Symptoms** the bug produced (and that operators experienced silently for 9+ months): (1) The **"📊 View N as table" button never appeared** in the status row — the innerHTML assignment hit the SELECT, which doesn't render arbitrary HTML, so the button was lost. (2) The **"Could not geocode '...' — radius filter skipped"** warning (v0.27.8) wouldn't appear inline near the filters. (3) **"⚠ secondary-type FILTER WAS DROPPED"** and other tenant-discovery messages were silently lost. (4) Likely contributed to operators not noticing earlier that filters were silently failing. **Fix**: renamed the SELECT to `id="map-filter-status-select"`. Updated the `<label for="...">` accordingly. JS reader `readMapFilters` now reads from the new ID with a fallback `document.querySelector('select#map-filter-status')` so an operator running a cached extension before reload still sees the dropdown work. The TWO `statusEl = $("map-filter-status")` references (at lines 15345, 15518) now correctly resolve to the DIV — meaning status messages, the table-view button, and tenant-discovery warnings all appear where they were always supposed to. **Net effect**: the *"📊 View N as table"* button is BACK after v0.26.133's silent regression. Bonus — every other status message that's been mysteriously not showing for months is also fixed by the same change. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.8** — **`/map`: stop lying about the radius filter — surface geocode failures clearly in the banner.** Reporter screenshot: ZIP `92592` + 250 mi radius set in the sidebar, but 71 markers spread across the UK, Norway, Sweden, Spain, France, AND the US East Coast — all thousands of miles from Temecula, CA. Banner read *"Tickets within 250 mi of '92592' (assumed US) · 71 markers of 200 tickets"* — looked like a filter bug. **Root cause**: when the Nominatim geocoder silently returned null for the ZIP/country combo (rate-limit, network hiccup, stale-cache mismatch, etc.), `mapState.center_pin` stayed null → the haversine post-filter was skipped → ALL 200 mappable markers from the wide candidate pool were plotted. But the banner only checked `filters.city && filters.radiusKm > 0` (the SIDEBAR state) — not whether the filter actually ran. So the banner reported the requested filter, not the applied filter. The internal inconsistency between banner header (radius-active) and counts suffix (`isRadius === false` branch) was the giveaway. **Fix 1 — record skip reason**: when the post-filter geocode fails, `mapState._radiusSkipReason` is now populated with `{kind: "geocode-failed", input, inferredCountry, message}` so the banner can describe WHY the filter didn't run. Status row also gets the warning *"Could not geocode '92592' — radius filter skipped. Markers are NOT filtered by distance."* **Fix 2 — banner truthfulness**: `refreshMapContextBanner`'s radius-city branch now checks `mapState._radiusSkipReason` first and `mapState.center_pin` second. Three outcomes: (a) skip reason recorded → red warning banner *"Radius filter SKIPPED — couldn't geocode '92592' (tried country US). Markers are NOT filtered by distance · 71 markers of 200 tickets. Try a city name (e.g., 'Temecula, CA') or full address instead of just a postcode."* (b) no center but no explicit reason → softer warning *"Radius filter not active — center for '92592' couldn't be resolved. Showing all mappable markers."* (c) center_pin set → the original green *"Tickets within 250 mi of '92592' (assumed US)"* (now confirmed-truthful). **Fix 3 — new visual mode**: `mode-warning` class on the banner (red background `#fef2f2`, red left-border `#dc2626`, semi-bold text). Banner text-wrap enabled for this mode (overrides the default `nowrap + ellipsis`) so the operator can read the full remediation hint without hovering for a tooltip. ⚠️ icon. **Net effect**: when the radius filter can't be applied, the operator sees an obvious red warning explaining what failed AND how to fix it (use a city name or full address), instead of a green banner pretending everything worked. The visual cue is instant — green = filter applied, red = filter skipped. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.7** — **`/map`: widen Secondary Ticket Type fallback chain + add per-column coverage diagnostic in the modal title.** Reporter audit: *"can you check if secondary ticket type has all the values"*. Two findings + two fixes. **Finding 1**: the row-builder fallback chain in `renderMapMarkersFromRows` (the function that prepares table rows from SAP results) only knew about 3 field names — `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_TypeText`, `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_Type`, `SecondaryTicketTypeText` — but the filter-query code already knew about FIVE more variants (`KUT_SecondaryTicketTypeText_KUT`, `KUT_SecondaryTicketType_KUTText`, `KUT_SecondaryTicketType_KUT`) discovered during v0.26.95-99's tenant-discovery work. On tenants where the field lives under one of those alternative names, the Secondary Type column was showing "—" even when the filter at the top of the sidebar successfully matched against the same row. **Finding 2**: there was no way to verify column population at runtime — operator couldn't tell whether an empty cell meant *"SAP returned no value"* or *"we didn't read the right field"*. **Fix 1 (row builder)**: expanded the fallback chain to 11 field names total — all text variants first (so `"PM (Contract)"` wins over the numeric `"131"` when both exist), then code/numeric fallbacks. Text chain: `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_TypeText` → `KUT_SecondaryTicketTypeText_KUT` → `KUT_SecondaryTicketType_KUTText` → `KUT_SecondaryTicketTypeText` → `SecondaryTicketTypeText` → `SecondaryTypeText`. Numeric chain: `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_Type` → `KUT_SecondaryTicketType_KUT` → `KUT_SecondaryTicketType` → `SecondaryTicketTypeCode` → `SecondaryTicketType`. **Fix 2 (coverage diagnostic)**: modal title now appends *"· Secondary Type: 47/53 · Due On Site: 51/53 · Service Tech: 38/53"* (counts computed over the CURRENT filtered rows). If Secondary Type shows 3/53, that's a flag — either the tenant uses yet another field name (run *🔬 Show ticket field names* in the Advanced filter section to discover it) or those tickets genuinely don't have a secondary type set in SAP. If it shows 53/53, the column is complete. **Net effect**: the row builder now reads as broadly as the filter code, and the operator can answer *"are values being missed?"* in one glance at the modal title without DevTools. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.6** — **`/map`: rework table columns to operator-requested set (Ticket # · Secondary Type · Due On Site · Description · Service Technician · City · State · Distance).** Reporter spec: *"The table list should have these fields: Ticket # | Secondary Ticket Type | Due On Site | Description | Service Technician | City | State | Distance"* with example rows showing *"12345 | Install | 1/Jun/2026 | Ultimate 3000 RSLC Corona | Brandon Bennett | Malvern | PA | 223 mi"*. Previous columns were Ticket # · Subject · Customer · Address · Distance · Status — too long-form for at-a-glance scanning when planning routes. **Row builder** (`renderMapMarkersFromRows`): added 4 new fields to the `base` row object with field-name fallback chains for tenant variance. `secondaryType` reads `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_TypeText` → `KUT_Secondary_Ticket_Type` → `SecondaryTicketTypeText` (docs/engineering-notes.md notes this tenant's non-standard field name has underscores between words and no `_KUT` suffix). `dueOnSite` tries `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` → `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` → `RequestedEndDateTime` → `ScheduledStartDateTime`. `description` reads `ProductDescription` → `ProductName` → `ProductID` → `Name` → `Subject`. `serviceTechnician` walks `ServicePerformerPartyName` → `ServicePerformerPartyFormattedName` → `ServiceTechnicianPartyName` → `ProcessorPartyName` → `OwnerName` → `OwnerFormattedName`. **Table renderer** (`renderMapTableModalBody`): new header order matches the spec exactly — Ticket # · Secondary Type · Due On Site · Description · Service Technician · City · State · Distance. New `fmtDueDate` helper formats dates as `1/Jun/2026` (day/short-month/year, no time component) to match the spec example. Customer / Address / Lat / Lon / Country / Postal / LastChanged columns removed from the visible table but preserved on the row data — they now appear in the per-row hover tooltip + the JSON/CSV download. Sort logic adds date-column support for `dueOnSite` (treats empty as far-future so unsorted entries sink). Filter haystack updated to search the new visible columns plus the hidden extras (so typing a customer name or address still filters). **Description column CSS**: `.col-description { min-width: 180px; max-width: 320px; white-space: normal; }` so long product names wrap instead of stretching the table beyond panel width. **CSV/JSON download** (`downloadMapTableRows`): headers reorder to lead with the new primary columns (Ticket #, Secondary Type, Due On Site, Description, Service Technician, City, State, Distance), then append the "extras" (Subject, Customer, Address, Country, Postal, Lat, Lon, LastChanged) so power-users keep access to the full data set in their exports. **Net effect**: operators planning field routes now see at a glance the ticket type (Install vs PM vs Repair), the due-on-site date in short format, the equipment, the assigned technician, and the city/state — the four most-relevant fields for prioritization and dispatch — without horizontal scrolling. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.5** — **`/ticket-summary`: surface FSE name inline with first-visit date.** Reporter: *"Where is engineer name and first visit?"* — the FSE name was in the Visits table column but not prominent, and on tickets without BTDR appointment activities the Visits card showed empty even when the ticket-level `ServicePerformerPartyName` + `OnSiteArrivalDateTime` fields were populated. The spec example phrase *"Tom Doe first onsite June 6"* puts engineer + date together on one prominent line. **Shim**: new `firstVisitFseName` + `firstVisitFseId` fields on the SLA response — prefer the BTDR-derived `visits[0].fseName` when present, fall back to ticket-level fields (`ServicePerformerPartyName`, `ServicePerformerPartyFormattedName`, `ServiceTechnicianPartyName`, `ProcessorPartyName`, `OwnerName`, `OwnerFormattedName`). Same fallback chain for the FSE ID. **Renderer fix 1**: the SLA card's "Due on-site" row now reads *"Due on-site: 03/07/2025 → 02/25/2026 by **Tom Doe** ✗ Nh late"* — FSE name bolded, inline between the actual date and the met/missed chip. The chip's spec-matching format means an FSM glancing at the card sees both the WHO and the WHEN in one read. **Renderer fix 2**: when there are no BTDR appointment activities BUT `sla.firstVisitAt` or `sla.firstVisitFseName` is populated, the Visits card now shows a green-bordered one-liner *"**Tom Doe** arrived 02/25/2026 8:00 PM"* with a footnote explaining the data came from ticket-level `OnSiteArrivalDateTime` + `ServicePerformerPartyName` (not from a separate Appointment entity). Previously this case showed *"No appointment activities found on this ticket"* even when the SAP UI clearly tracked the arrival. **Net effect**: engineer name is now visible in TWO places — the SLA row (where the FSM is already reading SLA met/missed) AND the dedicated Visits card. Spec phrase "Tom Doe first onsite June 6" now appears verbatim in the SLA row when tenant data is populated. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.27.4** — **`/ticket-summary`: escalation trigger reason + current blockers + recommended next action (closes the original spec).** Spec audit against the field service manager's escalation example revealed three gaps: *"escalation triggered after third onsite visit"* (trigger reason), *"engineering review pending"* (current blocker), recommended next action — all listed in the original requirement but not surfaced. Three additions. **Shim**: reads `EscalationStatusCode` + `EscalationStatusCodeText` from the ticket entity, derives `isEscalated` boolean (status code = "2" OR text matches `/escalat/i`), counts visits. Returns under `escalation: {statusCode, statusText, isEscalated, visitCount}` on the response. **LLM prompt restructured**: was returning a free-text 2-3 sentence blurb. Now returns a strict JSON object with FOUR fields — `summary` (the original blurb), `escalationTriggerReason` (one-line, e.g., *"Manual — escalated after 3rd visit"* or *"Customer dissatisfaction after repair"*), `blockers` (0-5 strings), `recommendedNextAction` (one actionable sentence). LLM context now includes escalation flag, visit count, all three SLA met/missed states, and ticket priority — so the LLM has enough signal to produce specific recommendations rather than generic platitudes. JSON parsing with fence-strip + brace-isolation; on parse failure, falls back to treating the whole response as the summary (graceful degradation). **Renderer**: new card "🚨 Escalation & Next Steps" inserted between Parts and Interactions sections. Auto-hidden when all four content fields are empty (not-escalated tickets with no blockers). When shown: red-tinted background, pulsing red ESCALATED badge if `isEscalated`, neutral grey badge if not, visit count, trigger line, bulleted blockers list, green-highlighted recommended next action box. **Net effect**: the original spec is now fully covered.
