A field service manager opens an escalated ticket, clicks 📋 Ticket Summary, and sees within seconds: opening date + age, SLA status (initial / on-site / resolution), visit history with FSE, labour + travel hours, parts consumed, escalation flag + trigger reason, current blockers, recommended next action, and the customer-interaction summary. All on one scroll. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.27.3** — **`/ticket-summary`: respect explicit ticket ID in the prompt (was silently ignored).** Reporter screenshot: textarea contained `/ticket-summary 3238825` but the page-detected ticket was 4386459 (different ticket). The progress strip showed *"for ticket 4386459"* — the handler was silently using the page-detected ticket and ignoring the typed ID, returning a summary for the wrong ticket without any warning. Root cause: handler signature was `({ ticketHint, endpoint, headers })` — it never read `rest` (the slash-command's argument portion). `resolveTicketId(ticketHint)` falls back to the page-detected ticket when ticketHint is set, which it always is on a ticket page. **Fix**: handler now destructures `rest` too. Parses the first whitespace-separated token as an explicit ID (`/ticket-summary 3238825` → `explicitId = "3238825"`), passes `explicitId || ticketHint` to `resolveTicketId`. Explicit ID wins; falls back to page-detected when no arg typed. Same `.split(/\s+/)[0]` parse handles trailing whitespace and accidental extra text safely. **Net effect**: typing an explicit ticket ID after the slash command now actually runs against that ticket, not the page's. Important when an operator is investigating multiple tickets in parallel or pasting an ID from chat/email/another tab. Same fix pattern applies to `/triage`, `/interactions`, etc. but those weren't reported — only `/ticket-summary` patched in this version. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.27.2** — **`/ticket-summary`: wire actual tenant SLA field names + add Resolution comparison + show SLA contract name.** Reporter shared the real tenant field shape: SAP UI's *"Initial Review Due"* maps to `FirstReactionDuedatetimeContent` (NOT `RequestFirstReactionByDateTime` that v0.27.0 used), *"Initial Review Completed"* maps to `RequestInProcessdatetimeContent`, *"Due On-Site"* is `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` (NOT `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime`), *"Arrived On-Site Date"* is `OnSiteArrivalDateTime` (authoritative — was being computed from BTDR first-appointment time, less accurate), plus two entirely new comparisons we weren't tracking: *"Resolution Due"* (`ResolutionDueDateTime`) vs *"Resolved On"* (`ResolvedOnDateTime`), and the SLA contract label `ServiceLevelObjectiveName` (e.g., *"SLA_DDIR-2HR_ONSITE-3D (SLA)"*). **Fix** in the shim: new `pickFirstDate(obj, ...keys)` helper walks a fallback chain of field names for each SLA comparison so we work across tenants. Three comparisons now tracked: (1) **Initial Review** — `FirstReactionDuedatetimeContent` → `RequestInProcessdatetimeContent` (with fallbacks to the older variants); (2) **On-Site** — `OnSiteArrivalDueDateTime` → `OnSiteArrivalDateTime` (the SAP-tracked actual, with `ScheduledStartDateTime` / `RequestedEndDateTime` fallbacks for tenants missing the dedicated field); (3) **Resolution** (NEW) — `ResolutionDueDateTime` → `ResolvedOnDateTime`. Each comparison reports `met` boolean + `hoursLate` (0 when met). Resolution has a 4th state: `resolutionOverdueDaysOpen` — when the ticket is still open AND the due date already passed, the chip shows *"⏳ open · Nd overdue"*. **Renderer** updates: SLA card now shows a contract-name subtitle pill (e.g., 📋 *SLA_DDIR-2HR_ONSITE-3D (SLA)*) when set, plus a third row for Resolution. Chip states per row: green ✓ met / red ✗ missed by Nh / red ⏳ open · Nd overdue / grey unknown. Label changed from "Initial response" → "Initial review" to match the SAP UI vocabulary. **Net effect**: SLA card now reflects the actual SAP-tracked dates the operator sees in the Timeline tab, with all three comparisons (initial review / on-site / resolution) side-by-side. The SLA contract name surfaces immediately so the operator knows which SLA terms apply. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.27.1** — **`/ticket-summary`: widen labour/travel/parts detection + add diagnostic when 0h detected despite line items existing.** Reporter screenshot: Debrief tab clearly showed 12 time entries with ACTUAL_LABOR + ACTUAL_TRAVEL rows, but the Effort card showed 0h for both. v0.27.0 only checked the `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode` field for exact-match `ACTLAB` / `ACTRVL` / `ZRP1` / `SRP1` strings. Two issues: (1) that field may be empty on this tenant — the SAP-internal code shows up in `Description` (e.g., *"ACTRVL - ACTUAL..."*) and `ProductExternalID` (e.g., *"ACTUAL_TRAVEL"*) instead, (2) the codes have synonyms — `ACTUAL_LABOR` / `ACTUAL_LABOUR` / `ACTUAL_TRAVEL` appear on some tenants. **Fix**: new `detectKind(li)` helper concatenates 8 fields (`UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode`, `ProcessingTypeCode`, `ItemListProcessingTypeCode`, `ServiceTransactionItemProcessingTypeCode`, `ProductExternalID`, `ProductID`, `Description`, `ProductDescription`) into one haystack and regex-matches: `\bACTLAB\b|ACTUAL_LABOR|ACTUAL_LABOUR` → labour, `\bACTRVL\b|ACTUAL_TRAVEL|ACTRAV` → travel, `\bZRP1\b|\bSRP1\b` → parts. Word-boundary anchors prevent matching e.g., "ACTLAB" inside "INACTIVELAB". New `detectQty(li)` tries `ActualQuantity` → `PlannedQuantity` → `Quantity` → `BillableQuantity` → `Duration` in order, returns the first non-zero. **Diagnostic block** in the Effort card: collapsible details element showing kind counts (Labour: N · Travel: N · Parts: N · Other: N) + one sample row per detected kind with the actual field values that matched + a 🪵 collapsible JSON dump of the first raw line item. Auto-opens (amber) when total hours = 0 but line items > 0 — so the operator sees immediately why detection failed. Collapsed (grey) otherwise. **Net effect**: on tenants where the processing-type code lives in Description / ProductExternalID, detection now picks it up. When it still fails, the diagnostic shows exactly which fields contain what — informs the next iteration without DevTools. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.27.0** — **New: `/ticket-summary` — one-page operational briefing for escalations.** Reporter's use case: a field service manager opens an escalated ticket and needs to understand the situation in under a minute (instead of digging through notes, emails, labor lines, parts history across multiple facets). Built as a new slash command that runs alongside `/interactions` — **/interactions is NOT modified**, the locked-down v0.26.127 code stays exactly as is. **New shim endpoint** `/api/get_ticket_summary` consolidates four parallel SAP queries into one HTTP response: (1) ServiceRequest entity for SLA + dates + customer + status fields, (2) ServiceRequestItemCollection filtered by `ParentObjectID` for line items (sums `ActualQuantity` where `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode` = `ACTLAB` → labour hours, `ACTRVL` → travel hours; lists rows where the code is `ZRP1` or `SRP1` as parts consumed), (3) `ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference` for interaction counts (notes / emails / calls / appointments) — and for appointments (TypeCode `13`), resolves each via UUID-strip to fetch the AppointmentCollection entity → extracts `StartDateTime` + `OwnerPartyID` / `OwnerName` (the FSE). (4) ServiceRequestTextCollection for note count. Computes SLA adherence client-side: `RequestFirstReactionByDateTime` vs `RequestInitialReceiptDateTime` (initial response met?), `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime` vs first appointment's `StartDateTime` (on-site SLA met?). Reports hours late when missed. **New slash command** `/ticket-summary` (chip: 📋 Ticket Summary, triage category — green border): handler fetches the summary endpoint in parallel with `/api/tools/get_ticket_notes` + `/api/get_ticket_activities` (same data sources `/interactions` uses, called independently here), composes a transcript of notes + emails, runs ONE focused LLM call asking for a 2-3 sentence factual summary (not the full `/interactions` structured output — separate prompt, separate format). Returns `kind: "ticket-summary-panel"`. **New `renderTicketSummaryPanel`** function — independent CSS namespace (`tsum-*`) so `/interactions` styles are unaffected. Sections: header chip (ID + subject + age + status + priority + customer/product/serial), SLA card (initial response due → actual + chip; due on-site → first visit + chip), Visits table (date + FSE name + subject), Effort card (3 tiles — labour hours / travel hours / total — plus line-item count), Parts consumed table (material ID + name + qty + processing-type chip), Interactions card (count chips + LLM blurb + hint pointing to `/interactions` for the full timeline), and an "↗ Open ticket in SAP C4C" button at the bottom (same `openTicketInC4C` helper as the map popup). Added to `isSlowSlash` regex so the progress strip activates. **Net effect**: one-click answer to "what's happening on this escalated ticket" — SLA status, visit history with FSE names, total effort spent, parts replaced, and a short narrative summary. `/interactions` remains the deep timeline view; `/ticket-summary` is the operational facts view. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.135** — **Merge: default ticket prefill is now `/triage <id>` (was `Summarize ticket <id>`).** Reporter pointed at the auto-fill in the textarea and asked what it was. Audit confirmed: the prefill was a plain-English LLM call routed through `/api/chat` with only the scraped SAP page DOM as context — generic output, didn't read notes/emails/serial-history. `/triage` does the same one-click experience with a structured prompt (Priority / Category / Next action / SLA risk) that uses the same scraped context PLUS the auto-detected ticket entity. **Fix**: when a ticket is detected on the active SAP page, the textarea now prefills with `/triage <id>` instead of `Summarize ticket <id>`. The "Detected ticket on the active SAP page: ..." selection hint above the textarea is unchanged. Operator clicks Ask → `/triage` slash command fires, gets the structured output. Non-ticket screens (contract, opportunity, lead, account, activity) keep their existing `Summarize <noun> <id>` prefill because `/triage` is ticket-only. **Backwards-compat**: the auto-triage path's "user-is-typing" detection (which prevents auto-triage from overwriting an operator's manual text) now recognizes BOTH the new `/triage` prefill AND the legacy `Summarize ticket` prefill as placeholders. So an operator running a cached/stale extension still gets auto-triaged correctly when the Options setting is on. The auto-pulse and auto-KB-resolve placeholder checks at lines 13091 and 13167 already handled both forms (added in earlier versions). **Net effect**: one less semi-broken default prompt in the panel. The chip row + the right-on-open prefill now both lead to a useful action instead of a generic LLM summary. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.134** — **Critical fix: restore broken `/parts-check` handler (was a stub returning undefined fields).** Reporter asked to verify parts-check was working without breaking /order-parts. Audit found a regression — the `/parts-check` slash handler did the ticket lookup, then immediately returned a panel object referencing `summary`, `productHint`, `subject`, and `stockByPart` as local variables — none of which were ever declared or assigned. The `runPartsInference` call (the helper that actually computes the recommendations + cross-checks warehouse stock) was missing from the body. Operators clicking the 🛠️ Parts check chip got an empty panel with `items: undefined`. The renderer hit the `items.length === 0` short-circuit and showed *"No parts could be inferred from past resolutions"* — which made it LOOK like the inference ran and returned nothing, hiding the bug. **Fix**: restored the call. `runPartsInference(t, endpoint, headers, { pageContext })` runs after the ticket lookup (same way `/order-parts` calls it on line 4169 — shared helper, signature unchanged). When inference returns `kind: "ok"`, the handler now properly unpacks `parts`, `summary`, `productHint`, `subject` and forwards them to the panel. When inference returns `kind: "info"` (no product on ticket) or `kind: "error"` (LLM failure), the handler forwards those returns as-is so the operator sees the helpful explanation message instead of a blank panel. Also wired through the diagnostic phase counts (`similarTicketCount`, `similarTicketsDetail`, `phaseCounts`, `includeProcTypes`, `lineItemsScanned`, `excludedByProcType`) so the *"Why these suggestions?"* drill-in block has its data — same payload that `/order-parts` already forwards. **/order-parts is untouched**: same `runPartsInference` helper, no signature change, no side effects. Both slash commands now share the inference pipeline cleanly. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.133** — **`/map`: Status filter checkboxes → dropdown + clicking a ticket in the map popup opens it directly in SAP C4S (matches table-view behavior).** Reporter: *"Status can be drop down, when you click on the ticket from map it should open the ticket in C4S like the same logic we built for table view."* Two UX cleanups. **(1) Status checkboxes → single dropdown.** The two stacked checkboxes (Open / Closed) consumed two rows and forced operators to think about combinatorics. Replaced with a single `<select id="map-filter-status">` with three options: *Open* (default), *Closed*, *Open + Closed*. `readMapFilters` maps the dropdown value back to the same `["open"]` / `["closed"]` / `["open","closed"]` array shape downstream code already expects — no other call sites needed to change. Falls back to legacy checkbox lookup if the new select isn't present (cached extension safety). **(2) Marker popup ticket click opens in C4S.** Previously: clicking a ticket row inside the map popup filled `/triage <id>` in the side-panel textarea and closed the map. The table-view link column (v0.26.30) has done the right thing for months — find an open SAP C4C tab, build the V1 `#Nav/0/<base64>` deep-link payload, navigate that tab to the ticket. Same `openTicketInC4C(ticket, options)` helper is now wired into the map popup's `.map-popup-ticket` rows. Ctrl/Cmd+click → opens in a new tab; plain click → reuses the existing SAP tab (browser convention). Failure paths: when no SAP C4C tab is open, a yellow inline `.map-popup-open-flash` div appears at the bottom of the popup with *"Couldn't open ticket NNN — no SAP C4C tab is currently open. Open SAP first, then click again."* — auto-hides after 6 seconds. V1-fallback path (tenant deep-link payload not yet known) flashes a blue notice that the ticket ID was copied to clipboard so the operator can paste into the universal search. The popup stays open on success so multiple "open in C4S" clicks on the same marker can chain quickly. **Net effect**: map view is now a one-click drill into SAP — same as the table view — instead of a side-panel detour through `/triage`. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.132** — **Dashboard: AI impact stats (replaces SAP ticket counts) + × close button.** Reporter: *"Instead of open tickets — show the stats how AI helped? Also how to close this dashboard?"*. The v0.26.131 stats showed SAP open-ticket counts, but those numbers don't say anything about what Ask C4S did FOR the operator — and the dashboard couldn't be dismissed when they wanted to focus on the textarea. **Stats rebuilt around AI impact**: three tiles now show **Today** (questions asked in the last 24h), **This week** (last 7 days), and **Time saved** (total runs × 4 minutes per question, formatted as `12m` / `1h 30m` / `3h` / `2d 4h`). All three computed from a new local-only log under `askc4s.ai-impact-runs` — `[{at: ms, command: "/triage"}, ...]` capped at 1000 entries. Tracker is pushed from `ask()` right after `pushHistory()`. Stat-meta footer now surfaces *"N total · most used in last 30 days: /interactions (12×) · ~4 min/question saved estimate"* so the operator can see their top workflow at a glance. Empty state: *"Ask a question or run a slash command to start tracking."* **Close button**: small × in the dashboard header (top-right). Click → sets `c4s.dashboard-dismissed=1` in `sessionStorage` and hides the dashboard. Survives focus / refocus changes for the rest of the panel session; resets when the panel reopens fresh (new tab, extension reload, side-panel close+reopen). Hover red-tinted for affordance. **Stat tiles no longer clickable** — they were drilling into `/map` in v0.26.131, but now they're info-only (a count of past runs has no useful drill destination). Removed `cursor: pointer` and hover-lift; kept the card shape. **Net effect**: dashboard now reflects the operator's own value-add ("you've saved ~3h this week using Ask C4S") instead of generic SAP queue stats, and they can dismiss it when they want a clean panel. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.131** — **Dashboard replaces the static "Ready when you are" empty state — live stats, quick actions, recent-as-cards.** Reporter: *"Can we show nice dashboard instead of this?"* — the previous empty state was a static card with three buttons (Search commands, Search KB, Explain this page) + a flat list of recent prompts. Replaced with a real at-a-glance dashboard. **Sections**: (1) **Greeting header** — time-of-day greeting (🌙 Good night / ☀️ Good morning / 🌤 Good afternoon / 🌆 Good evening) gradient-tinted, plus today's date in a muted right-aligned label. (2) **Three live stat tiles** — *Open tickets*, *Urgent* (priority 1 or 2, red text), *Due 7 days* (yellow text). Fetched async on dashboard open by calling `POST /api/tools/search_tickets` with `{status: "open", top: 200}` then computing counts client-side from the result rows (urgent = priority "1" or "2", due 7d = `ServiceExecutionDueDateTime`/`RequestedEndDateTime` within 7 days from now). 5-minute cache in `chrome.storage.local` so reopening the panel is instant. Loading state shows a shimmer skeleton on each tile. Click any tile → opens `/map` (drill-in). (3) **Stat-meta footer** — sub-line under the tiles showing data freshness ("Cached 2m ago · click a tile to drill in") OR scope info ("Across 47 open tickets") OR error ("Stats unavailable: HTTP 503"). (4) **Quick actions grid** — 4 tiles in 2×2: 🗺 Map view (fires `/map` immediately), 📚 Search KB (puts `/kb ` in textarea), ⌘ All commands (opens palette), ❓ Explain page. (5) **Recent prompts as cards** — top 5 history items rendered as compact monospace cards with hover highlight, instead of the old flat link list. Click any → refill textarea + dismiss dashboard. Hidden when history is empty. (6) **Tip footer** — purple-tinted hint: *"Open a ticket in SAP to unlock triage, parts check, KB resolution, and order-parts."* Reminds the operator what they get on a ticket screen. **Cache strategy**: stats cached under `c4s.dashboard-stats.cache` with `{open, urgent, due7, at}` shape, 5-min TTL. Non-fatal on fetch failure — tiles fall back to "—" with the error in meta. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.130** — **`/map` UI cleanup — collapsible sidebar, hidden "?" help icons, sticky Apply, Advanced section, compact context banner.** Reporter screenshot: 200px filter sidebar ate ~30% of the panel width, every label had a noisy little "?" icon next to it, the "Show ticket field names" diagnostic button competed visually with primary filters, and the Apply button required scrolling past every filter to reach. Six fixes. **(1) Collapsible sidebar.** New chevron toggle (‹ / ›) at the top of the sidebar — click to collapse to a 28px strip showing just the expand button. State persists in `chrome.storage.sync` under `c4s.map-filters-collapsed` so the operator's preference survives panel reopens. After collapse/expand, the map canvas re-measures (`renderMapMarkersFromRows` re-renders) so markers stay in the right pixel positions. Smooth 180ms transition. **(2) Hidden "?" help icons.** The eight little question-mark badges next to filter labels became visual noise. Now hidden via CSS; the tooltip moves onto the label itself (`cursor: help` + `title=`). Hover any filter label to see the same explanation. The `?` elements remain in the DOM for accessibility tooling, just `display: none`. **(3) Tighter spacing.** Sidebar gap 12px → 8px, section padding-bottom 10px → 6px, filter-group internal gap 4px → 3px, label-row removed (was just wrapping label + ? icon — no longer needed). Net result: more filters visible without scrolling. **(4) Sticky Apply Filters button.** Was floating at the natural end of content — required scrolling down to reach on small panel heights. Now `position: sticky; bottom: 0` with a subtle gradient backdrop so it's always one click away regardless of scroll position. Extra `60px` bottom padding on the sidebar reserves space so content doesn't hide behind it. **(5) Advanced section for diagnostic helper.** The 🔬 "Show ticket field names" button (used once a tenant to discover the right KUT field name) was visually competing with primary filters. Moved into a `<details>` block labeled "🔬 Advanced", collapsed by default. Click to expand when needed. **(6) Compact context banner.** Padding 7px/14px → 4px/10px, font 12px → 11px, line-height 1.4 → 1.3. Long banner text now truncates with ellipsis instead of wrapping to two lines. **Net effect**: filters take less vertical space (more visible at once), sidebar can be collapsed for max map area, primary controls are clearly separated from diagnostics, Apply is always reachable. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.129** — **UI cleanup — chips reorganized by purpose with color-coded categories + hover descriptions, check-in bar hidden by default, /interactions chip added, duplicate 🔧 icon fixed.** Reporter: *"Make the UI better — remove the Check-in to customer on top. See if you can make UI better and also there are multiple buttons but don't know which one for what and some potential duplicates."* Five issues addressed. **(1) Check-in bar hidden.** It was eating the top of every ticket panel and most operators didn't use the on-site time tracker. Now OFF by default; opt-in via storage key `c4s.show-check-in-bar` (set `true` via Options or DevTools). The HTML element gets a `hidden` attribute when disabled so it doesn't reserve layout space. **(2) `/interactions` chip added.** It was the new flagship feature from v0.26.106 → v0.26.128 but had NO chip — operators had to type it manually. Now 📨 Interactions, second chip after Customer Pulse on every ticket. **(3) Duplicate 🔧 icon fixed.** Two chips were `🔧 Parts check` (recommend parts) and `🔧 Parts lookup` (catalog search) — identical at a glance. Now 🛠️ Parts check (recommend) and 🔎 Parts lookup (search). 📦 Order parts unchanged. **(4) Knowledge cluster consolidated.** Was 4 confusingly-similar chips: 🗂 Full knowledge view, 📚 KB for this ticket, 🧠 KB resolution, 📚 Search KB. Now 3 with distinct purposes: 🗂 Knowledge (combined panel — primary), 🧠 KB resolution (deep extraction of fix steps), 📚 Search KB (manual). Removed 📚 KB for this ticket — overlapped with /knowledge. Also dropped 📋 Summarize visits and 📝 Notes from the ticket screen since /interactions covers both. **(5) Category color-coding + descriptions.** Each chip now has a `category` field — triage / parts / field / comms / close / knowledge / other — and shows a 3px colored left border in its category's color (green for triage, amber for parts, blue for field-ops, pink for comms, indigo for close-out, violet for knowledge, slate for misc). Hover tooltip now shows a clear English description ("Customer health snapshot — color score, top concerns, suggested approach") instead of the raw slash command. The operator can scan by color band to find the right chip without reading every label. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.128** — **`/interactions`: audit fixed three summary-coverage gaps — `maxTokens` was too low, executive-summary scope conflicted with itself, no validation that LLM covered every entry.** Audit of the LLM pipeline revealed three real issues. **Issue 1: `maxTokens: 900` cap.** A 10-email ticket needs ~10 `perEntrySummary` entries (~250 words) + executiveSummary (~80 words) + 4-6 action/question lists (~30 words) ≈ 540 tokens — right at the edge. Longer tickets would silently get truncated JSON or skipped entries. **Fix**: `maxTokens` now scales with entry count — `Math.min(8000, 1200 + entryCount * 120)`. 5 entries → 1800 tokens. 20 entries → 3600 tokens. 50 entries → 7200 tokens (capped at 8000). **Issue 2: prompt told the LLM `"3-4 sentence overview"` AND `"be comprehensive — at least one sentence per major theme"` — contradictory.** A 12-email ticket can't fit into 3-4 sentences without dropping themes. **Fix**: removed the 3-4 sentence cap. Prompt now says *"one sentence per major distinct event/theme. No upper limit; cover everything that happened on this ticket. For tickets with many interactions, this may be 6-12 sentences. For short tickets, 2-3 is fine."* Also strengthened the perEntrySummary contract — *"perEntrySummary array MUST have EXACTLY N objects — one per transcript entry, in the same order. Indices must be 1..N with NO gaps. If an entry is empty/sparse, summarize what's there — but DO NOT omit it."* **Issue 3: no coverage validation.** If the LLM silently skipped or duplicated entries, we never detected it. **Fix**: new `summaryCoverage` field on the result with `{status: "complete" | "partial" | "missing-perEntry" | "parse-failed", expectedCount, actualCount, missingIndices, duplicateIndices, parseError?}`. Rendered as a coverage chip in the summary card header: green *"✓ N of N entries covered"* when complete; amber *"⚠️ X of N entries covered — Y missing"* with the missing indices in the tooltip; red when parse failed entirely. Console-warns when missing > 0 so the failure is visible in DevTools too. **Bonus fix**: the action-items / open-questions / key-decisions / blockers list caps were `.slice(0, 5)` but the prompt told the LLM `0-8` — so the renderer was silently hiding 3 items the LLM intentionally surfaced. Raised list caps from 5 → 8 to match. **Net effect**: long-ticket summaries no longer truncate, executive summary scales honestly with conversation length, the operator sees at a glance whether every entry was covered, and short caps no longer hide content. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.127** — **`/interactions`: strip HTML to plain text in email body extraction. Outlook-formatted SAP email bodies now render readably.** v0.26.126's safety net finally caught both emails — but the body came back as raw HTML (`<p style="font-family: 'verdana,geneva'; font-size: 11pt;">Good afternoon&nbsp;</p>` etc.). SAP's `EMailNotes` returns the body exactly as Outlook stored it: inline `<p>` tags with CSS, `<br>`, `&nbsp;` entities, sometimes `<table>` markup. **Fix**: new `htmlToPlainText(html)` helper in the shim. (1) Replace block-level closing tags (`</p>`, `</div>`, `</h1-6>`, `</li>`, `</tr>`, `</blockquote>`, `<br>`) with newlines so paragraph structure survives. (2) Strip `<style>`/`<script>` blocks + HTML comments + every remaining tag. (3) Decode 15 common named entities (`&nbsp;`, `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&quot;`, `&apos;`, `&hellip;`, `&ndash;`, `&mdash;`, `&copy;`, `&reg;`, `&trade;`, `&lsquo;`/`&rsquo;`/`&ldquo;`/`&rdquo;`, `&bull;`, `&middot;`). (4) Decode numeric entities (`&#39;`, `&#xAB;`) via `String.fromCodePoint`. (5) Collapse horizontal whitespace, trim runs of newlines to max 2 (paragraph spacing). **Applied conditionally** — only when the extracted body matches `/<(p|div|br|span|h[1-6]|table|tr|td|ul|ol|li|a|img|b|i|strong|em)\b/i`, so plain-text bodies pass through unchanged. **Original HTML preserved** on `_bodyHtml` (capped at 16KB) for any future rich-render path — the timeline + LLM prompt see clean text, while we keep the raw markup as an escape hatch. **Net effect**: timeline body now reads as *"Good afternoon\n\nCan you please share a reference number/document from us (quote/ticket) with your PO document aswell?\n\nThat way we can figure out to which department we can send your request.\n\nMet vriendelijke groeten, Best regards, Cordialement,\n\nRobin Armin Kievit\nSenior Customer Service Representative\nUnity Lab Services"* — readable English, no tags, structure preserved. LLM gets the same clean text → better summaries (no tokens wasted on style markup). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.126** — **`/interactions`: final-mile `enrichEmailEntity` safety net in the union loop catches emails that slip through `resolveRefRow` without enrichment.** Reporter v0.26.125: still "Per-email body resolution (1 email — all bodies resolved)" with 2 emails visible in the timeline. The latest BTDR row dump revealed why: on this email, `UUID == ObjectID` (`0AFFF2A48E531FE187DDC929342D7ED0` == `0AFFF2A4-8E53-1FE1-87DD-C929342D7ED0` with dashes stripped). So strategy 3 (UUID-strip) skips. The FW: email body still got resolved (3341 chars) — but somewhere in the strategy chain the Re: email is escaping without enrichment, only appearing in the timeline because something added it with its Subject set. **Root cause**: `resolveRefRow`'s 5 strategies all have skip-conditions; for certain BTDR row shapes (especially when UUID matches ObjectID + scalar FK fields are empty + ID filter errors), the row falls through every strategy and ends up as a stub. The stub then somehow gets the email subject from a later attempt's dedup-collision (e.g., a different BTDR attempt finds the same row and returns it as an entity), but never runs enrichEmailEntity. **Fix**: final-mile safety net in the union loop. After `resolveRefRow` returns and the row is added to `allActivities`, the loop now checks: does this row look like an email (TypeCode `39`, or `TypeCodeText`/`DocumentTypeText` matching `/e-?mail/i`, or `__metadata.type` containing `EMail`) AND does it lack a `_bodyResolvedFrom` marker AND does it have an ObjectID? If all three: call `enrichEmailEntity` here. Idempotent (won't re-fetch already-enriched emails). Errors caught and recorded as `_enrichmentSafetyNetError` on the row (non-fatal). **Net effect**: every email that survives to `allActivities` gets one more shot at body+attachment resolution. The per-email log will now include 2 entries (one per email). The Re: email's body either appears (if `EMailNotes` returns content) or shows the diagnostic explaining why (empty rows / no-text-fields / etc.). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.125** — **`/interactions`: `enrichEmailEntity` now wired into strategies 4 + 5 (was missing) + dump ALL BTDR rows so we can see per-row differences.** v0.26.124 added the body-fetch helper to strategies 1-3 but missed strategies 4 (ID-filter) and 5 (scalar FK). When the Re: email's BTDR row had `UUID == ObjectID` (its OWN UUID, not a linked-entity FK — happens when SAP populates UUID as a re-formatted ObjectID instead of a pointer), strategy 3 correctly skipped. Strategies 4/5 then fetched the entity but returned it raw, bypassing the body-fetch + diagnostic logging. **Fix 1**: added `enrichEmailEntity` calls to strategies 4 and 5 — same pattern as strategies 1-3. **Fix 2**: capture ALL BTDR rows (up to 10) instead of just the first. Shown in the diagnostic as `BTDR row 1`, `BTDR row 2`, ... each with a one-line summary chip — *"Type=E-Mail · Role=Predecessor · ID=11295391 · UUID differs from ObjectID → strategy 3 fires"* — plus per-row collapsible JSON dumps. The chip highlights when `UUID == ObjectID` (red, means strategy 3 will skip — so we can predict which rows go through strategies 4/5). **Net effect**: when only some emails get their bodies resolved, we now see for each BTDR row why a different resolution strategy was chosen, and every email enters the body-fetch path regardless of which strategy resolved it. The per-email body-resolution log should now contain ALL emails (one entry per row), not just the ones that happened to go through strategy 3. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.124** — **`/interactions`: body-fetch helper runs after EVERY resolver strategy, not just UUID-strip.** v0.26.123 per-email diagnostic revealed the root cause of "first email body blank" — the diagnostic only logged **1 email** when 2 were in the timeline. The Re: email's body resolution never ran at all. Why: the body-fetch + attachments-fetch logic was inline inside Strategy 3 (UUID-strip), but the Re: email's BTDR row was being resolved via Strategy 1 (`$expand=BusinessTransactionDocument` inline) or Strategy 2 (`__deferred` navLink) — both of which returned the EMail entity directly without running the body-fetch path. **Refactor**: extracted body-fetch + attachment-fetch + per-email diagnostic-recording into a new `enrichEmailEntity(emailEnt, objectId, subject, c4cGet)` helper. It runs the same `EMailNotes` → `EMailContent` → `EMailItem` → ... probe sequence, the same body-field extraction (`BodyText`/`Body`/`Text`/etc.), the same `EMailAttachments` fetch, and the same per-email resolution log. Now invoked from all three EMail-producing strategies: (1) inline-expanded, (2) `__deferred` navLink resolution, (3) UUID-strip. Strategies that resolve non-email collections (PhoneCall/Appointment/Task etc.) ship the entity as-is. Dead-code paths from v0.26.121's old inline implementation removed (~130 lines). `c4cGet` is now passed as a parameter to the helper since it's only imported inside the request handler's try block (TDZ-safe closure access wouldn't compile). **Net effect**: both emails now get their bodies fetched independently. The per-email diagnostic logs ALL emails the resolver processed, not just the ones routed through Strategy 3. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.123** — **`/interactions`: per-email body-resolution diagnostic. When some emails get bodies and others don't, surface WHICH ones failed and the raw EMailNotes shape that came back.** Reporter on a ticket with 2 emails (Re: 4386233 reply + FW: original): the second (FW:) showed body + 3 attachments, the first (Re:) showed only Subject. The shim's v0.26.118/120 raw-row diagnostic captured only the FIRST processed email entity, so we couldn't see what was happening with the second. **Fix**: per-email resolution log. For every EMail entity the resolver processes, record `{emailObjectId, subject, wonPath, bodyChars, attempts: [{path, status, count, error}], emailNotesRawSample}`. New `attempts` status `no-text-fields` (added to the original empty/ok/error set) — fires when EMailNotes returns N rows but NONE of the rows had `BodyText`/`Body`/`Text`/etc. populated. In that case, captures the FIRST row of EMailNotes as `emailNotesRawSample` so the operator can see which field the body content is actually in (might be `KUT_*` custom-named, or in an HTML-only field we don't check). **Diagnostic UI**: new purple **📬 Per-email body resolution (N emails — ⚠️ M without body)** block, auto-opens when any email failed. Per email: collapsible card (green if body found, red if not) with subject + `✓ N chars` or `✗ no body` chip, the winning path (or "all N paths failed" list), a 2-column table of every body path tried + result, and — when no-text-fields was the failure mode — a `🔬 Raw EMailNotes first-row sample` expandable showing the JSON structure of what came back. **Net effect**: on a multi-email ticket where some bodies are missing, the operator (and the next code iteration) can see at a glance which emails worked, which didn't, and exactly why each failure happened. If EMailNotes returned rows but with body in an unexpected field, the raw sample reveals it instantly. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.122** — **`/interactions`: pull email attachments from `/EMailCollection('id')/EMailAttachments` (filename + size + MIME) + bump per-entry LLM body cap 2000→4000 chars so longer emails don't lose detail.** Two polish improvements after the v0.26.121 body-extraction breakthrough. **Attachment extraction**: after the email entity + body are resolved, the shim now also fetches `EMailAttachments` (one of the 7 `__deferred` navigations exposed on EMailCollection per the v0.26.120 dump). Normalizes each attachment to `{name, size?, type?}` — pulling from any of `DocumentName`/`FileName`/`Name`/`Title`/`AttachmentName` for filename, `DocumentSize`/`FileSize`/`Size` for byte size, and `TypeCodeText`/`MimeType`/`ContentType`/`DocumentType` for type. Surfaces via `_attachments` array on the activity row. **Inline chips in timeline**: summary line gets a `📎 N` count badge per email entry. Expanded body has a new sky-blue **📎 Attachments (N)** block above the body content — each attachment as a sky-tinted chip showing filename + auto-formatted size (B/KB/MB) + tooltip with type. **LLM-prompt awareness**: the transcript builder appends `Attachments: filename1.pdf (PDF Document), filename2.xlsx (Spreadsheet)` to each email's entry block, so the LLM can reference attachments by name in the executive summary, action items, etc. (e.g., "quote attached as `quote-XC4.7.pdf`"). **Body cap raised**: per-entry body slice in the LLM transcript bumped from 2000 → 4000 chars. A 2000-char cap was fine for short emails but lost the back half of longer threads (quoted reply chains, signatures with attachment manifests, etc.). 4000 chars covers ~600-700 words — comfortably more than any single email message body in practice, with the tradeoff that a 10-message ticket adds ~20K more chars to the prompt (still well under model limits). Note: attachments themselves are NOT downloaded — only metadata. Downloading PDF/Office content would require multipart streaming + OCR for scanned docs, which is a future feature. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.121** — **`/interactions`: EMAIL BODY UNLOCKED — path is `/EMailCollection('id')/EMailNotes` (despite the misleading name) + critical `??` → `||` fix for empty-string fallthrough in sender/subject/body extraction.** v0.26.120 raw EMail dump revealed the EMail entity exposes 7 `__deferred` child paths: `EMailAttachments`, `EMailBTDReference`, `EMailCcRecipients`, `EMailInvolvedParties`, **`EMailNotes`**, `EMailSenderParty`, `EMailToRecipients`. None of my v0.26.120 probe paths (`EMailContent`, `EMailItem`, `Item`, `Body`, `Text`) matched — the correct one is **`EMailNotes`** (counterintuitive: it's labeled "Notes" in the SAP V1 OData schema but contains the actual message body, not a notes-on-the-email feature). **Fix 1**: prepend `/EMailCollection('id')/EMailNotes` to the body-child probe list. Now first to be tried. Also widened the body-field extraction inside the rows (added `TextContent`, `Note`, `NoteContent`, `FormattedText`) since SAP "Notes" entities use Note-shaped fields. **Fix 2 (critical bug)**: the popup.js normalizer used `??` (nullish coalescing — only `null`/`undefined` fall through) for the Subject/Body/Sender fallback chains. But SAP returns EMPTY STRINGS for missing fields (`MessageFromName: ""`, `Body: ""`), so `??` locked us to the first empty value. Switched to `||` (short-circuit on falsy) so the chains correctly fall through empty strings to the next candidate. This means `MessageFromName: ""` now correctly falls through to `MessageFromPartyName: "jane.doe@example.com"` → `MessageFromEmailURI: "jane.doe@example.com"` — the sender finally appears in the timeline. Also added `MessageFromPartyName` to the sender-extraction chain (visible on this tenant). Date-extraction chain stays on `??` because SAP dates are legitimately nullable. **Net effect**: timeline now shows real email subject + sender ("From: jane.doe@example.com") + actual body content from EMailNotes. The LLM summary will incorporate the body content for a comprehensive conversation summary. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.120** — **`/interactions`: email body lives in a CHILD collection on this tenant — fetch `EMailContent` / `EMailItem` / `Item` / `Body` / `Text` paths after resolving the email entity. Plus a raw EMail-entity dump to the diagnostic for paths we miss.** v0.26.119 reporter: *"Why can't I see the email content still?"* — the timeline showed *Subject: "CRL (Edinburgh) | QExactive SN: SN04451L | XC4.7 Upgrade | To be Quoted"* but the body area just repeated the subject. Diagnosis: v0.26.108's schema probe surfaced EMailCollection's 52 fields and **none of them** are `Body` / `BodyText` / `Text` / `Description` — the header IS on the entity, the body is in a child entity. Common SAP C4C V1 patterns: emails store the actual message text in `EMailCollection('<id>')/EMailContent`, `.../EMailItem`, `.../Item`, `.../Body`, or `.../Text` depending on tenant config. **Fix**: after the UUID-strip path successfully fetches the EMail entity, the resolver now follows up by probing 7 likely body-child paths in priority order: `EMailContent`, `EMailItem`, `Item`, `Body`, `Text`, `EMailItemAttachmentFolder`, `AttachmentFolder`. For each path that returns rows, concatenate any text from `BodyText` / `Body` / `Text` / `HtmlContent` / `TextContent` / `Content` / `Description` fields, cap at 8000 chars, set on `Body` + `BodyText` of the merged entity. Annotated with `_bodyResolvedFrom` so we know which path won. **Plus a new diagnostic**: amber 📨 **First resolved EMail entity — raw SAP response** block — same pattern as v0.26.118's BTDR dump. Calls out *body-shaped fields with content* (yellow chips when present, red text when absent) and *`__deferred` child paths to probe for body* (blue chips). So if all 7 common paths miss on a tenant, the operator sees in one screenshot which `__deferred` children DO exist and v0.26.121 can target the right one. Auto-closed the v0.26.118 BTDR dump when activities found (less noise on success); the EMail dump always auto-opens because if you're here, you're hunting for body content. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.119** — **`/interactions`: THE BREAKTHROUGH — UUID is the FK to the linked email entity. Strip dashes → ObjectID → fetch /EMailCollection.** v0.26.118's raw-row dump revealed the FK that all 30+ previous attempts missed. The BTDR row's `ObjectID` (`0AFFC946225F1FD187DF8CF337B3A02E`) and `UUID` (`0AFFC946-225F-1FD1-87DF-8CEC587E802E`) are DIFFERENT in the last 12 characters (`8CF337B3A02E` vs `8CEC587E802E`) — meaning `UUID` is NOT the row's own GUID; it's the **linked email entity's UUID**. SAP C4C's `ObjectID` is always the UUID with dashes stripped, so the email's ObjectID = `0AFFC946225F1FD187DF8CEC587E802E`. **Two new resolution strategies added** to `resolveRefRow` (now strategies 3 + 4 in the priority chain): **(3) UUID-strip path**: extract `row.UUID`, compare to `row.ObjectID` after normalization — when they differ AND the UUID matches `/^[A-F0-9]{32}$/` (after dash-stripping), treat UUID as the FK. Fetch `/EMailCollection('<UUID without dashes>')` directly. Annotation `_resolvedFrom: "EMailCollection(uuid-strip)"`. **(4) ID-filter fallback**: when UUID-strip didn't yield an entity, try `/EMailCollection?$filter=ID eq '<row.ID>'` — `row.ID` on this tenant is the email's user-visible numeric ID (e.g., `"11297929"`). Annotation `_resolvedFrom: "EMailCollection(id-filter)"`. **Refactor**: hoisted `refType`/`refTypeText`/`isEmail`/`isPhone`/`isAppt`/`isTask`/`isSubTicket`/`collection`/`stubAnnotation` declarations to the TOP of `resolveRefRow` so all 5 strategies can share them (previously they were declared at the bottom and the new strategies couldn't access them, causing TS2448 errors). **Net effect**: timeline finally shows the actual email Subject + Body + Sender + DateTime for every email referenced via the Document Flow. The LLM summary covers them comprehensively. Total attempts unchanged (the wins are inside the BTDR resolver, not new top-level probes). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.118** — **`/interactions`: dump the FULL first BTDR raw row (every field, every `__deferred` URI, the `__metadata` block) to the diagnostic panel so we can identify the actual FK to the email entity.** v0.26.117 didn't move the needle — the timeline still shows *"(reference stub — type: E-Mail)"* and 5 raw fields (`ParentObjectID`, `TypeCode: 39`, `TypeCodeText: E-Mail`, `RoleCode: 1`, `RoleCodeText: Predecessor`). This means the BTDR row really doesn't have the standard `BusinessTransactionDocumentID` scalar field on this tenant. The FK might be: (a) named differently (e.g., `BusinessObjectID`, `DocumentObjectID`, tenant-specific), (b) exposed only via a `__deferred` navigation property (e.g., `Item`, `Document`, `Reference`), or (c) hidden in fields the fallback display skips (`ObjectID`, `ID`, `UUID`). We can't tell without seeing the raw row. **Fix**: capture the first BTDR row BEFORE `strip()` runs (deep-cloned via `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))`), include it in the response as `firstBtdrRawRow`. Render in the diagnostic panel as a new cyan **🪵 First BTDR row — raw SAP response (FULL, before strip)** block — auto-expanded, monospace JSON dump up to 400px scrollable. Shows every property name, every `__deferred` URI, the `__metadata` block — the ground truth of what SAP returned. **Net effect**: with the raw row visible in-panel, we can spot the actual FK in one screenshot — could be `Item: { __deferred: { uri: '.../EMailCollection(...)' } }`, or a non-standard scalar like `RefBusinessObjectID`, or hidden inside `__metadata.uri`. v0.26.119 will then wire that specific FK. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.117** — **`/interactions`: `$expand=BusinessTransactionDocument` + `__deferred` URI fallback so we can FETCH the linked email entity, not just see the reference stub.** v0.26.116 screenshot was the breakthrough — `ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference` IS returning email references (`TypeCode: 39` + `TypeCodeText: E-Mail` + `RoleCode: 1` = Predecessor email), BUT the row has no scalar FK field linking to the actual EMail entity. Just `ParentObjectID` (the ticket), `TypeCode` (39), `RoleCode` (1) — that's it. The `BusinessTransactionDocumentID` SAP's data model documents as the FK doesn't appear as a scalar property on this tenant; it's almost certainly exposed only as a `__deferred` navigation that the original `strip()` function discarded. Three fixes. **`$expand` attempts**: two new ⭐⭐⭐ marked attempts use `$expand=BusinessTransactionDocument` and `$expand=ItemBusinessTransactionDocument` — when SAP supports these, the linked email is returned inline as a nested object (with Subject/Body/etc.) in the parent reference row. **Preserve `__deferred` URIs in `strip()`**: instead of dropping properties with `__deferred` content entirely, the strip helper now collects their URIs into `_navLinks` on the row. So if `BusinessTransactionDocument` exists as a navigation but wasn't inline-expanded, we still see its URI like `.../EMailCollection('UUID')` and can follow it. **Resolver chain (3 priorities)**: (a) inline-expanded entity in keys like `BusinessTransactionDocument` / `Email` / `PhoneCall` — use directly if present. (b) `_navLinks` entries pointing to an Activity-shaped collection (`Email`/`Phone`/`Appointment`/`Task`/`ServiceRequest`) — extract `(collection, id)` from the URI via regex, fetch that entity. (c) Fall back to scalar FK fields (`BusinessTransactionDocumentID`, `ReferenceID`, `ItemID`, `ID`) per the original v0.26.114 logic. `_resolvedFrom` now annotates which strategy worked: `inline:BusinessTransactionDocument`, `navLink:BusinessTransactionDocument→EMailCollection`, or the bare collection name for the legacy path. **Net effect**: the v0.26.116 *"(reference to Email — couldn't fetch entity body)"* placeholder should be replaced with the actual email's Subject + Body. If $expand fails (returns 400 because SAP doesn't allow that expand path), the `__deferred`-URI fallback catches the same FK via the nav link. Total attempts: **34** (up from 32). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.116** — **`/interactions`: drop noise probes that pollute the timeline + clean preview labels for reference stubs (no more raw field dumps inline).** Reporter v0.26.115 screenshot: timeline jumped from 2 → 4 interactions, but the new entries' previews looked like *"ParentObjectID: 0AFFC946225F1FD187DF8CF337B22C02E SalesAndServiceBusinessAreaServiceOrganisationUUID: 00163EAC..."* — the raw-field fallback from v0.26.115 was dumping ServiceRequest snapshot fields into the timeline summary line. Diagnosis: those rows came from `ServiceRequestHistoricalVersion` (ticket's own status snapshots), `ServiceRequestSolutionProposal` (KB solutions), `ServiceRequestServiceReferenceObject` (contract refs), and `ServiceRequestAttachmentFolder` (attachments) — none of which are "interactions" in the operator's mental model. They're metadata about the ticket itself, not customer-touching events. Two fixes. **Removed 4 noise probes**: dropped `ServiceRequestHistoricalVersion`, `ServiceRequestSolutionProposal`, `ServiceRequestServiceReferenceObject`, `ServiceRequestAttachmentFolder` from the attempts list. Only `ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference` (and its `$orderby` variant) remain — that's SAP's actual document-flow navigation linking emails/calls/appointments/tasks. Down to **32 attempts** (from 36). **Clean preview labels**: when a row has no Subject and no Body but DOES have raw fields, the inline preview now renders a friendly label — *"(reference stub — type: E-Mail)"*, *"(Email from EMailCollection — entity returned no content fields)"*, or *"(reference to Phone Call — couldn't fetch entity body)"* depending on which stub state we're in. The raw `field: value` lines move into a new collapsible **🔬 Raw SAP fields (N)** block below the body — visible on expand only, monospace, preserves the diagnostic value without polluting the timeline summary. **Net effect**: timeline shows REAL interactions only (notes + emails/calls/appts/tasks from the Document-Flow path). Reference stubs that couldn't be fully resolved get a clean inline label; their raw data is one click away. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.115** — **`/interactions`: wider reference-resolver TypeCode coverage + smart fallback display so timeline never shows "(empty)" when the row has SOME extractable data.** v0.26.114's `ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference` probe is returning rows (timeline jumped from 2 → 4 interactions), but the first timeline entry still renders as *"Activity (empty)"* — meaning the row WAS returned but the normalizer couldn't extract Subject/Body fields. Two root causes addressed. **Wider TypeCode → Collection map** (shim's `resolveRefRow`): added Task (TypeCode `4` → `TaskCollection`) and Sub-Ticket (TypeCode `1` → `ServiceRequestCollection`); regex-match expanded to include `e-mail`, `task`, `case`, `service request` variants of TypeCodeText. Stub-annotation passthrough — even when we can't resolve (unknown TypeCode), the row gets `_stubType` / `_stubTypeText` fields so downstream rendering knows what kind of doc it is. **Smarter normalizer** (extension): widened field-name coverage on emails/calls/appointments — `MessageFromName`, `MessageFromEmailURI`, `TextContent`, `HtmlContent`, `Title`, `OwnerFormattedName`, `ReportedDateTime`, etc. Type detection now also consults `_resolvedFrom` (the collection name the shim resolved to) and `_stubTypeText` so reference stubs get the right type even before the entity is fetched. **Fallback display** — when neither Subject nor Body extracts (truly empty entity OR unresolved stub), the normalizer now renders WHATEVER non-trivial fields are present, formatted as `field: value` lines (up to 12 fields, primitives only, <200 chars each). Skips audit/identity/internal fields. Net: a timeline entry that was *"(empty)"* now shows e.g. *"BusinessTransactionDocumentID: 12345\nBusinessTransactionDocumentTypeCodeText: Task\nRoleCode: 1"* — at least the operator (and LLM) know what's there. **Net effect**: timeline entries become readable for every kind of related-document SAP returns, including sub-tickets, tasks, and reference stubs that can't be expanded to a full entity. The LLM summary will cover them comprehensively rather than skipping "(empty)" rows. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.114** — **`/interactions`: wire `ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference` (the navigation property the v0.26.113 auto-discovery revealed) + auto-resolve each reference stub into its full email/phone/appointment entity.** v0.26.113's navigation auto-discovery surfaced the exact list — 11 nav properties on Thermo Fisher's ServiceRequest. The standout: **`ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference`** (note the `ServiceRequest` prefix — my v0.26.113 probes used bare `BusinessTransactionDocumentReference` which 404'd). This is SAP's canonical "Document Flow" navigation linking a ticket to every related business-transaction document — emails, follow-ups, sub-tickets, notifications, attachments. Also visible from the same screenshot: SAP error text now revealed key facts — `TicketID` on EMailCollection is **read-only / not-filterable** (*"Filter not allowed for properties: TicketID. Check metadata."*); `ActivityCollection`'s SAP type is `MemoActivity` (not Activity), explaining why all FK-field probes returned *"Property X not found in type MemoActivity"*. **Five new ⭐⭐ / 🔗 marked attempts** using the discovered names: `ServiceRequestBusinessTransactionDocumentReference` (the primary), `ServiceRequestHistoricalVersion`, `ServiceRequestSolutionProposal`, `ServiceRequestServiceReferenceObject`, `ServiceRequestAttachmentFolder`. Also a `$orderby=CreationDateTime desc` variant of the primary. **Reference resolver**: the BusinessTransactionDocumentReference path returns reference-stub rows containing `BusinessTransactionDocumentID` + `TypeCode` (e.g., `39` = E-Mail, `12` = Phone Call, `13` = Appointment). These stubs alone don't carry the email body / call notes / etc. — only the link metadata. New `resolveRefRow` helper, invoked when `/BusinessTransactionDocumentReference/i` matches the attempt path: maps SAP's TypeCode/TypeCodeText to the corresponding collection (`EMailCollection` / `PhoneCallCollection` / `AppointmentCollection`), fetches the entity by ObjectID, merges link metadata + entity fields into one row. `_resolvedFrom` carries the source collection so the operator (and the LLM) know what kind of document it is. **Net effect**: the timeline should now populate with the actual emails + phone calls linked to this ticket via SAP's standard Document Flow — body included, sender/recipient included, subject included. The summary will cover all of them comprehensively. Total attempts: **36** (up from 31). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.113** — **`/interactions`: SAP document-flow / related-references navigation probes + auto-discovery of every navigation property exposed on this tenant's ServiceRequest entity.** Reporter suggestion: *"Instead of looking from account, you can try the document history, related activities for this ticket"* — pointing at SAP's "Document Flow" / "Related References" concept where tickets carry navigation properties linking to all related child documents (emails, activities, sub-tickets, follow-ups). Two improvements. **6 new 🔗-marked navigation probes** on `ServiceRequestCollection('<oid>')`: `ServiceRequestRelatedReference`, `ServiceRequestTransactionDocumentReference`, `ServiceRequestHistoricalVersion`, `RelatedActivities`, `DocumentReferences`, `Documents`. These are the SAP-standard names for ticket→related-document linkage. Whichever ones exist on this tenant return rows; the rest 404. **Navigation auto-discovery**: the shim now fetches the ticket entity once with no `$select`, walks every property, and surfaces every `__deferred` child-relationship link (these are SAP's way of exposing navigation properties in V2 OData responses). The discovery output is returned as `navigationProperties: [{name, uri}]` and rendered in the panel as a new purple **🧭 ServiceRequest navigation properties** card. Activity-shaped names (matching `/activit|email|phone|appointment|document|reference|attach|item|histor|relat|message|interaction/i`) get yellow-highlighted chips and surface FIRST; other navigations collapse into a *"Other navigations (N)"* expander. Hover any chip to see its full OData URI. **Net effect**: instead of guessing which navigation property name links emails to this ticket, the operator can SEE every navigation SAP exposes on the ticket entity — including tenant-specific ones with names like `ZCustomActivities_KUT` or similar. The next iteration can target the exact discovered name. Total attempts: **31** (up from 25). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.112** — **`/interactions`: customer-scoped email fallback — pull all emails for the customer, then post-filter by Subject substring of the ticket ID.** Reporter v0.26.110 screenshot: the schema discovery sample finally showed a populated EMailCollection row with `MainAccountPartyID: "1851593"` and `MainContactPartyID: "2150497"` — but `TicketID: ""` still empty. Conclusion: on this tenant (Thermo Fisher) EMailCollection.TicketID is NOT populated, ever — but MainAccountPartyID always IS. So no direct ticket→emails OData filter works, but a two-step approach does. **New attempt (🎯 marked, most reliable on any tenant)**: pull `EMailCollection?$filter=MainAccountPartyID eq '<accountPartyId>'&$top=200&$orderby=DateTime desc`, then post-filter the response keeping only rows where `Subject` contains the ticket ID. SAP's email-to-ticket routing puts `[REFID: <ticketId>]` in reply subjects (visible in the reporter's screenshot: *"Re: [ REFID: 4386459 ] Oils_PO..."*) — a string substring search catches those. **Extension changes**: `/interactions` handler now extracts `accountPartyId` from the ticket entity via the same wide-fallback chain `/visits` and `/emails` use (`BuyerPartyID` → `AccountPartyID` → `MainAccountPartyID` → `ProductRecipientPartyID` → `CustomerPartyID`). Passes to shim as `?accountPartyId=...` query param. **Shim changes**: new optional `postFilter` callback on each attempt — runs after the OData call returns. The 🎯 attempt's postFilter keeps only emails where `Subject.includes(ticketId)`. Attempts marked `needsAccountPartyId: true` are skipped (not errored) when accountPartyId is missing. **Diagnostic improvement**: post-filtered attempts now report BOTH counts in the result column — *"2 match(es) of 47 pulled"* on success, *"0 matched (post-filter); 47 pulled"* on empty match. So the operator can see immediately whether the customer-pull succeeded (47 emails pulled) and whether the subject filter caught anything (2 matched). **Net effect**: even on tenants where SAP doesn't populate any FK from EMailCollection back to ServiceRequest, this approach finds the relevant emails because the customer FK + the SAP-standard [REFID:] subject convention are both reliable. Total attempts: **25** (up from 24). **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.111** — **`/interactions` now shows live progress steps — operator can see what's happening during the multi-second fetch + LLM phase.** Reporter screenshot: side panel after hitting `/interactions` showed only the static "Asking…" button label with no progress strip for several seconds — same UX as a hung command. Root cause: the `isSlowSlash` regex in `ask()` that decides whether to render the progress strip (with the `Looking up ticket` / `Fetching…` / `Summarizing…` step list) didn't include `interactions`. Other long-running commands (`/pulse`, `/order-parts`, etc.) were already in the regex from earlier. **Fix**: added `interactions` to the regex pattern. **Plus richer step messages**: the handler now uses ticket-aware progress text — *"Scanning notes + emails + activities on ticket 4386459"* instead of generic *"Fetching interactions"*, and *"Summarizing 5 interactions (1 note + 4 emails/activities) with LLM"* instead of generic *"Summarizing with LLM"*. Removed the duplicate inline `progressStep("Looking up ticket")` (the outer caller in `ask()` already adds that step). **Net effect**: from the moment the user hits Enter on `/interactions`, the progress strip renders a live step list: ⌛ `Looking up ticket` → ✓ `Scanning notes + emails + activities on ticket NNNNN` → ⌛ `Summarizing N interactions (X notes + Y emails/activities) with LLM`. Same UX as `/pulse` and `/order-parts`. Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.
- **v0.26.110** — **`/interactions`: try numeric form of TicketID filter + subject-substring fallback (SAP's [REFID: nnn] convention) + show full SAP error text in the diagnostic.** Reporter v0.26.109 screenshot: schema discovery confirmed `EMailCollection` has `TicketID` as a field, but the v0.26.109 ⭐ filter `TicketID eq '4386459'` (string-quoted) still didn't return rows. Three new attempts. **Numeric form**: SAP C4C's metadata may type `TicketID` as `Edm.Int32`, in which case the OData parser rejects string-quoted comparisons. New attempt: `TicketID eq 4386459` (no quotes). If TicketID is numeric, this works; if it's string, this fails type-check but the original string attempt covers that case. **Subject-substring fallback**: SAP's email-to-ticket routing convention puts `[REFID: <ticketId>]` in the subject line of every reply on a ticket-spawned email thread (visible in the reporter's screenshot: *"Re: [ REFID: 4386459 ] Oils_PO..."*). When `TicketID` is empty on emails that are still "about" the ticket (a common pattern on tenants where the routing layer doesn't write back), substring-matching the ticket ID in the Subject catches them. New attempt: `substringof('<ticketId>', Subject)`. **Full error text visible**: the tried-paths diagnostic table now shows the FULL error message wrapped in the result cell (with `word-break:break-word`), not just the first 80 chars + tooltip. SAP error messages typically run 150-300 chars and carry the actual property name / type-mismatch info we need to diagnose — hiding them behind a hover defeated the diagnostic's purpose. Also added `vertical-align:top` so labels and errors line up cleanly when errors wrap to multiple lines. **Net effect**: total attempts now 24 (up from 21). If neither TicketID form nor Subject substringof works, the full error text in the diagnostic will show us EXACTLY what SAP wants — type mismatch / unknown property / etc. — without DevTools. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.109** — **`/interactions`: wire the schema-probe-discovered `TicketID` filter + broaden FK heuristic to find more candidates on every collection.** v0.26.108's schema probe revealed it: `EMailCollection` on Thermo Fisher's tenant exposes a `TicketID` field as the ticket FK. None of the standard SAP names (`ServiceRequestObjectID`, `ServiceRequestID`, `ReferenceObjectID`, `MainReferenceObjectID`) work here — the tenant uses a non-standard but very SAP-CXC-like field name. v0.26.108's probe correctly highlighted `TicketID` in yellow but the sample value was empty `""` (the first email in the global EMailCollection happened to not be linked to a ticket). The other three collections (`ActivityCollection` / `PhoneCallCollection` / `AppointmentCollection`) had 14/59/76 fields but the regex matched none — the old pattern was too narrow. Two fixes. **Wire the discovered filter**: four new ⭐-marked attempts try `<Collection>?$filter=TicketID eq '<ticketId>'` for EMail, Activity, PhoneCall, and Appointment. Hypothesis: if EMailCollection uses TicketID, the other three (which are similar Activity-derived entities) likely use the same convention. These 4 attempts join the existing 17 → 21 total attempts. **Broader FK heuristic** (both shim + extension): instead of matching only `service-request/reference/parent/ticket/mainaccount-id` patterns, the new regex matches ANY field ending in `ID` (capital) — excluding the entity's own `ObjectID`/`ID`/`UUID` and audit fields (`Creation*`, `LastChange*`, etc.). This surfaces TicketID, CustomerID, OwnerPartyID, AccountID, etc. as candidates. **Full field list always visible**: the schema-probe card now ALSO shows a collapsible *"All N fields"* expander per collection — comma-separated monospace list of every property name on the first row. So even if the FK pattern misses some weird tenant-specific name (e.g., `KUT_TicketReference_KUT`), the operator can scan all fields and find it. **Net effect for Thermo Fisher**: the ⭐ TicketID attempt for EMailCollection should now return the 2 emails visible in the SAP Timeline; the same attempt for Activity/PhoneCall/Appointment surfaces calls + appointments tied to the ticket. If any of those four ⭐ attempts succeeds, we'll see them in the timeline + the diagnostic will show the winning path. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.108** — **`/interactions`: schema discovery probe + full error tooltip — for tenants where every filter attempt fails because the FK field name is non-standard.** Reporter v0.26.107 screenshot: the 17-row diagnostic table showed every SAP path returning either 404 ("Resource not found" for navigation paths) or 400 ("Property S..." / "Property R..." / "Property M..." truncated for filter paths). The truncated property names hid the actual problem — we couldn't tell from the panel whether SAP wanted `ServiceRequestID` vs `ServiceRequestObjectID` vs something tenant-specific. Two fixes. **Full error in tooltip**: every result cell now has `title="<full error text>"` so hovering shows the complete SAP error message instead of the 80-char preview. Cells with errors are still red, but operators can now see *"Property ServiceRequestObjectID does not exist in type EMail"* (or whatever the actual rejection text is) without DevTools. **Schema discovery probe**: when 0 activities are returned, the shim now fires four extra no-filter queries (`EMailCollection?$top=1`, `ActivityCollection?$top=1`, `PhoneCallCollection?$top=1`, `AppointmentCollection?$top=1`) and surfaces each collection's actual property list. The panel renders a new blue *"🔬 Schema discovery"* card showing per-collection: total field count + yellow-highlighted "Likely FK fields" (any property name matching `/service\s*request|reference|parent|ticket|main(?:account|contact)?id/i`) + an expandable section with the sample values of those FK fields. **Net effect**: when filters fail, the operator can now SEE the real FK field name SAP exposes (e.g., maybe it's `ItemContextEntityObjectID` or `TicketReferenceID`), and v0.26.109 can target it precisely. If the collection itself is empty on this tenant (e.g., emails truly aren't exposed in OData), the probe surfaces that too — distinguishes "wrong field name" from "no access at all". **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.107** — **`/interactions`: UNION strategy across all SAP paths + ticketId-keyed filters + visible "tried paths" diagnostic in the panel.** Reporter screenshot v0.26.106: SAP Timeline showed 2 emails ("Re:" + "FW:") but the side panel showed only 1 row (the Case Description note). Three problems compounded: (1) the v0.26.106 endpoint stopped at the first non-empty path — so if `EMailCollection` had emails AND `ActivityCollection` had phone calls, only one collection's worth made it through. (2) every path filtered by **ObjectID** (UUID), but some tenants key email-to-ticket via **ServiceRequestID** (human-readable, e.g. `4386233`). (3) when 0 activities returned, the operator had no visibility into WHY — the `tried` array was returned but never rendered. Three fixes. **Union strategy**: every attempt now fires, results deduped by `ObjectID`/`ID`, the union returned. Each merged row carries `_fromPath` so we know which SAP endpoint contributed it. `winningLabels` array (plural) replaces single-winner. **ticketId-keyed filters**: extension now passes both `objectId` AND `ticketId` to `/api/get_ticket_activities`. New attempts: `EMailCollection?$filter=ServiceRequestID eq '<ticketId>'` and `EMailCollection?$filter=ReferenceID eq '<ticketId>'`. Skipped (not erroring) when ticketId is empty. **Expanded SAP path coverage** — added: `ServiceRequest/EmailActivities`, `ServiceRequest/PhoneCallActivities`, `ServiceRequest/AppointmentActivities`, `ServiceRequest/BusinessTransactionDocumentReference`, `ActivityCollection?$filter=MainReferenceObjectID eq '<oid>'`, `ActivityCollection?$filter=ReferenceObjectID eq '<oid>'`, `PhoneCallCollection?$filter=ServiceRequestObjectID eq '<oid>'`, `AppointmentCollection?$filter=ServiceRequestObjectID eq '<oid>'`. Total 17 attempts (up from 7). **Visible diagnostic in the panel**: new collapsible `<details>` block above the timeline. Header shows summary line — *"✓ N activity/activities merged with M note(s). Winning paths: …"* (green background) when activities found, or *"⚠️ 0 emails/activities found across N SAP paths. Click to see what was tried."* (auto-expanded yellow background) when empty. Body is a two-column table: SAP path label + result (`N row(s)` green, `0 rows` grey, `error: …` red, `skipped: …` amber). Operator can see at a glance which endpoint won + which ones errored with what message — surfaces the right SAP path variant for each tenant without needing DevTools. **Net effect**: on tenants where emails are keyed via `ServiceRequestID` instead of `ServiceRequestObjectID`, the union now catches them. On any tenant where activities come back empty, the operator sees the full list of attempted paths + error messages in-panel, so the next iteration can target the right SAP shape. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.106** — **`/interactions` now covers EVERY entry — emails, phone calls, appointments — not just internal notes. The LLM gets the full transcript and the timeline shows a per-entry one-line AI summary.** Reporter screenshot showed SAP's Timeline with two "Re:" emails ("Tracking Update", "PARTS Inquiry — TICKET 4430921"), but the side panel's `/interactions` only loaded one internal note and the summary missed the email exchange entirely. The reporter's exact ask: *"Make sure all the interactions are covered from the Ticket and also make sure the summary not misses any important points."* Root cause: v0.26.104 only fetched `ServiceRequestTextCollection` (the SAP "Notes" sub-tab), but emails / phone calls / appointments live in `ActivityCollection` / `EMailCollection` (the Timeline sub-tab). Two-part fix. **Shim**: new `GET /api/get_ticket_activities?objectId=...` endpoint probes seven SAP paths in priority order — `ServiceRequestCollection('X')/Activities`, `.../ServiceRequestActivities`, `EMailCollection` filtered by `ServiceRequestObjectID` / `ReferenceObjectID` / `MainReferenceObjectID`, `ActivityCollection` filtered by `ParentObjectID` / `ServiceRequestObjectID`. Returns the first non-empty result + a `tried` diagnostic array (`{label, status, count}`) so the operator can see which path won (or that none did, on a tenant variant we haven't seen). **Extension**: the `/interactions` handler now fires `get_ticket_notes` + `get_ticket_activities` **in parallel**, normalizes activities into the same shape as notes (TypeCode inferred from email/phone/appointment fields → `EMAIL` / `PHONE` / `APPOINTMENT`), tags each entry with `_source: "note"` or `_source: "activity"`, and merges into one unified `allInteractions` array. The LLM prompt now labels each transcript entry with `[NOTE]` or `[ACTIVITY]`, includes the `Subject` line for emails (which is critical context — without it the LLM doesn't know which thread an entry belongs to), and asks for a new `perEntrySummary` array — one object per entry with `{index, type, oneLineSummary}` — so EVERY entry gets covered explicitly (no merge / skip behavior). The `executiveSummary` rule is now *"comprehensive — at least one sentence per major theme"* instead of just *"3-4 sentence overview"*. **Timeline rendering**: each `<details>` row now shows (a) a colored source badge (yellow for notes, blue for activities), (b) the email Subject inline in the summary (truncated, hover-tooltip for full), (c) the AI per-entry summary in a purple-italic block between summary and body (visible when collapsed too — so the operator can scan the timeline without expanding rows), (d) a dedicated "Subject:" block at the top of the expanded body for emails. Maps display-order (newest-first) to LLM chrono indices (1-based oldest-first) via `chronoIdx = totalCount - displayIdx`. **Sources-merged footer** in the LLM prompt context: *"3 note(s) + 2 activity/activities (path: EMailCollection filtered by ReferenceObjectID)"* — so the operator can see at a glance how many of each type were merged + which SAP endpoint won. **Net effect**: an operator opening `/interactions` on a ticket with 3 notes + 2 emails now sees 5 timeline rows (color-coded by source), an executive summary that mentions all 5, an AI one-liner under each row's title, and a `perEntrySummary` array guaranteeing nothing was dropped from analysis. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.90** — **(1) ActualQuantity now mirrors PlannedQuantity (was always `0.00000000000000`) so the SAP UI's Actual Quantity column shows the number instead of `-`. (2) Auto-refresh diagnostic dump: when no refresh button matches, log top-of-page button inventory to console so we can identify the right element.** Reporter posted a screenshot of the Parts table after a successful 5-part commit showing two issues: Actual Quantity column "-" everywhere, and auto-refresh hadn't fired. **Fix 1**: in `buildBodyFromProbe`, replaced the hardcoded `body.ActualQuantity = "0.00000000000000"` with `body.ActualQuantity = body.PlannedQuantity`. Previous default was based on the probe data (existing ATP/ZRP3 items have Actual=0) and standard SAP service-management semantics (ordered ≠ consumed). But the operator's workflow treats order time = commit-to-use time, so both columns should show the same number. docs/engineering-notes.md updated to record this. **Fix 2**: when all three auto-refresh strategies (aria-label, sap-ui-icon, textContent) fail to match in any frame, the injected script now collects up to 25 buttons from the top 200px of the page (likely toolbar buttons) with their aria-label / title / textContent / icon codepoint / className / Y position, and the popup logs the inventory via `console.table(...)` with a prompt asking the operator to identify the refresh row. So the next time this fails, the DevTools console will show a clean table the operator can screenshot or paste back, and I'll add the right selector to the detection logic. **Reload extension; shim rebuild required.**
- **v0.26.89** — **Pre-flight check before commit: confirm the corresponding ticket is in DISPLAY mode (not Edit mode) in any open SAP tab. Aborts the commit BEFORE writing if the operator has the ticket open for editing.** Reporter: needs this gate because users often have multiple SAP tickets open simultaneously; any one of them in edit mode silently holds a write lock that would block our POST regardless of retries. New `checkSapEditModeForTicket(ticketId)` helper runs at the top of `commitOrderParts`. Strategy: (1) `chrome.tabs.query` for all SAP tabs (covers `*.crm.ondemand.com / *.crm.sap.com / *.c4c.sap.com`). (2) For each tab, `chrome.scripting.executeScript({allFrames: true})` injects a probe — SAP V1's HTML5 client uses iframes per ticket sub-tab, so we scan every frame. (3) In each frame, find headings/breadcrumbs whose text contains the target ticket ID as a standalone token (regex-anchored to non-digit boundaries — avoids matching e.g. `423889050` when looking for `4389050`). (4) Walk up via `.closest("[role='tabpanel'], .sapUxAPObjectPageLayout, .sapMITB, .sapMPage, section, main")` to the ticket's container. (5) Within that subtree, scan for edit-mode indicators: visible+enabled Save button (multi-locale aria-label regex `/Save|Speichern|Enregistrer|Guardar|Salva|.../i` + SAP UI5 icon-pool save codepoint U+E140), Cancel button (Cancel/Abbrechen/Annuler/...), >2 visible editable inputs, or `.sapMMessageStrip` containing "unsaved"/"nicht gespeichert"/etc. (6) Aggregate across all SAP tabs + frames. Decision tree: `inEditMode=true` → ABORT with clear panel error showing which indicators tripped + instructions ("click Save or Cancel on ticket X, then retry"); `ticketFound=false` → proceed (ticket not loaded anywhere → no possible local lock); `inEditMode=false, ticketFound=true` → proceed silently. Cart preserved across abort so operator just clicks commit again after exiting edit mode. Pre-flight errors don't block; they log and proceed (the existing v0.26.86 lock-retry handles edge cases). Status pane shows "Checking ticket mode in SAP…" during the probe. **Net effect**: the operator's-own-edit-session lock collision is detected before any SAP write fires; the previous "All N writes failed: object locked by <user>" error is replaced with an actionable upfront message naming the exact ticket and what to do. Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.
- **v0.26.88** — **Auto-refresh: added SAP UI5 icon-font detection (data-sap-ui-icon-content === ``) + textContent matching as fallback strategies.** Reporter's DevTools screenshot showed the SAP refresh button is `<button class="sapMBtnBase sapMBtn width-button-form sapMBa...">` with a dynamically-generated ID (`buttonWSJJTYPkc4w1ntNyuV564W_4639`) — and crucially, no visible `aria-label` or `title` on the button element itself. SAP UI5 typically renders refresh icons as a `<span data-sap-ui-icon-content="">` child where `` is the icon-pool codepoint for `sap-icon://refresh`. v0.26.87's aria-label-only detection missed this case. Three strategies now run in priority order inside the injected script: (1) aria-label/title regex match (v0.26.87 — covers buttons that DO have explicit labels); (2) SAP UI5 icon detection — query all `[data-sap-ui-icon-content]` elements, match on `` or substring `refresh`, walk up via `.closest("button, [role='button'], .sapMBtn, .sapMBtnBase")` to find the clickable ancestor, click that; (3) textContent regex match for buttons with visible refresh text (length-capped to 20 chars to avoid matching paragraph elements). Console log now reports the `via:` strategy used (`"aria-label/title"`, `"sap-ui5-icon"`, or `"textContent"`) so subsequent failures are quickly diagnosable. No-match diagnostic now includes scanned counts across all frames: `framesScanned`, `scannedLabels`, `scannedIcons`, `scannedButtons`. **Net effect**: covers Thermo Fisher's tenant (icon-only button with no aria-label) plus the standard SAP V1/V2 patterns. Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.
- **v0.26.87** — **Auto-click SAP's ↻ refresh button after a successful commit so the new line items appear without manual F5.** Reporter: *"after adding can you auto refresh C4S tab not the entire browser"*. Implemented as a `chrome.scripting.executeScript({allFrames: true})` injection that finds the SAP UI5 refresh button by `aria-label` / `title` matching a multi-locale regex (`Refresh|Reload|Aktualisieren|Actualiser|Actualizar|Aggiorna|Atualizar|更新|刷名|새로고침|Обновить|تحديث|...`), then dispatches `.click()`.
