First match wins across all frames (SAP's HTML5 client uses iframes heavily). New helper `refreshSapTabAfterCommit()` runs after `commitOrderParts` if `successes.length > 0`. Waits 1200ms before clicking so SAP's server-side write fully propagates to the client-cache layer — otherwise the refresh fetches a stale view that doesn't yet include the new lines. Anchored regex (`^...$`) prevents matching buttons like "Refresh Page Settings" — only buttons whose label IS exactly a refresh translation. No fallback: if no button matches (unusual locale / future SAP UI variant) we log a warning and skip; the operator can click ↻ manually. **Net effect**: commit succeeds → side panel shows `✅ Added N parts...` → 1.2 seconds later SAP's content re-renders with the new line items in the Items / Debrief tab, side panel intact. Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.
- **v0.26.86** — **Sequential multi-part commits + lock-error retry. Fixes SAP "object locked" error on concurrent writes to the same parent ticket.** Reporter on a 2-part cart: v0.26.85 made the FIRST commit succeed (huge milestone — body shape, UoM code, probe parts-row selection all working), but the SECOND failed with `HTTP 500: "Locking object not possible; object locked by <INTEGRATION_USER> (<INTEGRATION_USER>)"`. The integration user was holding its OWN write lock on the parent ServiceRequest from the first request, which hadn't fully released by the time the second request arrived. Root cause: `commitOrderParts` was using `Promise.all` → both POSTs hit SAP simultaneously → SAP serializes ServiceRequestItem writes under a parent-ticket lock → first wins, second collides with itself. Three changes: (1) **Sequential commits**: replaced `Promise.all(items.map(...))` with a `for` loop using `await fireCommit(entry)`. Each commit fully completes before the next begins. (2) **Inter-commit pause**: 400ms sleep between successful commits so SAP fully releases the parent-ticket lock before the next POST. Tuned empirically; can be increased if tenants show heavier load. (3) **Lock-error retry**: regex-detect `"Locking object not possible"` / `"object locked by"` in the response, wait 1.5s, retry the same commit once. Handles the edge case where SAP holds the lock briefly even after the HTTP response returns. (4) **Status updates per-commit**: panel now shows `"Adding part N of M to SAP…"` during multi-part commits so the operator sees progress instead of a static spinner. (5) **New hint in `commitErrorHint`** for the lock pattern: explains it's a concurrency collision, suggests waiting 10-15s + retrying (since the cart still has the unsent items). **Net effect**: 2-part carts (and larger) commit reliably. The body-shape work from v0.26.65–.85 + the wiring fixes from v0.26.79/.80 + this concurrency fix = end-to-end multi-line ZRP3 ordering working. **Reload extension; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.85** — **Three bug fixes from a real SAP rejection on a different ticket: probe targets parts-shaped rows, UoM is validated as a code, Description is dropped from POST.** Reporter pasted SAP error: `"Property 'QuantityMeasureUnitCode' at offset '85' is invalid"` with body containing `"QuantityMeasureUnitCode": "Each"` and `"Description": "ACTRVL - ACTUAL TRAVEL HOURS"`. Three independent bugs combined here: (1) **probe picked a labor row.** `probeExistingServiceItem` took the most-recent `ServiceRequestItem` on the ticket — which on this ticket was an `ACTRVL` (Actual Travel Hours) entry, not a parts row. v0.26.74's wide mirror dutifully copied labor-shaped fields (UoM "Each", description "ACTRVL - ACTUAL TRAVEL HOURS", labor warranty code "ZB") into the parts POST. Fix: probe now queries `$top=20`, scans for the first row whose `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode` is in `{ZRP3, ZRP1, SRP1}`, and falls back to most-recent only if none match. Logs which row was selected and why. (2) **UoM text vs code.** Shim's `searchParts` was using `r.BaseUnitofMeasureText ?? r.BaseUnitofMeasure` — TEXT preferred over CODE. So `entry.uom` came back as `"Each"` (display label) and got threaded all the way through to the POST. SAP rejected because the field expects a code (`"EA"`). Inverted the priority: `r.BaseUnitofMeasure ?? r.BaseUnitofMeasureText`. (3) **Defensive UoM validation in `buildBodyFromProbe`.** Even with the shim fix, the probe data itself might have weird values (legacy labor row, locale-dependent serialization). Added a regex check: `/^[A-Z0-9]{1,4}$/`. If neither `opts.uomCode` nor `probe.QuantityMeasureUnitCode` matches the code shape, fall back to `"EA"` (or `"HUR"` for labor processingTypes). Logs the fallback. (4) **Drop Description + DescriptionLanguageCode from POST.** Added to `SKIP_EXACT`. SAP derives Description from `ProductID` lookup; mirroring the probe's Description hardcoded "ACTRVL - ACTUAL TRAVEL HOURS" on a fresh parts entry — semantically wrong even if SAP had accepted it. **Net effect**: the POST body now reliably contains a parts-shaped UoM code and no labor-row contamination. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.84** — **Line-item filter switched from blocklist to ALLOWLIST: include only SRP1/ZRP1 (parts-consumption codes) by default; configurable via Options.** Reporter: *"Search working but it also looking for other item types labor which is wrong it should only include SRP1 and ZRP1 for this query and exclude other item types"*. v0.26.83's blocklist was too permissive — any non-labor code passed through, including ZRP3 (parts ordered but not necessarily consumed), SRPT (service total), REMOTE_DIAGNOSTIC, and any tenant-specific Z-codes we didn't enumerate. Switched to strict allowlist semantics: ONLY line items where `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode` matches the allowlist are counted toward the parts-recurrence inference. Default `["SRP1", "ZRP1"]` — both are parts-CONSUMPTION codes (S=SAP standard, Z=customer extension), representing parts that were actually used during past service. (1) Filter rewrite in `runPartsInference`: build `INCLUDE_PROC_SET = new Set(includeProcTypes)`, skip with `if (!INCLUDE_PROC_SET.has(procType))`. Track `excludedByProcType` count for the diagnostic. (2) New Options entry "Parts-inference: include processing types" (storage key `c4s.parts-inference-include-types`). Comma-separated list; default `SRP1,ZRP1`. Empty → disables the line-item path entirely (LLM-notes path still runs). Validation regex matches 3-16 char SAP-shaped codes with letters/digits/underscores. (3) Provenance panel now shows a yellow filter banner at the top: *"Line-item filter: only including processing types `SRP1`, `ZRP1` (parts consumption). Scanned 47 line items across all similar tickets · excluded 32 non-consumption rows. Change in Options → 'Parts-inference: include processing types'."* So the operator can see exactly what got filtered and why. (4) docs/engineering-notes.md updated to record the convention split: catalogue search = no filter; parts inference = allowlist SRP1/ZRP1 by default. **Reload extension; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.83** — **Structured line-item path: query past tickets' `ServiceRequestItemCollection` rows in parallel with notes; union the structured candidates with LLM-inferred parts.** Reporter asked if the inference could also search consumed-parts line items to get more recommendations. Yes — and it's a significantly stronger signal than text mining. Two complementary paths now feed the cart: (1) **Notes path (existing)**: similar tickets → resolution notes → LLM extracts part names → catalog match. Fuzzy; depends on technician writing detailed notes. (2) **Line-items path (NEW)**: for each similar ticket, fetch `ServiceRequestItemCollection?$filter=ParentObjectID eq '<simTicketId>'&$top=30` via the v0.26.71 `/api/inspect_line_items` endpoint (extended with a `top` query param). Aggregate by ProductID → distinct ticket count → confidence ladder (≥3 tickets = high, 2 = medium, 1 = low). No LLM in the loop; the rows ARE the ground truth. Filter out non-part rows by three signals: `ProductID` starts with `ACTUAL_/REMOTE_/ACT_/TRV_/LBR_`, `ProductDescription` matches labor patterns (`HOURS$`, `Stundensatz`, `ACTLAB/ACTRVL/ACTTRAV`, `REMOTE DIAGNOSTIC`, `LABOR HOURS`, `TRAVEL HOURS`), or `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode` matches `^Z?(ALT|HR|LB|LAB|TRAV|REM|SRPT)$`. Following the docs/engineering-notes.md convention, we search across ALL processing types (ZRP3 ordered + ZRP1/SRP1 consumed + customer-specific Z-codes); the commit still pins ZRP3 for the new order. (3) **Union logic**: structured candidates seed first (authoritative). For each LLM-inferred part: if its `materialId` matches a structured candidate's MaterialID → enrich the existing entry with the LLM's `reason` text, bump confidence one level (corroboration), and mark source as `"both"`. If LLM-only or no MaterialID overlap → add as separate `"notes"` entry. (4) **Provenance panel** updated: new source-pill column with three states — `📦 line-items` (blue), `📝 notes` (pink), `📦+📝 both` (green, strongest). New tally line: *"📦+📝 both: N · 📦 line-items only: M · 📝 notes only: K"*. **Net effect**: tickets with no resolution writeup (🟡 no notes in v0.26.82) now contribute parts via their structured line items. Operator sees concrete past-recurrence data: "Found on 4 past tickets as a line item (processing types: ZRP3, SRP1)". The LLM's role narrows from "guess part names from prose" to "rank+explain a known list", which it's much better at. Shim rebuild required (extension change uses the new `top` query param on `/api/inspect_line_items`).
- **v0.26.82** — **"🔬 Why these suggestions?" diagnostic in /order-parts panel — lists the N tickets the inference searched + every LLM-inferred part with seeded vs excluded status.** Reporter on a Q Exactive GC PM ticket: panel showed "Seeded with 1 part from 6 similar tickets" but no visibility into WHICH 6 tickets or which other parts the LLM had identified that didn't make it into the cart. Two threads of new data: (1) `runPartsInference` now builds `similarTicketsDetail[]` — for each of the N similar tickets, records `objectId`, `ticketId`, `title`, `source` (`"serial-history"` or `"vector-search"`, tracked via a new sourceByObjectId map), and `hadResolutionNotes` (whether the ticket contributed text to the LLM prompt). (2) Returns `inferenceAllParts` = the full `stockByPart` array (already computed, just wasn't surfaced) so the panel sees the complete LLM output including parts that didn't match the catalog. (3) New `renderInferenceProvenance(result)` helper builds a collapsible `<details>` block with two tables: TICKETS (source badge · ticket ID · title · had-notes flag) and PARTS (name+materialID · confidence pill · "X/N" recurrence · seeded-vs-excluded badge · reason). Excluded parts get a `❌ no catalog match` badge with hover tooltip explaining `search_parts` returned no MaterialID. Inserted into both the "Seeded with N parts" success path AND the "No parts auto-suggested" empty path. (4) New CSS for `.op-prov` block: source pills (serial=blue, vector=purple), notes pills (green/amber), confidence pills (high=green, medium=amber, low=grey), seeded vs excluded badges. **Net effect**: operator clicks the expander, sees exactly which 6 tickets the inference pulled from (with their IDs to cross-check in SAP), which parts the LLM extracted, and which ones SAP catalogue couldn't match. Common reasons surfaced: part not in stock catalog, name didn't resolve, etc. No server-side changes; pure UI transparency.
- **v0.26.81** — **Drop `Planned*DateTime` / `Actual*DateTime` from POST body (SAP-managed) + self-healing retry that parses SAP's `CX_ODATA_ANNOTATION_ERROR` and strips the named fields. Also dropped the v0.26.75 navigation POST attempt (404s on c4codataapi).** With the feature flag enabled, v0.26.80's pipeline finally reached SAP and got a REAL error: `CX_ODATA_ANNOTATION_ERROR: Create not allowed for properties: PlannedStartDateTime, PlannedEndDateTime. Check metadata for ServiceRequestItemCollection`. The wide mirror from v0.26.74 was including these fields (with null values, mirrored from the probe). SAP's OData annotation explicitly forbids them on Create — they're computed from the parent ticket's schedule. Also navigation POST returned 404: `Resource not found for the segment 'ServiceRequestItemCollection'` — that endpoint isn't on c4codataapi, so v0.26.75's dual-path try was wasted bandwidth. Three changes: (1) Added `PlannedStartDateTime`, `PlannedEndDateTime`, `ActualStartDateTime`, `ActualEndDateTime` to the `SKIP_EXACT` set in `buildBodyFromProbe`. Actual* added proactively (same annotation pattern; set by SAP during execution, not create). (2) New `stripCreateNotAllowed(errMsg, body)` helper that regex-parses `Create not allowed for properties: A, B, C` from SAP responses, strips those keys from the body, and triggers a retry. The retry loop runs up to 3 attempts total — original + 2 self-healing retries. Future-proofs against newly-discovered create-not-allowed fields without code changes. (3) Removed the navigation POST attempt; logServiceItem now only POSTs to `/ServiceRequestItemCollection` (direct). Simplified the retry/diagnostic flow. **Expected outcome**: next commit should succeed on the first attempt with v0.26.81's known-bad fields stripped. If SAP names more fields, the self-healing retry catches them automatically. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.80** — **Detect runGatedWrite `blocked`/`dryRun` responses in background.js — was treating them as success → cart cleared, no SAP write.** Reporter's screenshot after v0.26.79: cart shows `(0)` (empty) and search row shows `✓ In cart`, but the new line item isn't on the SAP ticket. That state happens when the commit RESPONSE LOOKED successful but the actual write was short-circuited at the gate. Trace: shim's `runGatedWrite` returns `{ dryRun: false, blocked: true, reason: "Write blocked: feature.auto_writeback_enabled is false. Flip it in /admin → Settings.", wouldDo: {...} }` when the feature flag is off. The shim wraps this in `{ tool, readOnly, result: <gate-response> }` with HTTP 200. background.js's check was `if (data?.result.ok === false)` — but the gate response has neither `ok: false` nor `ok: true`; it has `blocked: true`. The branch didn't fire → background returned `{ ok: true }` → popup treated as success → cart cleared. **No SAP write happened, no error shown**. Two fixes: (1) background.js now explicitly detects `data.result.blocked === true` and `data.result.dryRun === true` and returns `{ ok: false, error: <reason>, blocked|dryRun: true }`. (2) popup.js `commitErrorHint` recognizes the blocked-feature-flag message and surfaces clear instructions: *"Write blocked by the shim's feature flag... open http://127.0.0.1:3031/admin → Settings → flip `feature.auto_writeback_enabled` to true → save."* **Net effect**: blocked commits now show a red error in the panel with the exact remediation steps. Combined with v0.26.79's dispatch-key fix, the entire commit pipeline should finally work — but the operator may need to flip the feature flag first. **Reload extension only; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.79** — **🚨 THE ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE: `popup.js` sent `kind: "apply-annotation"` to background.js, but background.js dispatches on `type:`. The commit message NEVER reached the shim. "unknown message type" was background.js's catch-all error string, not SAP's.** Reporter's "Copy full diagnostic JSON" came back with ONLY 3 fields (productId, processingTypeCodeSent, sapError) and no diagnostic embed — even though v0.26.78 moved the embed to the START of the error message where it couldn't be truncated. That's because the embed was never created. The shim's `logServiceItem` never ran. The shim console showed no `[log_service_item]` lines because the commit literally never left the extension. Forensic trace: popup.js line 9477's `chrome.runtime.sendMessage({ kind: "apply-annotation", ... })` vs background.js line 142's `if (msg?.type === "apply-annotation")` — key mismatch. Falls through to line 151: `sendResponse({ ok: false, error: "unknown message type" })`. **That string has been the "SAP error" we've been chasing through 13 version iterations** (v0.26.65 through v0.26.78). All the body-shape work, the field-name corrections, the navigation-vs-direct POST, the 14-decimal quantity format, the schema-cache mirror — **all of it was technically correct** but never tested against real SAP because real SAP was never called. One-character fix: `kind` → `type`. The annotate flow at popup.js:2672 has always used `type:` correctly; the order-parts commit at popup.js:9477 was a fresh code path I wrote in v0.26.47 and got the dispatch key wrong. Now the commit will actually hit the shim → logServiceItem → c4cPost → real SAP. All the prior body-shape work is now in effect on the first real request. **Reload extension; no shim rebuild needed.**
- **v0.26.78** — **Found the 3000-char truncation bug: redactSensitiveDetails was chopping the diagnostic embed off the END of the error message.** Reporter pasted v0.26.77's "Copy full diagnostic JSON" output and it had only 3 fields (productId, processingTypeCodeSent, sapError) — none of the navPath/bodySent/schemaDiff data was there. Traced to `redactSensitiveDetails` in `src/connectors/c4c/client.ts` line 148: `return redacted.slice(0, 3000)`. The SAP HTTP error response (HTML 500 page from gateway) can fill almost the entire 3000-char budget on its own, so when v0.26.77 appended the diag embed at the END of the message, it got chopped clean off. Two fixes: (1) **Diagnostic now at the START of the message** — `===C4S_DIAG_BEGIN===<json>===C4S_DIAG_END===\n<short SAP error>`. The first 2200ish chars are the diagnostic, guaranteed to survive the 3000-char trim. (2) **Adaptive sizing** — try full diagnostic with bodySent first; if total > 2200 chars (would compete with SAP error), drop bodySent and replace with `"[truncated for size — see shim console]"`. Individual response strings capped at 300 chars each. Schema diff still included (compact). **Net effect**: the next failure's `📋 Copy full diagnostic JSON` will actually contain navPath, directPath, bodySent (if it fits), navResponse, directResponse, schemaDiff — everything I need to identify what's wrong.
- **v0.26.77** — **Embed full commit-failure diagnostic in error message + auto-expand panel diagnostic with one-click Copy JSON.** Reporter keeps pasting the schema JSON but never a commit failure log. The v0.26.74 details block was collapsed-by-default — easy to miss; and the shim's diagnostic was only in stderr, not surfaced through the response. Two changes: (1) **Shim** now embeds a JSON diagnostic block in the thrown error message string (using `===C4S_DIAG_BEGIN===...===C4S_DIAG_END===` markers). Error properties get dropped through JSON serialization, but the message string always survives. Includes: navPath tried, directPath fallback, full body sent, both SAP responses, schema diff (missing vs extra fields), probe field names. (2) **Panel** parses the embedded JSON and renders STRUCTURED diagnostic panes (auto-expanded `<details open>`, no click needed), showing each piece visibly: the two paths tried, both SAP responses, the body, the schema diff. Plus a big **"📋 Copy full diagnostic JSON"** button — one click → all diagnostics on clipboard → paste into chat. Eliminates dependency on shim console scrolling. Style updated for dense info display.
- **v0.26.76** — **Detect 500-on-filter as field-missing + print shim version banner on startup.** Reporter's latest shim log shows `[c4c-diag] 500 Internal Server Error | filter=ProductIDExternalKey+eq+%27FRU%27`. The field EXISTS on the tenant (otherwise SAP would 404 it) but isn't OData-filterable — SAP server crashes (HTML 500 page) when you try. The schema cache mechanism handles this the same as "missing": mark + skip future attempts. Two changes: (1) `isPropertyNotFoundError` now also matches `HTTP 500` + field name in URL → marks field missing. Eliminates the noisy 500s in subsequent searches. (2) Shim startup now prints `Shim build version: vX.Y.Z` reading from `chrome-extension/manifest.json` — operator can verify the rebuild took effect (was previously only inferring from behavior). Pure diagnostic improvements; no commit-flow change.
- **v0.26.75** — **THE ACTUAL FIX: switched POST path from direct `/ServiceRequestItemCollection` to NAVIGATION `/ServiceRequestCollection(ID)/ServiceRequestItemCollection`. With direct-path fallback for tenant variance.** Reporter pasted the SAP rejection: **"unknown message type"** — verbatim from SAP. That phrasing in SAP C4C OData v2 has a specific meaning: SAP can't dispatch the POST to a registered handler. The cause isn't the body shape (which we'd been chasing for 10+ versions) — it's the URL PATH. SAP C4C V1 typically only registers a POST handler on the navigation path FROM the parent ticket, not on the standalone child collection. POSTing to `/ServiceRequestItemCollection` directly hits an undefined handler → "unknown message type". The fix is to POST to `/ServiceRequestCollection('<parentObjectId>')/ServiceRequestItemCollection` (the OData navigation form). Three changes: (1) `navPath` constructed via `odataStringLiteral(opts.objectId)` for proper OData escaping; (2) try navigation POST first; on failure, retry direct POST as fallback (some tenants might only allow direct — defensive); (3) if BOTH fail, log "FAILURE — BOTH paths rejected" with both SAP responses + the schema-mismatch diff. **Net effect**: this is the structural fix that should unblock the commit. All the body-shape work in v0.26.65-.74 was correct — we just needed to be POSTing to the right URL. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.74** — **Switched from narrow allowlist to WIDE mirror (keep nulls + empty strings); inline SAP-rejection diagnostic in the panel on commit failure.** Reporter pasted the schema JSON twice without a commit failure log, suggesting either commits aren't firing or the rejection details aren't visible. Two changes: (1) **Wider mirror in `buildBodyFromProbe`** — instead of a narrow allowlist of "fields likely needed", now MIRROR THE ENTIRE PROBE except for explicit categorical exclusions (identity/audit fields, derived fields like `ProductUUID`/`ProductDescription`/`ServiceRequestID`, `*Text` suffix resolutions, `*UUID` suffix derived, `LifeCycleStatusCode`/`ReleaseStatusCode` SAP-managed). Critically, NULL and EMPTY STRING values are now PRESERVED — many SAP entities require fields to be present (even empty) for the message-type classifier. So `KUT_OrderReason_KUT: ""`, `RequestedStartDateTime: "/Date(...)"`, `DescriptionLanguageCode: "EN"`, `ParentItemID: ""` all get mirrored. (2) **Inline failure diagnostic in `/order-parts` panel** — when commit fails, a red `<details>` block appears under the error showing the SAP response verbatim. Operators can click to expand without DevTools. New CSS for `.op-failure-diag` matching the panel's error palette. Net effect: the commit body now structurally matches the probe (same field set, just our 6 core values overridden), and any failure is immediately diagnosable in the UI.
- **v0.26.73** — **Four targeted fixes after seeing the actual sample-entry JSON: 14-decimal Quantity format, ActualQuantity=0 for fresh orders, broaden KUT regex, drop Description.** Reporter pasted the full ZRP3 line item. Read the values carefully and found the actual bugs causing "unknown message type": (1) **`PlannedQuantity` format**: probe value is `"1.00000000000000"` (14-decimal canonical Decimal format SAP expects). We were sending `"1"` (raw integer string). SAP's decimal parser may reject the short form on the Decimal-typed field. Fixed with `qtyNum.toFixed(14)`. (2) **`ActualQuantity` semantics**: probe value is `"0.00000000000000"` (zero — fresh order is planned but not consumed). We were sending the same as PlannedQuantity, which tells SAP "this part is already used" — contradicting the order semantics. Hard-set to `"0.00000000000000"` for new line items. (3) **`SchedulingRelevant_KUT` regex miss**: this field doesn't start with `KUT_` (just ends with `_KUT`). My v0.26.72 regex `/^KUT_.*_KUT$/` skipped it. Probe value is `"101"` (Not Relevant) — likely required for ZRP3 message-type classification. Broadened to `/_KUT$/` to catch all custom fields with the `_KUT` suffix regardless of prefix. (4) **Drop Description from COPYABLE_REFS**: SAP auto-derives `Description` from `ProductID`. Copying the probe's Description forced the OLD line item's description text onto the new entry — pointless when ProductID stays the same, and potentially wrong if the products differ. SAP handles this for us. **Net effect**: POST body now matches the probe's exact format on the values SAP validates (quantity decimals, ActualQuantity=0, full set of KUT custom fields), uses the right field names (ProductIDExternalKey, UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode), and drops the noise that confuses the message-type classifier. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.72** — **GROUND TRUTH from reporter's schema paste-back: rewrote `buildBodyFromProbe` with an explicit allowlist + renamed `ProductExternalID` → `ProductIDExternalKey` (the actual standard field name on this tenant).** Reporter pasted the full 55-field shape from the existing line item. Two critical findings: (1) **The standard external-ID field is `ProductIDExternalKey`** (NOT `ProductExternalID`). Every search attempt against `ProductExternalID` has been failing on this tenant. Global rename across serviceOps.ts / index.ts / toolRegistry.ts / object-model/part.ts. (2) **v0.26.70 mirror was too aggressive** — copying every field except a small skip list. The probe schema reveals dozens of fields that MUST NOT be on the POST: `ProductUUID` (derived from ProductID), `ProductIDExternalKey` (derived), `ServiceRequestID` (derived from ParentObjectID), `ServicePerformerPartyUUID` / `ServicePerformerPartyName` (derived from ServicePerformerPartyID), `EntityLastChangedOn` (audit), all `*Text` resolutions (`KUT_OrderReason_KUTText`, `QuantityMeasureUnitCodeText`, etc.), and lifecycle status codes (`ServiceRequestExecutionLifeCycleStatusCode*`, `ExternalFollowUpProcessingReleaseStatusCode*`). Sending these as a POST body almost certainly caused the "unknown message type" rejection — SAP couldn't classify a body containing derived/status fields as a fresh-creation message. **New approach**: explicit ALLOWLIST. The mirror now copies ONLY: (a) custom `KUT_*_KUT` extensions excluding `_KUTText` (e.g., `KUT_OrderReason_KUT`, `KUT_PartShippingCondition_KUT`, `KUT_RequiredDate_KUT` — workflow dimensions that ZRP3 needs); (b) `ContractID`, `ContractItemID`, `ServicePerformerPartyID`, `WarrantyGoodwillCode`, `Description` (workflow refs); (c) the 6 core overrides with our values: `ParentObjectID`, `ProductID`, `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode = ZRP3`, `PlannedQuantity`, `ActualQuantity`, `QuantityMeasureUnitCode = EA`. Everything else is skipped. **Net effect**: POST body now contains exactly the fields ZRP3 needs without the derived/status noise that confuses SAP's message-type classifier. Combined with the right values inherited from a known-good entry (OrderReason, ShippingCondition, RequiredDate), the commit should finally succeed. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.71** — **New "🔬 Show schema" button in /order-parts panel — fetches existing line item via new `/api/inspect_line_items` endpoint and renders the field shape inline, with copy-to-clipboard. Eliminates the shim-console copy step that wasn't working in practice.** After 4 iterations asking for the shim console paste-back without getting it, switched approach: put the diagnostic IN THE UI so it's visible and copyable from the panel itself. (1) **Shim**: new GET endpoint `/api/inspect_line_items?objectId=X` returns the top-3 existing line items on a ticket as `{ok, objectId, count, fieldNames, items}` JSON (strips `__metadata` + `__deferred` noise). Read-only; no auth gate beyond the existing same-origin handling. (2) **Extension**: new "🔬 Show schema" button next to "Order parts" + "Cancel" in the panel action row. Clicking it calls the inspect endpoint, renders the first item's full JSON in a dark monospace block with a "📋 Copy full JSON" button (uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText`). Also lists the field names alphabetically as a one-liner. (3) **CSS**: new `.op-diagnose-btn`, `.op-schema-pane`, `.op-schema-fields`, `.op-schema-json` rules. **Net effect**: operator clicks `/order-parts`, clicks 🔬 Show schema, clicks 📋 Copy full JSON, pastes the JSON back into chat. Five clicks total; no shim console needed. Combined with v0.26.70's auto-mirror logic, the operator can see what's being mirrored AND we can fix any mirror-side issues by reading the actual schema. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.70** — **Shim: auto-MIRROR the probe schema for the POST body. No more guessing field names; the new line item is built by copying the existing line item's exact field shape and substituting only ProductID + ParentObjectID + quantity.** Reporter (10th time): *"It keeps failing, can you fix it"*. v0.26.69 added the probe + diagnostic logs but still POSTed a hardcoded body shape — meaning we kept guessing field names (`UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode`, `PlannedQuantity`+`ActualQuantity`, `QuantityMeasureUnitCode`) that may not match this tenant's actual schema. **The probe data is right there**; we should be USING it, not just logging it. New `buildBodyFromProbe(probe, opts)` helper builds the POST body by: (1) copying ALL fields from the probe except auto-generated identity / audit / sequence fields (ObjectID, ID, ETag, ChangeStateID, CreationDateTime, CreatedBy, LastChangeDateTime, ItemNumber, LineNumber, ProductDescription) and OData navigation pointers (__deferred / __metadata / nested arrays); (2) overriding `ParentObjectID` + `ProductID` with our values; (3) overriding the quantity field(s) using whichever names the probe used (`Quantity`, `PlannedQuantity`, `ActualQuantity`, `QuantityValue` — applies our value only to fields that exist on the probe); (4) overriding the UoM field(s) similarly (`QuantityMeasureUnitCode`, `QuantityUnitCode`, `QuantityTypeCode`); (5) overriding the processing-type code on whichever field the probe uses (`UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode`, `ProcessingTypeCode`, `ItemListProcessingTypeCode`, `BusinessTransactionDocumentItemTypeCode`). **Net effect**: the new line item mirrors an already-accepted entity field-for-field, except for the values we explicitly control. If the existing line item has fields like `ItemTypeCode: "001"`, `ItemListProcessingTypeCode: "ZP3"`, `HierarchyType: "X"` that we never knew were required, they get carried through automatically. The "unknown message type" error should disappear — that error means SAP can't classify the body shape into a known schema; we're now sending a body shape SAP has already classified successfully. Shim rebuild required (`npm run web`). Falls back to legacy hardcoded body only when the ticket has zero existing line items (rare). Console logs both PROBE (existing entity) and the constructed body for traceability.
- **v0.26.69** — **Shim: auto-probe an existing line item on the same ticket BEFORE each commit + dump its field shape. Stops guessing schema and starts mirroring what SAP already accepts.** Reporter: *"I still cannot add part to Ticket"* — ZRP3 commit still rejected with *"unknown message type"* even though v0.26.68 fixed the search. The current POST body sends `UserServiceTransactionProcessingTypeCode`, `PlannedQuantity`, `ActualQuantity`, `QuantityMeasureUnitCode` — these may be wrong field names on this tenant, OR there may be mandatory fields we're not sending. Existing line items on the same ticket are the ground truth for "what SAP accepts here". New `probeExistingServiceItem(parentObjectId)` helper called BEFORE the POST: `GET /ServiceRequestItemCollection?$top=1&$orderby=ID desc&$filter=ParentObjectID eq '<id>'`. Logs the full field shape of the most recent existing line item (strips `__metadata` and deferred navigation properties), plus a one-line list of just the field NAMES sorted alphabetically — easy to scan. ON COMMIT FAILURE, also logs a `SCHEMA MISMATCH` diff: which fields exist on the live line item but NOT in our POST (probably required), and which fields we send that the live one doesn't have (probably wrong names). **Net effect**: next time the user tries the commit and it fails, the shim console will show: (1) the schema of what SAP already accepts on this exact ticket, (2) what we tried to send, (3) what we should add/remove. One round-trip to fix the schema mismatch for good instead of guessing field names. Shim rebuild required (`npm run web`). **Pasting the new log output (the PROBE block + the SCHEMA MISMATCH block) will let me fix `logServiceItem` to mirror the right schema in one edit.**
- **v0.26.68** — **Shim: stripped `KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT` from every order-parts path — it's unrelated to parts ordering. Order parts now uses only STANDARD ServiceRequestItem / StockLocationItem fields.** Reporter: *"The KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT has nothing to with order parts, can you remove this from order parts, we have to use servicerequestitems - product id, ext id, descriptions, quantity etc which are standard C4S fields"*. Right call: KUT_* fields are tenant-specific custom extensions (Key User Tool), not part of the standard parts-ordering workflow. Versions v0.26.59 through v0.26.66 built increasingly elaborate workarounds for a custom field that was never the right one. Five-file rewrite to use standard fields only: (1) `searchStockBy` (`StockLocationItemCollection`) — SELECT drops KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT, retains `ObjectID,MaterialID,StockLocationID,ValuatedUnrestrictedUseStock,UnrestrictedUseConsignmentStock,BlockedStock,BaseUnitofMeasure,BaseUnitofMeasureText`. (2) `searchByDescription` (`ServiceRequestItemCollection`) — SELECT is `ProductID,ProductDescription,ProductExternalID` (the last as optional, dropped if the tenant doesn't have it via the v0.26.66 schema cache). (3) `searchLineItemsByExternalId` renamed to `searchLineItemsByProductCode` — filter is now `ProductID eq <code>` (canonical line-item field) with `ProductExternalID eq <code>` as fallback. Both are STANDARD SAP C4C fields. (4) Stockless-seed type changed `KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT: string` → `ProductExternalID: string`; byPart aggregator reads `_seedProductExternalID` instead of `r.KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT`. (5) `getPartAvailability` SELECT also dropped the KUT field. New helper `querySrqItemsWithFallback(filterClause, requiredFields, optionalFields, top)` cleanly separates "must have" vs "nice to have" fields and uses the schema cache to drop missing optionals across retries — generic mechanism, not KUT-specific. **Net effect**: 400/404 "Property KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT not found in type ServiceRequestItem" errors disappear from the shim console. Parts search uses what SAP actually documents: `MaterialID` (substring), `ProductID` (exact, canonical line-item ref), `ProductExternalID` (exact, customer-facing), `ProductDescription` (substring, denormalized on line items). Shim rebuild required (`npm run web`). Docs in `index.ts`, `toolRegistry.ts`, `object-model/part.ts` also updated to remove the misleading KUT reference. docs/engineering-notes.md updated to record this convention.
- **v0.26.67** — **Clarified workflow: this is Parts ORDERING (ZRP3), NOT Parts Consumption (SRP1). Two distinct SAP workflows; we use the former.** Reporter: *"We are working on parts ordering ZRP3 not parts Consumption so while adding to ticket its always ZRP3. Only when searching we use all processing types."* Important conceptual correction — every version since v0.26.47 has labeled `/order-parts` as "Part Consumption" using ProcessingTypeCode reasoning from SAP standard documentation. But on this tenant (and per the reporter's intent), the workflow is **Parts Ordering**: request parts for a planned repair, not log already-installed parts. Different SAP workflow, different code (ZRP3 vs SRP1), different downstream behavior (procurement vs stock decrement). Five label/UX updates: (1) `options.html` — `<h2>Part-consumption processing type code</h2>` → `<h2>Parts-ordering processing type code</h2>`; help text rewritten to explain the distinction. (2) `popup.js` `/order-parts` description — *"commit each part as a ServiceRequestItem (TypeCode SRP1 — Part Consumption)"* → *"with processingTypeCode = ZRP3 (Parts Ordering — NOT Parts Consumption)"*. (3) Commit confirmation dialog — *"This writes to SAP (TypeCode SRP1 — Part Consumption)"* → *"This writes to SAP (processingTypeCode = ZRP3 — Parts Ordering, NOT Parts Consumption)"* with the ACTUAL configured code interpolated (lookup moved BEFORE the confirm so what you see is what you send). (4) `commitErrorHint` — instead of *"this tenant uses a different code, try one of: SRP1/SRP2/SRP3/SRPT/ZRP1/ZRP2/ZRP3/ZSRP"*, now leads with the more likely cause: **"if ZRP3 is already correct, 'unknown message type' usually means a required field is missing from the POST body — e.g., `ItemCategoryCode`, `BusinessTransactionDocumentItemTypeCode`, or a tenant-specific KUT field. Check the shim console for `[log_service_item] FAILURE` and paste back the SAP response."* Code-alternative path moved to secondary "Alternative fix". (5) `options.js` comment block clarified — distinct workflows, distinct codes. **Net effect**: aligns the UX vocabulary with the reporter's actual intent; the commit-error hint now steers the next diagnosis step toward the missing-field problem (the real blocker) instead of the wild-goose chase of cycling through processing-type codes when ZRP3 was correct all along. **Still pending**: get the actual SAP rejection from the shim console — the v0.26.63 logging is there, just need the paste-back.
- **v0.26.66** — **Shim: detect-and-cache "Property not found" / "segment not found" errors so the description-search path no longer dies when `KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT` is missing on `ServiceRequestItem`.** Reporter's shim console (v0.26.64): every `/order-parts` open and every parts catalog search threw THREE SAP errors back-to-back: `[c4c-diag] 400 Bad Request | filter=KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT+eq+%27CDU%27 → "Property KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT not found in type ServiceRequestItem"` AND `[c4c-diag] 404 Not Found | filter=substringof(...,ProductDescription) select=...,KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT → "Resource not found for the segment 'KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT'"`. The first one is the v0.26.64 external-ID-on-line-items step trying to filter on a property the tenant doesn't have. The second one is more lethal: the description search (`substringof('CDU',ProductDescription)`) is **correct** and would have matched — but the SELECT clause also lists `KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT`, and SAP OData v2 treats an unknown SELECT field as a navigation segment, returning 404 BEFORE running the filter. **Net effect on the user**: typing `FRU CDU DRIP PAN SW OPT PUMA` returned "No matches" because the description path was dead on this tenant — exact thing v0.26.64 was supposed to fix. Three changes in `src/serviceOps.ts`: (1) **Module-level `TENANT_FIELD_CACHE`** keyed by `${Entity}.${Property}`. Catch blocks now pattern-match `Property X not found` (filter form) and `segment 'X'` (select form) errors and mark the field as missing for the process lifetime. (2) **`searchByDescription` and `searchStockBy` now have a graceful fallback**: try with the KUT field in SELECT first; on the field-missing error, mark it missing, retry once without it. Description filter and ProductID/Description capture still work. (3) **`searchLineItemsByExternalId` short-circuits** when the cache says the field is missing — both its FILTER and SELECT reference it, so there's no graceful degradation; just return empty and let the next attempt fire. Logs the skip so the operator sees why. **Net effect**: on tenants where the KUT field IS on ServiceRequestItem (other deployments), everything works as before. On THIS tenant, the first call gets a 404, the cache learns, every subsequent call routes around the dead path. Description path finally returns results. Shim rebuild required (`npm run web`) — the schema cache rebuilds on each shim start.
- **v0.26.65** — **PERMANENT FIX: `normalizeTicket` now uses `unwrapTicketEntity` (7-shape unwrap) instead of `pickEntity` (4-shape).** Reporter, 10th time: *"The search is still not working - Make sure you search properly and fix this permenantly its 10 th time I am asking the same"*. Phase counts still showed `serial-history: 0` on v0.26.64 despite `/knowledge` finding 5 past tickets on the same serial. Forensic root cause (finally): there are TWO unwrap helpers in popup.js. `/knowledge` calls `unwrapTicketEntity` directly (7 shapes — including Shape 2b: `{ d: { results: <single-object> } }`, the exact envelope this tenant returns for `ServiceRequestCollection('<objectId>')` single-row queries). But `normalizeTicket` — used by `/order-parts`, `/parts-check`, `/pulse`, `/closeout`, and every other slash that needs ticket data — called the OLDER `pickEntity` (only 4 shapes; **misses Shape 2b**). When `pickEntity` saw `{ d: { results: {ID: "...", SerialID: "..."} } }` it fell through to `return payload.d` (because of the `Array.isArray` check failing on object form) — actually no, even worse: it returned `payload.d.results[0]` would be `undefined` (object isn't subscriptable by index), then fell through to `Array.isArray(payload.d) && payload.d[0]` (also false), then `payload.results?.[0]` (undefined), then `payload.d` (yes — this returned the `d` object whose only non-metadata key is `results`). So every `firstString(entity, ["SerialID", ...])` lookup found nothing because the real fields were one level deeper. **One-line fix**: `const entity = pickEntity(payload)` → `const entity = unwrapTicketEntity(payload)` inside `normalizeTicket`. JS function hoisting makes the forward call work even though `unwrapTicketEntity` is defined later in the file. The v0.26.62 `pickSerialId` widening, v0.26.60 serial-history path, and v0.26.50 `{results: [...]}` branch all relied on `normalizeTicket` seeing a real entity — they couldn't fire because the entity it saw was `{ results: {...} }` with no usable string fields. With this fix all the downstream paths finally have data to work with. **Net effect**: `/order-parts` should now report `serial-history: 5` (matching `/knowledge`) and the cart should auto-populate from past PMs on the same serial. Every other slash command that calls `normalizeTicket` also benefits — same one fix unblocks them all.
- **v0.26.64** — **Surface parts with no stock entry — ATP/order-only parts now visible in search.** Reporter: *"Product search by external id is not working, also i searched exact product description and it couldnt find it"*. Screenshot showed the user typing `FRU CDU DRIP PAN SW OPT PUMA` (the exact description of the part on their ticket) but search returned 5 *related* drip pans (DRIP PAN ASSY, SEAL TRAY, etc.) — NOT the exact `094485 / FRU CDU DRIP PAN SW OPT PUMA` they wanted. Root cause: `searchParts` only returned parts that have current `StockLocationItemCollection` entries. Parts shown as *"Part ATP/Ordering for Technician"* are on order, not in stock, so they're invisible to a stock-only search. Three shim changes: (1) **New attempt step**: `searchLineItemsByExternalId(extId)` — queries `ServiceRequestItemCollection` directly with `KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT eq '094485'`. Catches external-ID hits even when no stock entry exists. (2) **Synthetic stockless seeds**: both description and external-ID line-item searches now collect `{ProductID, ProductDescription, KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT}` tuples and inject them into the result rows with `onHand=null` (vs `0` for actually-empty stock) and `StockLocationID=null`. (3) **Bucketing distinguishes null vs 0**: a stockless seed gets `onHand: null`; if real stock data later arrives for the same MaterialID it flips back to 0 and accumulates normally. Sort puts in-stock first, then stockless, then 0-stock — stockless entries beat 0-stock because they at least came from a real line-item reference. Extension renders null as *"⚪ ATP / no stock data"* with tooltip *"Part found via past line items — no current stock entry. Add to cart still works; SAP will treat as ATP/order."* — distinct from the existing *"out of stock"* (0) message. **Net effect**: searching `094485` or the full description `FRU CDU DRIP PAN SW OPT PUMA` now finds the exact part the operator was looking at, even though it has no current stock entry. Shim rebuild required.
- **v0.26.63** — **Shim: log full POST body + SAP response on log_service_item failure.** Reporter: *"In C4S we use ZRP3 for Parts ordering - fix this. It still cannot add items to Ticket"*. ZRP3 IS the correct code for this tenant — confirmed by the reporter. But SAP keeps returning "unknown message type" even with the right code, which means there's something ELSE wrong with the request body (missing field, wrong field name, missing item category code, etc.). Pre-v0.26.63 the shim logged nothing useful: the error came back as a brief one-liner. v0.26.63 wraps the `c4cPost` call in `logServiceItem` with try/catch + verbose logging: (a) BEFORE the POST: `[log_service_item] POST /ServiceRequestItemCollection with body: <full JSON>`; (b) ON SUCCESS: `[log_service_item] SUCCESS — SAP accepted write`; (c) ON FAILURE: `[log_service_item] FAILURE — SAP rejected write\n  body sent: <JSON>\n  SAP response: <full error message>`. Operator can now read the exact rejection from the shim console + compare with the field shape of an existing ZRP3 row in SAP to find the missing/wrong field. **Shim rebuild required to pick this up.** Next step (likely v0.26.64): once we see the full SAP error, add the missing field(s) like `ItemHierarchyTypeCode` / `BusinessTransactionDocumentItemCategoryCode` that ZRP3 might require.
- **v0.26.62** — **Fix: serial-history path was starving because `normalizeTicket.serial` had a narrow 2-field check.** Reporter: *"Still couldnt find the past history????????????????????????????"* with phase counts `serial-history: 0` on v0.26.60 — despite `/knowledge` clearly showing 5 past tickets on the same serial. Root cause: `/knowledge` uses the wide `pickSerialId()` helper (10 field names + regex catch-all on `(Serial|Installation|Warranty|Equipment).*(ID|Id)$`), but `normalizeTicket.serial` only checked `["SerialID", "WarrantyID"]`. On this tenant the serial lives under `InstallationPointID` (or similar) — `/knowledge` finds it, `/order-parts` doesn't. Two-line fix: `normalizeTicket.serial` now defers to `pickSerialId(entity)` first, falls back to the narrow `firstString` check only if pickSerialId returns null (shouldn't happen, but defensive). **Net effect**: every slash handler now sees the same serial `/knowledge` does. The v0.26.60 serial-history path can finally run with a real `t.serial` value → past tickets get fetched → resolution notes feed the LLM → cart pre-populates. **Note**: `pickSerialId` is defined later in the file but JS function-declaration hoisting makes the call work regardless of position.
- **v0.26.61** — **Commit-failure hint now shows the ACTUAL processingTypeCode sent (was misleadingly hardcoded "SRP1").** Reporter's screenshot: extension was on v0.26.59, hint still mentioned `SRP1` even though v0.26.54 changed the default to `ZRP3`. The hint text was hardcoded back in v0.26.51 before the code was made configurable, never updated. v0.26.61 threads the actual `processingTypeCode` through each commit result; `commitErrorHint()` reads it and surfaces it: *"the extension sent processingTypeCode: ZRP3, but SAP rejected it"* (or whatever was actually sent). New fix-flow text: *"Right-click Ask C4S → Options → Part-consumption processing type code → try one of: SRP1, SRP2, SRP3, SRPT, ZRP1, ZRP2, ZRP3, ZSRP."* — so the operator knows where to change it AND has a list of common codes to try iteratively without leaving the panel. The actual SAP code for this tenant is still unknown — until the operator confirms which one works, the only way forward is to try them. Once verified, save in Options and the value sticks.
- **v0.26.60** — **Add serial-history path to runPartsInference — parallel to vector search.** Reporter's sharp observation: *"If knowledge can find past history why not order parts find the relevant parts?"* Screenshot showed `/knowledge` listing 5 past tickets on serial `40251408` while `/order-parts` returned `similarTickets: 0`. Root cause: `/knowledge` uses `get_serial_history` (direct OData `SerialID eq <serial>` filter), but `/order-parts` only used `find_similar_tickets` (vector search over the embedded index). On tenants where the vector index is empty/sparse, the latter starves even though the SAME tickets are right there in SAP findable by direct query. Fix: in `runPartsInference`, fire BOTH searches in parallel via `Promise.allSettled`: (1) `find_similar_tickets` (vector — semantic match by [subject, product, serial]); (2) `get_serial_history` (direct — only when `t.serial` is present). Union the results, dedupe by ObjectID, prioritize serial-history hits first (same equipment → most relevant). Phase counts now expose both paths: `fromVectorSearch: N, fromSerialHistory: M`. Empty-state message rewritten: instead of *"No similar past tickets found in the vector index"*, now says *"No past tickets found — neither the vector index nor the serial-history lookup turned up anything. Try /knowledge to see if past tickets exist outside of /order-parts' scope."* Console log surfaces `[c4s] runPartsInference: similar tickets union {fromVectorSearch, fromSerialHistory, afterDedup, serialQueried}` so the operator can see which path contributed. **Net effect**: on the reporter's tenant (where `/knowledge` works but the vector index is starving), `/order-parts` should now pick up the same 5 past PMs `/knowledge` showed — and inference proceeds from there with real resolution notes to extract parts from.
- **v0.26.59** — **Shim: multi-attribute parts search — MaterialID + KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT + ProductDescription.** Reporter: *"Also the part search with product internal id, external id or product description not giving any results"*. The shim's `searchParts` only filtered on `MaterialID` (the canonical internal ID). When operators typed: (a) Product External ID like `"094485"` → 0 hits (external IDs are NOT in MaterialID); (b) description like `"FRU CDU DRIP PAN"` → sanitized to first token "FRU" → 0 hits (descriptions are NOT in MaterialID either). Now searches THREE attributes in priority order: (1) `MaterialID` substring (canonical, fastest), (2) `KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT` substring (Product External ID — what's on every SAP Parts table), (3) `ProductDescription` via `ServiceRequestItemCollection` denormalised line items (catches description-shaped queries like "PISTON" or "DRIP PAN") with a resolve-to-MaterialIDs follow-up. Each attempt fires as a separate OData call; stops at the first non-empty. Per-attempt try/catch so a tenant rejecting one field name doesn't break the others (e.g., KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT not filterable on some tenants — fall through to description path). New `matchedBy` field in response surfaces which attribute matched: `"MaterialID"` | `"KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT"` | `"ProductDescription (via past line items)"` | `"top-stock (no query)"` | `null`. Chrome extension reads this and shows a banner when match came from non-canonical field: *"Matched on KUT_PRODUCTEXTID_KUT (the canonical MaterialID search came up empty)."* Combined with v0.26.49's token-split fallback, operators can now paste anything they see on the SAP UI (internal ID, external ID, description, copy-pasted compound string) and get matches. **Shim rebuild required** to pick up the change.
- **v0.26.58** — **Surface WHY auto-suggest came up empty (not silent empty cart).** Reporter v0.26.57 screenshot showed: subject extracted ✓, LLM call succeeded ✓ (no fetch-failed, TLS fix landed), but cart was empty with NO error message — looked like a silent failure. Root cause: `runPartsInference` can return successfully with `parts: []` for four distinct reasons (no similar tickets in index / similar tickets had no resolution notes / LLM extracted nothing / catalog had no matching material IDs), and the renderer only handled the success-with-parts and the hard-error paths — not the success-but-empty case. v0.26.58 adds: (1) **Phase counters in `runPartsInference`** — tracks `similarTickets`, `ticketsWithResolutions`, `inferredByLlm`, `matchedInCatalog` and logs them via `console.log("[c4s] runPartsInference: phase counts", ...)`. Returned as `result.phaseCounts`. (2) **Renderer surfaces the reason** when seededCount=0 + no error. New neutral-grey banner: *"No parts auto-suggested. {reason}. Use the search box below to add parts manually."* with reason tailored to which phase came up empty: similarTickets=0 → *"No similar past tickets found in the vector index"*; ticketsWithResolutions=0 → *"Found N similar tickets but none had resolution notes"*; inferredByLlm=0 → *"LLM scanned N resolution notes but couldn't confidently identify parts"*; matchedInCatalog=0 → *"LLM inferred N parts but none matched the parts catalog"*. Plus a phase-breakdown sub-line: *"similar: 5 · with notes: 0 · LLM inferred: 0 · matched catalog: 0"* for transparency. (3) **New CSS `.op-info-neutral`** — grey-bordered variant distinct from amber "failed" so the operator can tell "this didn't break, just no data" apart from "this broke". **Net effect**: empty cart now tells you which step of the pipeline produced zero — actionable info instead of silent confusion.
- **v0.26.57** — **Shim: wrap /api/chat in try/catch so the actual error reaches the extension (was: opaque HTTP 500).** Reporter screenshot showed `Failed to load resource: ... 127.0.0.1:3031/api/chat 500 (Internal Server Error)` — that's the shim's generic Express error handler converting a thrown exception into a body-less 500. The extension had no way to display anything useful. v0.26.57 wraps the `chat()` call in `/api/chat` handler with try/catch + returns `{ ok: false, error: msg, hint, stack }`. Plus pattern-detects four common causes and adds an actionable hint: `api key` → *"Set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in your .env file"*; `rate_limit / 429` → *"wait 30s and retry"*; `overload / 529 / 503` → *"retry in a moment"*; `401 / unauthor` → *"API key is invalid — check it in .env"*. Also logs the thrown error + first 5 stack frames to the shim console with `[/api/chat] handler threw:` prefix so the operator sees it without needing the extension at all. **Net effect**: next failure tells you what to fix instead of just "500" with no context. Shim rebuild required (`npm run web`) to pick up the change.
- **v0.26.56** — **Tighter product regex + pass page context to LLM (smarter than regex hunting).** Console output from v0.26.55 testing showed two issues: (1) the page-context product regex matched *"External ID"* (a column label) as the product value because the SAP page text reads "Product External ID 094485" and my pattern captured "External" after "Product". (2) Separately, the LLM `/api/chat` call returned HTTP 500 — fetch-fails handled but inference failed. **Two fixes**: (1) **Tightened regex chain** with three patterns tried in order of specificity: `Product External ID 094485` → captures the code; `Description PISTON,PMP,...` → captures line-item description; generic `Product: <value>` as fallback. Plus a label-blocklist (`External|Internal|Description|ID|Number|Code|Name|...`) that rejects label-like captures, and a "contains-digit OR all-caps" filter that ensures we capture a real code (not a label phrase). (2) **runPartsInference now accepts an `opts.pageContext` parameter** — when passed, the LLM prompt includes *"Visible SAP page context (extract product / line items / serial / equipment from this if missing above): ..."* with up to 4000 chars of scraped page text. The LLM is much smarter than regex at extracting structured info from column-row text like the Parts table — it can read "Product External ID 094485 Description PISTON,PMP,.1250,ICS3/5,SAPHIR" and understand 094485 is the code and PISTON,PMP... is the description. Caller updated in /order-parts to pass `$("prompt").dataset.pageContext`. **Net effect**: the LLM gets the visible UI text directly + the inference is no longer blocked by my brittle label regex.
- **v0.26.55** — **Bypass tenant field-name variance: use scraped SAP page context as subject/product fallback.** Reporter pushed forward on /order-parts: *"order parts neither recognizing from history, nor identifying product internal id, external id, product descriptions. It should propose the products by checking historical tickets."* Root insight: we've been chasing OData field-name variance for ~10 versions (canonical list widening, KUT wildcards, pickEntity unwrap, in-panel diagnostic), but the **visible UI text is always there** — the extension's content.js already scrapes the SAP page DOM into `$("prompt").dataset.pageContext` for the chat path. We just weren't using it for /order-parts inference. v0.26.55 adds a page-context fallback in the /order-parts handler: when `normalizeTicket().title` is null, scan the scraped page text for the SAP-standard header pattern `<id> - <subject>` and use the captured subject. When `normalizeTicket().product` is null, scan for `Product:` / `Material:` / `Produkt:` / `Materiel:` / `Modell:` / `Model:` labels (covers en/de/fr SAP locales) and capture the value. Both fallbacks log to console when they fire so we know they're catching what OData missed. **Net effect**: as long as the SAP page is visible (which it must be for the operator to be looking at a ticket), the inference has SOMETHING to work with — no more "No product/symptom" on tenants with non-standard OData field names.
- **v0.26.54** — **Default processing-type code: ZRP3 (was ZRP1 — that was the wrong Z-code).** Reporter: *"ZRP3"*. v0.26.52's default `ZRP1` was a guess based on the Z-prefix convention; the actual code this tenant uses for part consumption is `ZRP3`. Updated: Chrome extension default in `popup.js` (the `/order-parts` commit), Options-page input default in `options.js`, placeholder + help text in `options.html`. Other tenants can still override via Options — the field accepts any 3-5 char alphanumeric code (validated regex unchanged). Shim schema unchanged from v0.26.52 (already accepts any valid code).
- **v0.26.53** — **In-panel diagnostic: click "Show entity fields" right next to the error — no DevTools needed.** Reporter kept hitting "No product/symptom" on different tickets and tenants. Asked for DevTools console output four times; never came. Realized requiring operators to open DevTools + paste JSON is a non-starter — most service ops users don't have that workflow. v0.26.53 attaches the diagnostic directly to the failure result. **What changed**: (1) `/order-parts` handler now snapshots the entity's string fields (filtered to non-empty, len < 200, first 60) onto `result.diagnosticFields` when `inferenceError` is non-null. Defensive try/catch — never blocks rendering. (2) `renderOrderPartsPanel` checks for `diagnosticFields` and renders a collapsible `<details>` element under the amber error: *"🔍 Show entity fields (47) — paste this back to me to fix auto-suggest"*. Click expands a clean two-column table (field name on the left, value on the right). User can Ctrl+A inside the table → Ctrl+C → paste, OR screenshot the panel — both work. (3) Helper text: *"Tip: copy this table and paste back. I'll find the field carrying the subject and add it to the canonical list."* Plus CSS for the table (monospace field-name column, hover row highlight, word-break for long values). **Net effect**: next time auto-suggest fails, the operator sees a clickable diagnostic right in the side panel — one click → screenshot → fixed in the next version. Removes the "open DevTools" prerequisite that's been blocking this loop for 5 versions.
- **v0.26.52** — **ZRP1 default + configurable processing-type code (extension + shim).** Reporter: *"Use ZRP1 instead of SRP1 for parts"*. SAP tenants frequently introduce custom Z-prefix codes for line-item types (Z = customer extension namespace) — this tenant uses `ZRP1` for Part Consumption instead of SAP's standard `SRP1`. Three changes across the stack: (1) **Shim zod schema** (`src/index.ts`): `processingTypeCode: z.enum(["SRP1","ZALT","SRP0"])` was rejecting custom codes at the shim before they reached SAP. Loosened to `z.string().regex(/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9]{2,4}$/)` so any 3-5 char alphanumeric SAP code passes through; SAP itself does the actual validation. (2) **Shim `logServiceItem` impl** (`src/serviceOps.ts`): the TS type was `"SRP1" | "ZALT" | "SRP0"` (also restrictive) and the UoM default checked `processingTypeCode === "ZALT"` (would mis-classify a Z-prefix labor code). Loosened to `string` + new `isLaborCode` regex `/^[A-Z]?(ALT|HR|LB|LAB)$/i` for the HUR vs EA default. (3) **Chrome extension**: hardcoded `processingTypeCode: "SRP1"` in `/order-parts` commit is now read from `chrome.storage.sync["c4s.part-processing-type-code"]` (default **ZRP1**). New Options field "Part-consumption processing type code" with validation regex + uppercase normalization. Empty/unset value falls back to ZRP1. **Net effect**: Thermo Fisher tenant should commit successfully out-of-the-box; other tenants change the Options field once. Shim rebuild required (`npm run web`) to pick up the loosened schema — until that happens, even with the right code at the extension end, the shim would still reject.
- **v0.26.51** — **Two diagnostics: "unknown message type" commit-hint + exhaustive entity-field dump.** Reporter pushed forward with /order-parts on the Japanese tenant: parts catalogue search worked (v0.26.49 token-split), cart built fine, but commit failed with *"All 1 writes failed. 11125067 : unknown message type"* — SAP rejecting the shim's hardcoded `processingTypeCode: SRP1`. Auto-suggest also STILL showed "No product/symptom" even on v0.26.50 (pickEntity fix). Two diagnostic improvements: (1) **`commitErrorHint()` helper** — pattern-matches the failure messages and appends a contextual hint when recognised. Three patterns: `unknown message type|invalid.*type.*code|invalid processing` → "Likely cause: the shim writes parts using `processingTypeCode: SRP1` (Part Consumption), but this tenant uses a different code. Common alternatives: SRP2 (Service Activity), SRP3 (Item), SRPT (Part Total). Check the shim console for the SAP request body, then update `log_service_item` to use the right code." Plus auth-failure and property-not-found patterns with their own hints. (2) **Exhaustive entity-field dump** in `runPartsInference` when no product/subject found — now logs `ENTITY KEY COUNT`, `ALL KEYS (sorted)`, `ALL NON-EMPTY STRING FIELDS` (first 40), plus the previous title-shape and product-shape regex filters. Also stashes the raw entity on `window._lastEntity` so the operator can poke at it interactively in the console. The previous v0.26.48 diagnostic returned empty arrays when the shape filters happened to miss the actual field name; v0.26.51 dumps unfiltered so the next paste-back shows EVERY non-empty string field. **Note**: v0.26.50's pickEntity fix should already make `runPartsInference` see real entity data on this tenant. If it STILL shows empty arrays, that's the smoking gun that the unwrap is still wrong AND we'll see all the keys in the new dump.
- **v0.26.50** — **Fix the actual root cause of "no product/symptom": tenant returns `{results: [...]}` directly, without OData `d` wrapper.** Reporter ran the v0.26.48 diagnostic one-liner and pasted: `KEYS: results` / `NON-EMPTY STRINGS: {}`. Smoking gun: the entity my code was probing had only one key — `results`. The actual ticket lives inside `t.results[0]`. My `pickEntity` knows how to unwrap `{d: {results: [...]}}` (standard OData v2 envelope) and `{ticket: ...}` (already-extracted wrapper), but this particular Japanese-tenant deployment strips the `d` and returns `{results: [...]}` directly — likely a proxy layer normalized away the OData envelope but kept the array shape. Without the `d`, my unwrap chain bottomed out at `return payload` and treated the wrapper as the entity → 0 keys looked usable → every slash command that depends on `normalizeTicket` (auto-suggest, knowledge view, pulse, close-out, etc.) failed with "No product/symptom" or returned empty. **Fix**: added an explicit branch in pickEntity for `payload.results?.[0]` and `Array.isArray(payload.results)`. Two-line change. **Net effect**: this tenant should now work across ALL slash commands, not just /order-parts. The v0.26.48 widened field lists + v0.26.40 KUT address fallback also kick in now that pickEntity returns the right object to scan.
- **v0.26.49** — **Parts catalogue search: token-split fallback when the full query has no matches.** Reporter copied a SAP part description verbatim — `"PISTON,PMP,.1250,ICS3/5,SAPHIR"` — into the search box. The catalogue returned *"No matches"* because that compound string isn't how the catalog indexes parts: it has commas, dots-without-spaces, slashes, and concatenates 5+ separate attributes into one row. New fallback: when the full query returns 0 results AND contains punctuation (commas, slashes, dots, spaces), split on `[,/\s]+|(?<=\D)\.(?=\D)/` (commas/slashes/whitespace OR a dot between letters), filter to tokens with at least 2 alphanumeric chars, sort by length descending (longer = more specific), and retry with each token until one returns hits. Capped at 5 token retries to avoid runaway. When a token search succeeds, a small blue banner above the results indicates which token matched: *"Full query had no matches — showing results for token 'PISTON'."* — so the operator knows the result set is broader than they typed. Updated empty-state hint to surface the SAP-description-is-a-compound problem: *"Tip: SAP part descriptions like 'PISTON,PMP,.1250,...' are concatenations — try just one token like the material ID, or the simplest noun like 'PISTON'."* **Net effect**: copy-paste from a SAP Parts table now lands hits in the catalogue automatically.
- **v0.26.48** — **Widened `normalizeTicket` title + product field lists; added wildcard fallback + diagnostic dump.** Reporter saw *"Couldn't auto-suggest parts (No product/symptom — can't infer parts — Add a product or subject to the ticket first.)"* on a real ticket that visibly has both a product and a subject in the SAP UI. Root cause: `normalizeTicket()` only checked `Name`/`SubjectName`/`Subject`/`Description` for title and `ProductID`/`ProductName` for product — and the tenant uses different field names. Three changes: (1) **Widened canonical lists**: title now also checks `ServiceRequestSubject`, `ServiceRequestName`, `LongName`, `Headline`, `ProcessingTypeName`, `RequestText`, `KUT_Subject_KUT`, `KUT_Description_KUT`; product also checks `ProductDescription`, `ProductExternalID`, `InstallationPointDescription`, `InstalledBaseDescription`, `Material`, `MaterialID`, `MaterialDescription`, `KUT_ContractItemDesc_KUT`, `KUT_Product_KUT`, `KUT_Material_KUT`. (2) **Boundary-aware wildcard regex** as a last-resort sniff: title-shaped fields = `/(?:^|[_A-Z])(?:Subject|Title|Headline|Topic)(?:$|[_A-Z])/`; product-shaped = `/(?:^|[_A-Z])(?:Product|Material|Equipment|Installation|InstalledBase|Asset)(?:$|[_A-Z])/`. Same boundary trick as the v0.26.22 lat/lon regex — avoids false matches on `Translation`/`Escalation` while catching KUT custom names. Product wildcard skips pure-hex values (UUIDs) since we want descriptions, not IDs. (3) **Diagnostic dump** in `runPartsInference`: when both productHint and subject are empty, scan the raw entity for any string field matching title-ish or product-ish name patterns, log to `console.warn` with all values. The error text now hints: *"See DevTools console for the fields that ARE present — paste them back to me and I'll widen the canonical list."* Plus `normalizeTicket` now preserves the raw entity on `t._raw` so downstream diagnostics have access to it. **Net effect**: tenants with custom field names should now work without code changes for the common cases; remaining edge cases produce a paste-able diagnostic to extend the list.
- **v0.26.47** — **/order-parts now auto-suggests parts from similar tickets + serial history.** Reporter: *"can you search the parts for similar issues, serial no, tickets etc to come up with list?"* — yes, that workflow already existed in `/parts-check` (find similar tickets → read resolutions → LLM infer parts → cross-check stock), but standalone `/order-parts` opened an empty cart, forcing operators to type part names blindly. Now `/order-parts` automatically runs the same inference and pre-seeds the cart. **Implementation**: (1) extracted `/parts-check`'s 150-line inference workflow into a reusable `runPartsInference(t, endpoint, headers)` helper — vector search via `find_similar_tickets` (top 5 keyed by [subject, product, serial]), `get_ticket_notes` per match, LLM call with stitched resolutions returning structured `{inferredParts, summary}`, parallel `search_parts` stock check per inferred part. (2) `/parts-check` handler refactored to use the helper — no behavior change, ~120 lines removed. (3) `/order-parts` handler now calls `runPartsInference` after the ticket lookup. Inferred parts that match the catalogue (have a `stock.materialId`) seed the cart with quantity 1 each. (4) Panel renderer surfaces the inference summary: *"Seeded with 4 parts from 5 similar tickets. Review quantities below, then commit."* + an italic sub-line with the LLM's one-sentence summary. When inference fails (no product/symptom on ticket, LLM error, etc.), shows an amber warning *"Couldn't auto-suggest parts ({reason}). Use the search box below."* — panel still opens, manual search still works. (5) Added `/order-parts` to the slow-slash regex so the progress strip shows "Finding similar tickets…" / "Reading resolutions…" / "Inferring parts with LLM…" / "Checking warehouse stock…" — 5-15s wait now has feedback. **Net effect**: clicking 📦 Order parts on any ticket with a product or symptom now opens a cart already populated with parts the tech is likely to need, ranked by recurrence across similar past resolutions. One-click commit for the obvious cases; edit/remove/add via search box for the rest.
- **v0.26.46** — **Fix: Order parts title STILL showed ObjectID for some tickets — fall back to detectedTicket.** Reporter screenshot showed *"Order parts — ticket 0A718B1C…"* on ticket 4431042 — different ticket from v0.26.44, different tenant data, neither `ID` nor `ServiceRequestID` nor `TicketID` was populated as a string OR number on the lookup response (v0.26.44's number-coercion fix didn't catch this case). Root cause: SAP's `lookup` response for THIS specific ticket type has the ticket ID under some yet-undiscovered field name. Defensive fix: use the resolved ticket ID we ALREADY have in scope (from `resolveTicketId(ticketHint)` → variable named `id` in the handler) as a fallback before falling through to ObjectID slice. Three places updated: (1) `/order-parts` handler return — `displayId = t.id || id || objectId.slice(0,8)+"…"`, ctx.ticketId = `t.id || id`. (2) `openOrderPartsPanel` call from /parts-check's "Order these" button — falls back to `$("prompt").dataset.detectedTicket`. (3) Same fallback chain in panel title. **Net effect**: panel always shows the human ticket ID the operator was working on, regardless of whether the OData lookup response surfaces ID under the canonical name. Note: this only fixes the DISPLAY. If you need to verify the actual lookup-response shape on this tenant to find the real ID field name, paste the output of (in DevTools console): `(await fetch("http://127.0.0.1:3031/api/lookup", {method:"POST",headers:{"Content-Type":"application/json"},body:JSON.stringify({input:"4431042"})})).json()` — I'll add the right field to the canonical list.
- **v0.26.45** — **Annotate diagnostics: phase-tagged console logs + actionable inline error hints.** Reporter: *"annotate still failing dont know why"* — and the inline status only said *"Annotate failed: {message}"* with no breakdown of WHICH step broke (shim unreachable / Anthropic API key missing / rate-limit / overloaded / malformed JSON). v0.26.45 instruments the path: (1) `fetchPageAnnotations` now logs `console.log("annotate: fetchPageAnnotations starting", {endpoint, ticketObjectId, contextLen})` at start, then `console.error` at every failure point with phase markers. (2) Three failure branches now produce specific user-visible messages instead of one generic one: **network/shim down** → *"Couldn't reach the shim at .../api/chat — fetch failed. Is the shim running on port 3031?"* · **non-JSON response** → *"Shim returned non-JSON response (HTTP 500). Check shim console for errors."* · **shim error JSON** → preserves the shim's error message + appends a contextual hint: `api key`/`anthropic` → *"Check the shim's .env has ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set."*; `rate limit`/`429` → *"Anthropic API rate-limited — wait 30s and retry."*; `overloaded`/`503` → *"Anthropic API overloaded — retry in a moment."* (3) Top-level catch in `annotatePage` logs full error to `console.error` with stack, surfaces the first 220 chars inline so the operator sees a useful summary. **Next failure**: open DevTools console (right-click side panel → Inspect), click 🪄 Annotate, and the failure phase + cause are immediately obvious in the logs.
- **v0.26.44** — **Fix: panel titles fell back to ObjectID slice when ticket ID was a number.** Reporter screenshot showed *"Order parts — ticket 0AAD1B77…"* instead of *"Order parts — ticket 4430921"* — the user had ticket 4430921 detected, the panel title should match. Root cause: `firstString()` only accepts string values, but some SAP OData responses serialize numeric IDs without string quotes (e.g., `ServiceRequest.ID = 4430921` as Edm.Int32 instead of Edm.String depending on tenant metadata). When `entity.ID` is a number, `firstString` returned null → `normalizeTicket().id = null` → title fell back to the ObjectID slice. Fix: extended `firstString` to accept finite non-zero numbers and coerce them to strings. Rejects NaN/Infinity/0 (0 typically means "unset" in SAP). Same defensive pattern as the v0.26.20 `pickCoord` helper. **Net effect**: panel titles ("Order parts", "Close-out", "Customer pulse", "Knowledge view", etc.) now show the human ticket ID across all tenants regardless of how SAP types the field.
- **v0.26.43** — **Better error message when the shim is down: actionable hint instead of opaque "fetch failed".** Reporter: *"whatever i click its failing, annotate not working too?"* — root cause was the HTTP shim on port 3031 not running, but the displayed error was just *"PM kit analysis failed · fetch failed"* with no indication of WHAT to do. Two improvements: (1) **`ask()` catch block** — pattern-detect `fetch failed`/`Failed to fetch`/`NetworkError`/`ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`/`ECONNREFUSED`/`ETIMEDOUT` and replace the generic error with a multi-line actionable hint: *"The shim isn't running, has crashed, or is on a different port. Fix: open a cmd window in the repo root and run: npm run web — or double-click start-c4s-dev.bat. Look for 'listening on 3031' in the shim's console to confirm."* Plus the underlying error string for diagnostics. (2) **`renderResult` for `kind: "error"`** — same pattern-detection, same hint appended. Catches the case where the result-payload contains a fetch-failed message but didn't throw at the top level. **Net effect**: when the shim goes down for any reason (crashed, killed, never started, wrong port), every slash command and Annotate action now tells the operator exactly what to do, instead of a 2-word opaque error.
- **v0.26.42** — **i18n: country-aware zipcode detection + locale-aware table sort + more postcode patterns.** Reporter: *"are you making sure the tickets are global with different languages?"* — fair check. Tenant data already showed Korean customers (`주식회사 리가켐바이오사이언스`), German universities, French sites. Unicode TEXT display worked fine, but country-aware geocoding and sorting did not. Three improvements: (1) **Layered country detection** in new `buildPostcodeGeocodeParams()` helper. Priority: postcode shape (UK `SW1A 1AA` → GB, Canada `A1A 1A1` → CA, Japan `100-0001` → JP) → ticket-context country (read from `_allSearchRows[].country` — already extracted by v0.26.40 KUT sniff) → browser locale (`navigator.language` → ISO-3166-1-alpha-2) → US-default. The hardcoded `country=US` for 5-digit input is gone; now a German operator typing `60311` (Frankfurt) gets Germany without needing to add a prefix. (2) **More postcode patterns**: UK + Canada + Japan + generic 4-7 digit numeric (covers DE/FR/ES/IT/KR/AU). (3) **Locale-aware sorting in table modal**: replaced `<`/`>` with `String.prototype.localeCompare(b, undefined, {sensitivity: "base", numeric: true})` so Korean/Japanese/Chinese/German sort by user's locale rules rather than Unicode code points. Numeric columns (lat, lon, distance) keep direct numeric comparison. Status banner now also surfaces the country SOURCE: *"assumed country: KR (ticket context)"* / *"assumed country: DE (browser locale)"* / *"assumed country: US (US-default)"* so operators can spot wrong assumptions and add a country prefix if needed. **Known remaining gaps**: RTL languages (Arabic/Hebrew) display text correctly but the layout doesn't flip; date filter strings ("Last 30 days") aren't localized; no IME-specific input handling beyond what browsers provide natively.
- **v0.26.41** — **"Show non-mappable" toggle in the table modal — verify why a specific ticket isn't on the map.** Reporter: *"Why some tickets from the list view are skipped?"* — looking at SAP list-view ticket 4430986 in the left pane and finding it absent from the right-pane table modal's 33-ticket list. The 33 only counts tickets WITH SAP-stored lat/lon; tickets without coords were silently filtered out. Now the modal header includes a *"Show non-mappable"* checkbox. When checked: the table shows the FULL search-result set (not just mappable), with non-mappable rows greyed-out and italicized. lat/lon cells show `—` with a hover tooltip explaining WHY the ticket was skipped: *"Has address but no SAP-stored Latitude/Longitude — can't be plotted on the map"* (`address-only`) / *"Only a customer name on the ticket — no service-location address or coords"* (`customer-only`) / *"No usable location data on this ticket at all"* (`no-location`). The title bar reflects the mode: *"33 mappable tickets"* (toggle off) vs *"78 tickets (33 mappable, 45 non-mappable)"* (toggle on). New mapState field: `_allSearchRows` (the full result set with `mappable: bool` and `skipReason: string` annotations on each row). The mappable-only set still drives the actual map; this toggle only affects the verification table. **Limitation surfaced**: if a ticket from your list view isn't in the table even with toggle on, the search probably didn't return it (filter mismatch or top=50 cap). Try the "📋 Use list-view tickets" button to import the visible list-view tickets directly into the candidate pool — that bypasses the filter-driven search.
- **v0.26.40** — **Address column was blank in the table view — added KUT address fallback + wildcard sniff.** Reporter: *"why address is blank in table view?"*. The tenant stores street address in custom KUT extension fields, NOT in canonical `ServiceLocationStreet/City/State/PostalCode`.
