The earlier inspect output showed `KUT_AddressName1_KUT: "Cambridge Consultants Ltd"`, `KUT_AddressName2_KUT: "Cambridge Science Park"`, with the standard street/city/state/postal fields all empty. My `extractTicketLocation` was only checking the canonical names, so `address = ""` → the table modal showed `—`. Two-tier fallback added: (1) **KUT_AddressName1-4_KUT join**: when canonical address is empty, build the address by joining non-empty `KUT_AddressName1_KUT` → `KUT_AddressName2_KUT` → `KUT_AddressName3_KUT` → `KUT_AddressName4_KUT` with `, `. Catches Thermo Fisher's address-line schema directly. (2) **Wildcard KUT address sniff**: if even (1) returns nothing, scan all ticket keys for a boundary-aware regex `(?:^|[_A-Z])(?:Street|City|State|Country|Postal|Zip|Region|Address)(?:$|[_A-Z])`. Same boundary rules as the v0.26.22 lat/lon wildcard so words like `Translation`/`Escalation`/etc. don't false-match. Collects all populated string values + dedupes + joins. **Net effect**: the table modal's Address column now shows *"Cambridge Consultants Ltd, Cambridge Science Park"* (or similar from KUT lines) on tickets where canonical address is empty, instead of `—`. Markers' popup also benefits since they share the same `loc.address` string.
- **v0.26.39** — **Removed the verbose hover tooltip from the status row.** Reporter: *"do not show mouse hover on last search results description its too big so remove mouse hover"*. The 7-bullet status-vocabulary tooltip I added in v0.26.25/v0.26.27 was a fine idea at the time, but after the v0.26.25 relabeling ("had mappable coordinates" instead of "with SAP coords") and the v0.26.26 "N of M shown" lead-line, the inline status text reads cleanly on its own. The big tooltip popping up whenever the cursor crossed the status area was more distraction than help. Stripped the entire `title="..."` block from `#map-filter-status`. The status row still shows full breakdown inline; users who want a deeper explanation can refer to the README or the v0.26.25/v0.26.27 changelog entries.
- **v0.26.38** — **Map filter sidebar: 3 logical sections + hide inline hints, surface as hover tooltips.** Reporter: *"can you keep the help descriptions show on mouse hover and sectionize"*. Previously the sidebar was a flat 7-control list with wordy italic hint paragraphs under several inputs — visually noisy, hard to scan. New layout: **🎫 What to show** (Status / Priority / Date range), **📍 Where** (Serial / City-address-ZIP / Radius), **⚙️ Limit** (Max tickets). Each section gets a small uppercase header with an emoji icon and a faint underline separating it from the next. Within each control, the inline `.map-filter-hint` paragraph is replaced by a small `?` help-badge to the right of the label — the full explanation lives in the `title=` attribute, surfaced on hover. New CSS: `.map-filter-section` (vertical group with border-top separator), `.map-filter-section-header` (uppercase brand-colored), `.map-filter-label-row` (flex container for label + ? icon), `.map-filter-help-icon` (14px circular badge with hover state — grey → brand color). **Plus**: City filter label simplified from *"City or service-location address"* (verbose) to *"City / address / ZIP"* (scannable), placeholder updated to reflect zipcode support. **Hint content preserved + improved**: every previous inline hint now lives in a tooltip, plus new tooltips for Status / Priority / Date range / Max tickets that previously had no explanation. **Net effect**: sidebar is roughly 30% shorter visually, but no information is lost — power users can read all explanations via hover, casual users see a clean control panel.
- **v0.26.37** — **Default behavior changed: navigate the EXISTING SAP tab instead of opening a new tab.** Reporter: *"No its still loading in new window"* — even with v0.26.36's `index + openerTabId + focused:true` improvements, the perceived "new window" issue persisted. Root cause: when a side panel is attached to one Chrome window but the SAP tabs live in another, opening any tab anywhere will feel like "elsewhere" to the user. Solution: stop creating new tabs by default. v0.26.37 switches the default flow to **navigate the existing SAP tab in place** via `chrome.tabs.update(sapTab.id, {url, active: true})`. The previous SAP tab state is lost (browser-back still works) but the mental model is dead simple: *"the SAP tab now shows this ticket"*. No new tabs, no new windows, no ambiguity. Browser convention preserved: **Ctrl+click (or Cmd+click on Mac)** still opens in a new tab — for users who want to keep multiple tickets open. Link tooltip + footer hint updated: *"Click ticket ID ↗ → open in existing SAP tab (Ctrl+click for new tab)"*. New return shape: `{mode, tabId, opened}` where `opened ∈ "reused-tab" | "new-tab"` — callers can use this to surface confirmation if needed. Window-focus call retained from v0.26.36 so the SAP window comes forward even when the navigation happens in a background window.
- **v0.26.36** — **Tab-placement fixes so ticket links don't feel like "new window".** Reporter: *"clicking the ticket id opens the right ticket but opens in new window instead of opening as tab in existing C4S window"*. Investigation: v0.26.32 already passed `windowId: sapTab.windowId` to `chrome.tabs.create`, so the new tab IS opening in the SAP tab's window. But because the side panel is sometimes attached to a different window than the SAP tabs, the new tab opened behind the user's currently-focused window — looked like "elsewhere". Three improvements: (1) **`index: sapTab.index + 1`** — places the new tab immediately to the right of the existing SAP tab in the tab strip instead of at the end. Visually groups the two SAP tabs together. (2) **`openerTabId: sapTab.id`** — Chrome treats opener/opened tab pairs as related (e.g., closing the new tab returns focus to the opener), and many users have tab-grouping extensions that auto-group these. (3) **`chrome.windows.update({focused: true})`** after creation — brings the SAP tab's window to the foreground. Critical for multi-window setups where the side panel and SAP tabs live in different Chrome windows. Without this, the new tab opens in a window the user can't see; with it, the SAP window pops to the front and the user sees the new tab immediately. **Net effect**: clicking a ticket ID feels like the new tab "joins" the existing SAP tabs in the same window, with the window itself coming forward if needed.
- **v0.26.35** — **Chips auto-fire ask() instead of waiting for a second click.** Reporter: *"avoid clicking ask when i click on little tiles it should trigger ask automatically"*. Previously, clicking any slash chip (the `/pulse`, `/triage`, `/map`, etc. row above the textarea) OR a built-in slash-command template chip (💚 Customer Pulse, ✅ Close-out, 🪟 Map view, etc.) would fill the textarea but require a second click on Ask to fire. Now: a single chip click fills the textarea AND fires `ask()` in the same handler. Two paths updated: (1) `renderSlashChips` — every slash chip's onclick now calls `ask()` at the end. (2) `renderTemplateChips` — onclick checks `tpl.prompt.startsWith("/")` and auto-fires only for slash-command templates; free-text templates still wait for manual Ask so the user can edit the prompt before sending. **Shift+click overrides** the auto-fire — useful when you actually want to edit the inline argument (e.g., `/visits 20` instead of the auto-detected default). Both chip handlers detect the shift modifier via the click event and skip `ask()` when it's held. Tooltip text updated to surface the new behavior: *"description — click to run (Shift+click to edit first)"*. The ⌘K command palette already had this logic (`runImmediately` flag for slash items), so no change there. **Net effect**: one click to run any common workflow.
- **v0.26.34** — **Real V1 ticket deep-link encoder — reverse-engineered from a sample URL.** Reporter pasted a working V1 ticket URL: `.../newclient.html?app.component=/SAP_UI_CT/Main/root.uiccwoc&rootWindow=X&redirectUrl=/sap/byd/runtime#Nav/1/eyJ...`. Decoded the base64 fragment to: `{"thingparams":{"Key":"<32-hex-ObjectID>"},"inPort":"Inspect","target":"/BYD_COD/ServiceOnDemand/Collaboration/COD_SR_TI.TI.uicomponent","winId":"<32-hex>"}`. Three corrections to my earlier guesses: (1) **Fragment prefix is `#Nav/1/`** for entity-detail views; `#Nav/0/` is for home/landing. v0.26.33 used `#Nav/0/` based on the home-page URL I saw initially — wrong polarity. Switched back to `#Nav/1/`. (2) **Payload schema** is `{thingparams: {Key: ObjectID}, inPort: "Inspect", target: "...COD_SR_TI.TI.uicomponent", winId: <random-hex>}` — not the `{t, n}` shape I guessed from `decodeFragmentTicket`. The simple shape never worked; that's why all my clicks routed to Home. (3) **Query string is mandatory**: `?app.component=/SAP_UI_CT/Main/root.uiccwoc&rootWindow=X&redirectUrl=/sap/byd/runtime` — without it the C4C router doesn't fully bootstrap navigation context. ObjectID normalization: strip dashes (some OData responses return hyphenated `00163E83-2D24-...`, the URL needs unhyphenated `00163E832D24...`) and uppercase. Random `winId` generated per call via `crypto.getRandomValues(Uint8Array(16))` → 32-char hex. Override the reference tab's pathname when building the URL (the user might be on a home page that doesn't have the bootstrap query string set). **Net effect**: clicking a ticket ID in the table modal now opens that specific ticket directly in SAP C4C V1, not the Home page. Two-step paste workaround from v0.26.32-33 is gone — full deep link works.
- **v0.26.33** — **Correct V1/V2 naming + use `#Nav/0/` (not `#Nav/1/`) for V1 deep links.** Reporter: *"its service cloud v1 not v2"*. My v0.26.32 mislabel — `newclient.html` is C4C HTML5 / Service Cloud V1, NOT V2. V2 (launched 2023) uses a different `/sap/c4c/ui/` URL pattern. Two corrections: (1) **Detection regex**: `isV1` matches `/HTML5/newclient\.html` or `/sap/ap/ui/clogin`; `isV2` matches `/sap/c4c/ui/`. Result `mode` values: `"v1"` (deep link succeeded) | `"v1-fallback"` (deep link build/open failed, used clipboard fallback) | `"v2-fallback"` (V2 — no encoder yet). (2) **Fragment prefix**: switched from `#Nav/1/` to `#Nav/0/` — matches what the user's tenant actually uses. **Still TODO**: the exact JSON payload shape V1 expects to navigate to a specific ServiceRequest isn't yet known. The v0.26.30 `{t: ObjectID, n: "ServiceRequest"}` shape is a guess based on the existing `decodeFragmentTicket` logic; the user's screenshot showed `_sWoLkId/_sViewId/_sPerformanceWoLkId` shapes on the Home view, suggesting V1 uses a richer schema. To finish: need a sample URL from a real ticket in this tenant. The modal status message for `v1-fallback` now explicitly asks for one: *"deep-link navigation payload not yet known for this tenant"*.
- **v0.26.32** — **Fix two bugs with v0.26.30 ticket-link: wrong page on V2 + new window instead of new tab.** Reporter: *"When I click on ticket it opens c4s screen but not the ticket, second issue it always opens new window instead of a tab in existing screen"*. Root causes: (a) tenant is on **SAP Service Cloud V2** (URL `.../HTML5/newclient.html#Nav/0/...`) which uses a fundamentally different navigation schema than the **Classic HTML5 client** (`#Nav/1/{t, n}`) my encoder was built for — Classic-style payloads send V2 to its default Home route; (b) `chrome.tabs.create({url, active: true})` without an explicit `windowId` opens in the *last-focused normal window*, which from the side-panel context is sometimes a different window than where SAP is open → looks like "new window every time". Fixes: (1) **windowId**: pass `windowId: sapTab.windowId` so the new tab always joins the SAP tab's window. (2) **V2 detection**: regex matches `/HTML5/newclient\.html` or `/sap/c4c/ui/` — when detected, instead of building a broken Classic deep link, we (a) copy the ticket ID to the clipboard, (b) focus the existing SAP tab via `chrome.tabs.update` + `chrome.windows.update({ focused: true })`, (c) return a `{ mode: "v2-fallback" }` result. The modal footer flashes a blue 8s message: *"Ticket ID 4430921 copied to clipboard. SAP tab is focused — paste it into the universal search bar at the top of SAP (Service Cloud V2 doesn't support deep-link navigation yet)."* — explicit about why this isn't a full deep link, actionable for the user. (3) **Classic path unchanged** — still opens a new tab with the encoded `#Nav/1/{t, n}` URL, just now in the right window. **TODO before v0.26.33**: need a sample SAP V2 ticket URL (with a real ticket open, copy URL from address bar) to reverse-engineer the V2 navigation payload shape and build a proper V2 deep-link encoder.
- **v0.26.31** — **Toolbar-icon click now opens the side panel by default; new ⧉ pop-out button switches to a detached window.** Reporter: *"by default can you make this extension open as sidepanel and give a button to popout?"*. Three changes: (1) **`default_popup` removed from manifest action** — without this property set, the side panel can be wired up as the click target. (2) **`chrome.sidePanel.setPanelBehavior({ openPanelOnActionClick: true })` in `background.js`** — fires on `onInstalled` and `onStartup`, both for first-time installs and browser-restart scenarios. Net effect: clicking the toolbar icon (or pressing `Ctrl+Shift+Y`) opens the side panel directly. (3) **New ⧉ pop-out button** in the side-panel header — visible only when `body.mode-sidepanel` is set (CSS-toggled). Click → `chrome.windows.create({ type: "popup", width: 480, height: 720, url: "popup.html?from=sidepanel-popout" })`. The new window detects `from=sidepanel-popout`, applies `mode-detached-popup` body class so the existing in-window header buttons (↗ back to side panel · ✕ close) appear. Extended `IS_DETACHED_POPUP` to recognize `from=sidepanel-popout` alongside the existing `from=in-page`. **UX flow now**: click icon → side panel · need a free-floating window for screenshot / side-by-side comparison / dragging to another monitor → click ⧉ · want to return to the docked panel from the popped-out window → click ↗ (existing button). Manifest `commands._execute_action.description` updated to *"Open Ask C4S (side panel — v0.26.31+ default)"* so the keyboard-shortcut help text doesn't lie.
- **v0.26.30** — **Ticket ID is a clickable hyperlink → opens the ticket directly in SAP C4C.** Reporter: *"If I click on ticket id can it open in c4s?"*. Yes — the table-modal Ticket ID column now renders as an underlined link with a `↗` (open-in-new-tab) arrow. Clicking the link opens that ticket in a new tab in SAP C4C, bypassing the extension's `/triage` flow. The rest of the row still routes to `/triage` on click (two actions, one row — pick the one you need). **Implementation**: new `openTicketInC4C(ticket)` helper — reverses the existing `decodeFragmentTicket` logic. (1) Finds any currently-open SAP C4C tab via `chrome.tabs.query` (matches the manifest host list: `*.crm.ondemand.com`, `*.crm.sap.com`, `*.c4c.sap.com`). (2) Takes the reference tab's origin + path so the tenant URL pattern is preserved (some tenants use `/sap/ap/ui/clogin`, others `/sap/c4c/...`). (3) Builds the navigation fragment by base64-encoding `{t: ObjectID, n: "ServiceRequest"}` — same payload shape v0.4.3's decoder reads. (4) Opens via `chrome.tabs.create`. **Graceful fallback**: when no SAP tab is currently open, the modal footer turns amber for 6 seconds with the message *"Couldn't open ticket 4430921 — no SAP C4C tab is currently open in this browser. Open SAP first, then click the link again."* — instead of failing silently. Hyperlink click uses `stopPropagation` so the row-click `/triage` handler doesn't double-fire. Updated footer hints reflect both actions: *"Click any row → /triage in extension. Click ticket ID ↗ → open in SAP C4C."*
- **v0.26.29** — **Table-view modal — see every mappable ticket with its address + lat/lon + distance.** Reporter wanted a verification view: *"show list view of the tickets with address and lat long for those 152 tickets found in first run show hyperlink and when user clicks show in a table view."* New `📊 View N as table` button appears in the status row whenever the search returned tickets with SAP-provided coordinates (regardless of whether the radius filter dropped them). Click → opens a full-bleed modal with a sortable table showing: **Ticket ID** (monospace, blue) · **Customer** · **Address** · **State** · **Latitude** (6-decimal monospace) · **Longitude** (6-decimal monospace) · **Distance** (only in radius mode, colored green inside / amber outside). Click any column header to sort (asc → desc → asc). Free-text filter input in the header — searches across ID, subject, customer, address, state, lat/lon string. Click any row → fills `/triage <id>` in the prompt textarea + closes the map + map modal (jump straight to that ticket's details). Outside-radius rows greyed to 55% opacity so the visual hierarchy mirrors what's on the map (markers shown vs not). Default sort = distance ascending when in radius mode (closest first); free-form otherwise. Footer summarises: *"7 inside 100 mi radius (shown on map) · 145 outside (greyed out below)."*. **Net effect**: the operator can now answer "which exact tickets are mappable, what coords do they have, and where do they sit relative to my search center?" without leaving the map panel. New mapState fields: `_allMappableTickets` (full pre-radius list) + `_radiusCenter` (so the modal can recompute distance independently). Each row carries `distanceKm` + `insideRadius` flag pre-computed in `renderMapMarkersFromRows` so the modal doesn't have to re-haversine on render.
- **v0.26.28** — **Show extension version in the header badge instead of the shim host:port.** Reporter: *"show the version name instead of ip port"*. Previously the top-right badge said `127.0.0.1:3031` — accurate but rarely useful (almost always localhost during dev, and not actionable when users see it). Replaced with the extension version pulled live from `chrome.runtime.getManifest().version` — renders as `v0.26.28`. The endpoint URL moves into the badge's hover tooltip: *"Extension v0.26.28 · Connected to 127.0.0.1:3031"* — still accessible when you need to verify which shim you're hitting. Made the testing/diagnostic workflow much faster: every screenshot the user shares now self-identifies which version was running, so we don't have to ask *"did you reload?"* before diagnosing.
- **v0.26.27** — **Fix: status-row hover tooltip showed "N tickets" / "N mi" — read like literal bugs, not algebra placeholders.** Reporter: *"Mouse hover shows N tickets instead of actual count"*. The v0.26.25 tooltip used `N` as a mathematical "any number" placeholder in lines like *"outside N mi radius"* and *"N tickets no location"*. To a user that reads as "the variable wasn't filled in" — exactly the kind of half-rendered template that signals a bug. Rewrote every line to use prose descriptors (*"outside `<distance>` radius"*, *"`<count>` mappable tickets shown"*) and expanded the vocabulary to cover seven status-row terms instead of five. Same hover-tooltip mechanism; just unambiguous wording. Also added explanations for `full history (date filter dropped for radius)` and `scoped to state: XX` — both of which appeared in actual status rows in recent sessions and weren't documented in the tooltip.
- **v0.26.26** — **"N of M shown" — surface the radius-drop relationship clearly.** Reporter asked: *"if 152 had coords why its not displayed on map?"* — exactly the kind of question that signals the status row is hiding the right answer. Previously the row went *"9 tickets at 7 locations · scanned 200, 152 had mappable coordinates · ... · 85 outside 100 mi radius · ..."* — the "85 outside" was a LOCATION count not a TICKET count, and was buried mid-line. The 152→9 reduction looked unexplained. Three fixes: (1) **Track tickets-dropped-by-radius separately** — sum the per-marker ticket counts when dropping markers outside the radius, store on `mapState._droppedTicketsByRadius`. Previously only location count was tracked. (2) **Lead the radius-mode status row with the relationship**: *"9 of 152 mappable tickets shown at 7 locations (143 outside 100 mi radius)"* — answers "why only 9?" in one line, before any other detail. (3) **Mirror the same format in the context banner**: *"Tickets within 100 mi of '01730' (assumed US) · 9 of 152 mappable tickets shown"* — same relationship visible at the top of the map without scrolling down to the status row. Non-radius modes keep the simpler "M markers of N tickets" form. The status row's other detail (skipped, no location, smart-strip drops) shifts to the right of the lead, less prominent — same information, better hierarchy.
- **v0.26.25** — **De-jargoned status row.** Reporter asked: *"what is 152 with sap coords mean?"* — the engineering-internal label "with SAP coords" had leaked into the user-facing status. The phrase only made sense if the operator knew the two-tier coord-extraction pattern (SAP-provided vs Nominatim-geocoded). Rephrased every status-row variant: *"152 with SAP coords"* → *"152 had mappable coordinates"*; *"N skipped (no SAP coords, geocoding too slow for radius)"* → *"N had only an address (skipped — geocoding is too slow for radius search)"*; non-radius mode *"N via SAP coords"* → *"N pinned from SAP-stored coordinates"*; helpful-error *"N with SAP coords — none within radius"* → *"N were mappable — none within radius"*. Plus a HOVER TOOLTIP on the entire status row explaining the full vocabulary: scanned / had mappable coordinates / had only an address / outside radius / no location. The tooltip uses HTML `title=` attribute so it works without a custom popover — surfaces on hover anywhere on the status text. **Net effect**: status row is now readable without product-specific context. The tooltip handles the deep-dive cases.
- **v0.26.24** — **Context banner under the map toolbar — "what am I looking at right now?".** Reporter asked: *"I see some tickets on initial launch of map view where are these coming from?"* — fair question, the default-view scope wasn't visually labelled. Operators had to read the filter sidebar to figure out what scope was active. New mode-keyed banner sits between the toolbar and the body, always visible, updates every time the search re-runs. Six modes with distinct colors + icons: (a) **default** (🌐 grey) — *"Default view — most-recent open tickets across all sites (last 30 days). Add a city / serial filter to narrow."* (b) **serial** (🔧 blue) — *"Tickets for equipment serial 8081085."* (c) **listview** (📋 yellow) — *"Showing the tickets you have open on the SAP list view. Click 📋 button to re-import."* (d) **radius-marker** (📍 green) — *"Tickets within 100 mi of LIFE TECHNOLOGIES Bedford site."* (e) **radius-city** (🎯 green) — *"Tickets within 50 mi of '01730' (assumed US)."* (f) **city** (🏙 purple) — *"Tickets in 'BEDFORD'."* All include a counts suffix like *"· 23 markers of 187 tickets"* once rendering completes. New helpers `updateMapContextBanner({mode, text, icon})` (direct setter) and `refreshMapContextBanner({rowCount, markerCount})` (mode-resolution from mapState + filter state, order: list-view → marker-radius → city-radius → city → serial → default). Wired into: `openMapPanel` (initial "Loading…"), `applyMapFilters` (post-render refresh), `useListViewTickets` (sets sticky `_fromListView` flag + refresh). New `mapState._fromListView` flag — sticky boolean, cleared by `applyMapFilters` so the banner doesn't lie when the operator manually applies a filter after a list-view import. CSS uses subtle pastel backgrounds + 3px left border in the mode color so the banner is informative without being visually heavy. **Net effect**: the operator can glance at the top of the map and immediately know what scope is active and how to change it.
- **v0.26.23** — **Zipcode disambiguation: detect 5-digit input, use `postalcode=` not `city=`, default `country=US`.** v0.26.22 unlocked KUT lat/lon extraction (152 of 200 tickets matched), but the reporter then typed `01730` in the city filter expecting Bedford MA and got markers scattered across Europe. Root cause: Nominatim treats `01730` as a city name (returns zero results or weird matches), and the same postcode is valid in 6+ countries (USA, Sweden, Poland, Czech Republic, Spain, France). Without a country hint Nominatim picks whichever match has higher OSM "importance" — usually European. So the radius search ran around a European center, returning European tickets within 50 mi of that wrong point. **Fix**: zipcode-pattern detection in the pre-geocode call. (1) `^\d{5}(-\d{4})?$` → US-shaped → Nominatim params `postalcode=01730&country=US` → returns Bedford MA precisely. (2) UK postcode pattern (`SW1A 1AA`-shaped) → `postalcode=...&country=GB`. (3) 4-6 digit numeric (ambiguous European) → `postalcode=...` without country hint (best-effort). (4) Anything else → `city=...` as before. New `mapState._inferredCountry` flag surfaced in the status row: *"assumed country: US (zipcode disambiguation)"* so the operator sees what was assumed and can correct if needed. Both the pre-geocode call in `applyMapFilters` AND the fallback re-geocode in `renderMapMarkersFromRows` use the same detection logic for consistency. **Future improvement**: read country from the active ticket's `ServiceLocationCountry` / `KUT_RPCountry_KUT` instead of defaulting to US — that handles non-US deployments automatically. For now the US default is correct for the target customer (Thermo Fisher US service ops).
- **v0.26.22** — **Casing fix: KUT_RP*L*atitude_KUT, not KUT_RP*l*atitude_KUT.** Reporter ran the v0.26.21 diagnostic one-liner and got the smoking-gun result: `HAS KUT_RPlatitude_KUT? false → undefined` while the array of KUT keys clearly contained an entry that LOOKED like `KUT_RPlatitude_KUT` at console-font zoom. Mutually exclusive — the actual field name is `KUT_RPLatitude_KUT` (capital L), following SAP's standard CamelCase convention (abbreviation "RP" + new word "Latitude" capitalizes). My v0.26.19 entries had a lowercase `l` and quietly missed every match — the case-sensitive wildcard regex `/(^|[_A-Z])lat(itude)?($|[_A-Z])/` also missed because the literal `lat` only matches lowercase, not capital `Lat`. Two fixes: (1) **Add both casings** to the canonical lat/lon lookup list — `KUT_RPLatitude_KUT` (correct) AND `KUT_RPlatitude_KUT` (defensive); same for longitude. (2) **Case-flexible wildcard regex**: `/(^|[_A-Z])(?:Lat|lat)(?:itude)?($|[_A-Z])/` — accepts either capitalization on the "Lat" prefix while keeping the boundary check strict (still requires uppercase-or-underscore boundary, so "Translation"/"Escalation"/"Related" don't false-match). Same shape for `Lon`/`lon`. **Net effect**: Thermo Fisher tenant's real field names now match on Tier A immediately. Future tenants with either casing also work. The boundary check still rules out "translation" and similar false-positive substring matches.
- **v0.26.21** — **Make diagnostic logs actually visible by default.** v0.26.20 added a "✓ matched lat/lon" log to confirm coord extraction was working, but used `console.debug` — which Chrome DevTools HIDES by default unless you toggle Verbose in the level filter (most operators never do). Result: the diagnostic was effectively invisible, and a tester reported the log "missing" when it was actually firing — just suppressed by the default Default-levels filter. v0.26.21 promotes three diagnostic logs to visible levels: (a) successful coord match → `console.log` with ✓ prefix; (b) failed coord extraction → `console.warn` with ✗ prefix (yellow border in DevTools); (c) per-search outcome diagnostic → `console.log`. All three now appear at the default DevTools level. Failure logs use warn so they're visually distinct from success logs in a busy console. **Net effect**: open DevTools, run the map, see the diagnostic immediately — no need to know about the Verbose toggle.
- **v0.26.20** — **Robust coord extraction + always-on success logging.** v0.26.19 added `KUT_RPlatitude_KUT` to the canonical Tier A list, but used `firstString()` which only accepts string values. SAP/shim can return KUT-extension lat/lon as EITHER a string `"52.21..."` or a number `52.21...` depending on the OData $metadata declaration of the extension field. v0.26.20 replaces the `firstString` call with a local `pickCoord` helper that: (a) accepts both string and number values; (b) requires the value to contain a decimal point (rules out integer codes / scores that the regex tier might have accidentally caught); (c) parses to a float and returns both the value and the source field name. Also: **logs every successful coord match** (once per `latSource|lonSource` pair per session) — previously only wildcard-tier matches were logged. Now even canonical matches log: `[c4s] map: matched lat/lon — lat=KUT_RPlatitude_KUT (=42.4808132), lon=KUT_RPlongitude_KUT (=-71.2636443)`. Single-glance confirmation in the console that the right field was used and the right value was extracted. **Net effect**: removes the last possible failure mode where lat/lon values exist on the entity but were typed as numbers instead of strings (or vice-versa).
- **v0.26.19** — **Added tenant-specific KUT field names to the canonical lat/lon list (Thermo Fisher tenant).** Reporter ran the v0.26.16 "Inspect first ticket" diagnostic and surfaced the actual KUT field names on the Thermo Fisher tenant: `KUT_RPlatitude_KUT` = `42.4808132` and `KUT_RPlongitude_KUT` = `-71.2636443` (RP = Registered Product). The v0.26.18 boundary regex `/(^|[_A-Z])lat(itude)?($|[_A-Z])/` should already match `KUT_RPlatitude_KUT` (the `P` before `lat` satisfies the `[_A-Z]` boundary), but adding the explicit field names to the canonical first-tier list is essentially free and removes any dependency on the wildcard logic for this tenant. Added: `KUT_RPlatitude_KUT`, `KUT_Latitude_KUT`, `KUT_Lat_KUT`, `Z_Latitude` for latitude; matching set for longitude. **Net effect**: Thermo Fisher tenant now matches lat/lon on the first tier, the fastest path. Other tenants with `KUT_*` extension fields will still be caught by the boundary regex (Tier B) unless they use one of these specific names, in which case they'll also hit Tier A directly.
- **v0.26.18** — **Fix: markers were rendering in Africa instead of where the tickets are; tile-loading 404s on negative tile coords.** Reporter screenshot: ticket 4430921 in Bedford MA was producing 153 markers clustered in West/Central Africa, with the status row honestly reporting *"153 via SAP coords"* — meaning my wildcard-field-match had falsely picked up something. **Root cause**: v0.26.16's regex `/lat(itude)?/i` is a substring match — it matched ANY field whose name contains "lat" anywhere, including `Translation`, `EscalationStatus`, `RelatedID`. Those fields often hold small numeric values (status codes 1-9, scores 0.0-1.0, etc.) that pass the `|v| ≤ 180` sanity check and become bogus coordinates. Different ticket → different fake field value → 88 different "locations" sprinkled across the world. **Fix**: tighter boundary-aware regex `/(^|[_A-Z])lat(itude)?($|[_A-Z])/` — requires "Lat"/"Latitude" to sit at start-of-string, after underscore, or at a CamelCase boundary (capital letter). Matches: `Latitude`, `GeoLatitude`, `Z_Latitude`, `Latitude_KUT`, `LatitudeMeasure`. Doesn't match: `Translation`, `Escalation`, `RelatedID`. **Plus stricter value sanity**: (a) lat must be in `[-90, 90]` (not just ≤180); lon in `[-180, 180]`; (b) value must contain a decimal point (`-71.2636443` ✓, `5` ✗) — real coordinates always have 4-6 decimal places of precision, ID/code/score fields don't; (c) `(0, 0)` is rejected as null-island, since SAP returns it for un-geocoded tickets and we'd rather show no marker than place every un-geocoded record off the coast of Africa. **Also fixed**: `undefined.tile.openstreetmap.org/<z>/<x>/<y>.png` 404s visible in the DevTools network tab. Cause: JS `%` operator returns negative for negative operands (e.g. `-1 % 3 === -1`), so the subdomain lookup `["a","b","c"][(tx+ty) % 3]` returned `undefined` when the user panned far west or north. Replaced `(tx+ty) % 3` with `Math.abs(tx+ty) % 3` — guarantees a valid index. Tiles now load cleanly at every pan position.
- **v0.26.17** — **Smart-strip retry — adapts to ANY tenant field-name variance automatically.** Reporter screenshot showed `Query failed: HTTP 500 · "Property ServiceLocationCity not found in type 'ServiceRequest'"`. The cross-product fallback chain from v0.26.15 didn't help because it only knew about serial-field and state-field alternatives — `ServiceLocationCity` wasn't in any chain. Lesson: enumerating every possible field name doesn't scale. New approach: **parse the SAP error message, identify the offending property, and either swap to an alternative field name or strip the clause entirely, then retry**. Up to 8 attempts per search (covers tenants where 3+ different filter fields aren't exposed simultaneously). Each clause is annotated with `kind` (serial / state / city / base) + an `alternatives` array of field names to try before dropping. Pattern detection covers four common SAP error shapes: *"Property X not found"*, *"could not be resolved: X"*, *"Unknown property X"*, *"property X doesn't exist"*. Smart enough to keep the operator shape — when stripping `ServiceLocationCity` from a `substringof('92591', ServiceLocationCity)` clause, swap to `substringof('92591', ServiceLocationCityName)` not `ServiceLocationCityName eq '92591'` (different operator semantics). **City clause now carries alternatives**: ServiceLocationCity → ServiceLocationCityName → CityName → City → ServiceLocationCustomerCityName → IncidentLocationCity → drop. **Status row honest about what happened**: *"remapped: ServiceLocationCity→ServiceLocationCityName"* when a swap succeeded; *"dropped: city (not exposed by tenant)"* when no alternative worked. **Console diagnostic dump now includes** the full `finalClauses` array + `switchedFields` + `droppedKinds` for any post-mortem. **Net effect**: the extension now adapts to arbitrary tenant schema variance without code changes — a tenant that names city as `ZCityName_KUT` will get its city filter dropped automatically (smart-strip can't guess KUT names) but the query still succeeds, returning broader results with a clear "city filter dropped" hint in the status row.
- **v0.26.16** — **KUT (Key User Tool) extension-field detection for Latitude/Longitude.** Reporter screenshot showed SAP UI displaying `Latitude: 42.4808132` and `longitude: -71.2636443` on ticket 4430921, but the map status said *"197 tickets had an address but no SAP-provided Latitude/Longitude"* — meaning the OData response wasn't surfacing the values under the canonical field names. Almost certainly because they're **KUT (Key User Tool) extension fields** — custom fields added by the tenant admin that SAP surfaces in the UI but exposes via non-standard property names (e.g., `Z_Latitude`, `Latitude_KUT`, `LatitudeMeasure_Z`, or inside a `ServiceRequestExtension` navigation property). Three improvements: (1) **Wildcard regex fallback** in `extractTicketLocation` — when the canonical field-name list misses, scan every key on the ticket entity for `/lat(itude)?/i` and `/lon(g(itude)?)?/i`, casting the value to float and accepting if `|v| ≤ 180`. Catches `Z_Latitude`, `Latitude_KUT`, `LatitudeMeasureExtended`, etc. without needing tenant-specific configuration. Float-coerces only — won't accidentally match `LatencyStatusCode` because that doesn't parse to a number in the lat/lon range. (2) **Diagnostic dumps**: once-per-ticket-per-session `console.debug` logs (a) the location-shaped keys with values when lat/lon are missing — instantly tells the developer which custom field carries the data; (b) a one-time log when a wildcard match was used, naming the field, so the operator can verify in the console it picked the right one. (3) **"Inspect first ticket →" button** in the no-coords status row — clicking it logs the full ticket entity object + sorted key list + filtered location-shaped keys to console. Three logs in one click — the field name jumps out instantly. **Result**: tenants using KUT extension fields for coordinates now Just Work without a code change; tenants where the wildcard doesn't catch get a click-button diagnostic so the next code update can add the specific field name.
- **v0.26.15** — **Multi-field serial fallback + ticket-ID guard at apply time.** Reporter: *"i still see ticket id in serial field also the serial number field search not working"*. Two compounded gaps left from v0.26.14: (a) the ticket-ID guard only fired in the `/map` slash handler's auto-derive path — it did NOT fire when a stale chip persisted across sessions or when the user typed the wrong value into the filter input directly; (b) the v0.26.14 serial-drop fallback was binary (use SerialID or drop) — but tenants vary on serial field name (some use `EquipmentID`, some `InstallationPointID`, some `WarrantyID`), so dropping was a coarse remedy that lost the user's filter intent. Three changes: (1) **Multi-field serial fallback chain** in `applyMapFilters` — replaced the single-clause path with a cross-product loop over `[SerialID, EquipmentID, InstallationPointID, WarrantyID, InstalledBaseID, null]` × `[ServiceLocationStateCode, ServiceLocationState, null]`. Each combination is one SAP call; stops on first success. Net effect: a tenant where the equipment serial lives in `EquipmentID` now matches automatically without the operator having to know. Status row surfaces the discovery: *"serial matched on EquipmentID"*. (2) **Apply-time ticket-ID guard** — `applyMapFilters` now compares the filter value against `$("prompt").dataset.detectedTicket` and refuses to use it when they match, regardless of whether the value got there via auto-derive, stale chip, or user typing. Clears the input field + chip + shows status: *"Ignored serial "4430921" — it's the ticket ID, not an equipment serial. Showing all sites instead."* Console.warn fires so the diagnostic is in DevTools too. (3) **Per-search console.debug diagnostic** — every map search now logs a structured object: `{ serialInput, serialFieldUsed, serialDropped, stateScope, stateFieldUsed, stateFellBack, effectiveTop, rowsReturned }` — paste-able into bug reports, makes "why did I see N tickets?" answerable in one read. **Net effect**: on the reporter's screenshot scenario (ticket 4430921 with stale serial=4430921 in the filter), the next Apply will silently swap the filter to empty, show all customer sites, and clearly flag what happened — instead of a 500 + zero markers.
- **v0.26.14** — **Fix: `/map` auto-locked on the ticket ID instead of the equipment serial → SAP 500 → empty map.** Reporter opened `/map` on ticket 4430921 (LIFE TECHNOLOGIES). The scope chip showed `SERIAL: 4430921` (the ticket's own ID, not the equipment serial) and SAP returned HTTP 500 with *"P..."* (almost certainly `Property 'SerialID' not found` or a parse error on that value). Root cause: on misconfigured tenants `pickSerialId()` can pick up a field that contains the ticket's own ID — usually because the equipment record isn't populated and SAP defaults `SerialID` to the request ID. The auto-derive then silently filtered every map query to a no-match condition. **Three fixes**: (1) **Guard against ticketId match** in the `/map` slash handler — if `pickSerialId(rawEntity) || t.serial` returns a value equal to the ticket's own ID, treat it as "no serial available" and let the map show all sites for the customer instead of locking to a dead value. ~10 lines, ticket-ID resolution uses the existing fallback chain (`t.id`, `lookData.resolved.ticketId`, `lookData.ticket.ID`, `ticketHint`). (2) **Last-ditch retry-without-serial fallback** in `applyMapFilters` — after the state-narrowing fallback chain (v0.26.13) exhausts, if there's still a serial clause AND every attempt failed, retry once more with the serial clause removed. The serial filter is the single most-failure-prone clause across tenants; dropping it lets the operator see *something* (broader than they asked for, but actionable) instead of an opaque 500. Status row reports it: *"serial filter dropped (SAP rejected the value)"*. (3) **Better error display** — the previous code showed `Query failed: HTTP 500 · { "error": "SAP C4C request failed with HTTP 400: {\"error\":{\"code\":\"\",...` truncated mid-JSON. Now parses the nested SAP response and shows just the human-readable message: `Query failed: HTTP 500 · SAP: "Property 'SerialID' not found in type 'ServiceRequest'"` — actionable, scannable. Falls back to raw truncation only when the pattern doesn't match. **Net effect**: misconfigured tenants no longer break the map silently — they show all sites for the customer with a clear "we dropped your serial filter because SAP rejected it" hint instead of a blank screen with a cryptic 500.
- **v0.26.13** — **State-narrowed radius search — works on every tenant, drops scope ~50× server-side.** v0.26.11–12 gave up on server-side narrowing entirely (bbox approach was too fragile across tenants where Lat/Lon weren't filterable). The result worked but was capped at top=200 globally, which missed most tickets in busy regions. This release reintroduces server-side narrowing — but via `ServiceLocationStateCode` instead of Lat/Lon, because state codes are universally exposed + filterable on every tenant we've seen. **The flow**: (1) when in radius mode, figure out which state the search center is in — for marker-click "Find nearby" we read it off the source ticket directly (no extra API call); for city/zipcode + radius we pre-geocode via Nominatim's `addressdetails=1` to extract the state code from the response. (2) Add `ServiceLocationStateCode eq 'NC'` to the OData filter. (3) **Tenant-variance fallback chain** — try `ServiceLocationStateCode` first, then `ServiceLocationState`, then fall through to a global scan. Each attempt is a separate SAP call but only fires on 400/500 (field-not-found shape); 401/403/etc bail out immediately. (4) Status row reports the scope honestly: *"scoped to state: NC"* on success, *"state filter unavailable on this tenant — global scan"* on full fallback. **Why this is the right choice** (full reasoning in the v0.26.11/12 → v0.26.13 conversation thread): bbox approach (v0.26.9-10) failed on tenant Lat/Lon filterability variance; spatial-index approach (#5) needs Postgres+PostGIS infra + ETL pipeline + multi-tenant data isolation review — week+ of work and turns the extension into a data processor (GDPR/SOC2 implications). State-narrowed is the sweet spot: works on every tenant, ~80 lines of code, ~50× scope reduction (US has 50 states; typical tenant's per-state pool is a few thousand tickets vs millions globally). **Known limit**: a 100-mile circle near a state border misses out-of-state markers (we filter on the center's state only). v0.26.14 will add neighbour-state queries when the radius spans borders; for now status row hints at this. Skipped-Nominatim and date-filter-dropped diagnostics from v0.26.12 still apply. **Net effect**: radius search now narrows server-side reliably on every tenant, runs in ~2s end-to-end, and surfaces honest diagnostics about what it scoped and how.
- **v0.26.12** — **Radius search perf: "it's taking forever" → ~3s instead of multiple minutes.** v0.26.11's `top=500` + Nominatim-fallback combination was the perf killer — Nominatim's public rate limit is **1 req/sec** (their policy), so a 200-unique-address candidate pool meant 3+ minutes of waiting before the map rendered anything. Two changes: (1) **`top` reduced from 500 → 200** in radius mode — SAP fetch finishes in ~2s instead of ~10s; (2) **skip Nominatim entirely in radius mode** — only render tickets with SAP-provided `Latitude`/`Longitude`. Tickets that have an address but no precise coords get counted into a new "skipped for speed" bucket. Justification: in a radius search the operator wants the answer FAST, and a ticket with no SAP-verified coords can't reliably participate (we'd be guessing lat/lon from "Frankfurt" — ambiguous and slow). For non-radius `/map` views (default exploration mode), the full Nominatim fallback chain still runs — that path is about completeness, not "is this nearby?" **Status row honesty**: `scanned 187, 142 with SAP coords · 45 skipped (no SAP coords, geocoding too slow for radius)` so the operator sees the trade-off explicitly. New helpful-error variant when 0 of N candidates had SAP coords: *"45 tickets had an address but no SAP-provided Latitude/Longitude. Try without radius for slower-but-complete geocoding."* — points at the right escape hatch instead of just saying "nothing found." **Net effect**: a radius click now resolves in ~3s on a well-populated tenant; on a poorly-populated tenant it still finishes in ~3s but tells the operator clearly what wasn't geocoded and why.
- **v0.26.11** — **Radius search rebuilt: dropped the bbox approach entirely, switched to wide-pool + post-filter only.** v0.26.9 + v0.26.10 tried two server-side narrowing tricks (`Latitude ge X and Latitude le Y` bbox clauses, then auto-retry without bbox on failure). Both proved too fragile: the bbox path 500s on tenants where Lat/Lon are non-filterable; the retry left only a 200-row candidate pool which still wasn't enough. Reporter: *"100 mile radius but no tickets found"* → *"it still not working? any other way?"* So we tore out the bbox path completely. New radius behaviour: (1) **`top` bumped from 200 → 500** when radius > 0 — gives the haversine post-filter a much wider candidate pool to work with; (2) **date filter dropped** when radius > 0 — for "what tickets exist near this site?" the operator almost always wants the full history, not just last 30 days; a 2019 ticket on the same equipment is still a relevant signal. The OData query carries only status / priority / serial / city — no geographic clauses at all. The haversine post-filter does ALL the geographic narrowing client-side, reliably, on every tenant regardless of how they typed Latitude/Longitude. **New diagnostic in the status row**: `scanned 487, 142 had a location` — tells the operator how many of the 500 candidates actually had service-location data (huge signal for "is this tenant even populating ServiceLocation fields?"). When in radius mode it also says `full history (date filter dropped for radius)` so the operator understands why they're seeing older tickets. **Two new helpful-error paths**: (a) scan returned 0 tickets with location → "*This tenant may not populate ServiceLocation fields. Ask your SAP admin to confirm location fields are mapped.*"; (b) scan had tickets with locations but none within radius → "*Scanned 487 tickets (142 with locations) — none within 100 mi. Try a larger radius.*" — explicit, actionable, no more silent zero-marker maps. Tradeoff: scanning 500 instead of 50 adds ~1-2s; acceptable for an explicit user action (Apply button click) and far better than wrong-answer-fast. The simpler architecture (just `top=500` + post-filter) is also easier to reason about + works identically everywhere. **Net effect**: radius search now works on every tenant, with honest "here's what we looked at, here's why we got what we got" diagnostics in the status row.
- **v0.26.10** — **Fix: `Query failed: HTTP 500` when the radius bbox isn't supported on the tenant.** v0.26.9 added `Latitude ge X and Latitude le Y and Longitude ge A and Longitude le B` to the OData filter when in "Find nearby" mode. On tenants where SAP exposes `Latitude` / `Longitude` as **read-only / non-filterable properties** (or as string types that can't be compared with `ge`/`le`), the OData parser rejects the query with a 500. The user saw *"Query failed: HTTP 500"* and the map showed nothing. Fix: wrapped the search_tickets call in a two-attempt pattern. **Attempt 1**: with bbox + everything. **Attempt 2** (only if 1 failed AND bbox clauses were added): retry without the bbox clauses — keeps all the other filters (status / priority / date / serial / city), so it's still constrained, just not geographically constrained server-side. The radius post-filter still runs client-side (with `top=200` for a generous candidate pool — the v0.26.9 bump kicks in regardless of bbox). Status row reports the fallback: *"15 tickets at 6 locations · from 187 candidates (bbox not supported on this tenant — global search)"* instead of *"in bbox"*. The fallback is per-call (no permanent state), so if a later query has different filters that don't break the bbox, it'll be attempted again. **Net effect**: the radius search now degrades gracefully on every tenant — full server-side narrowing where supported, post-filter fallback where not. No more 500s.
- **v0.26.9** — **Fix: radius search returned 2-4 candidates instead of "everything within 100 mi."** Reporter clicked "Find nearby · 100 mi" on Ann Arbor and saw *"2 tickets at 1 location · 2 outside 100 mi radius"* — only 4 candidates total even though the tenant has 3.6M tickets. Root cause: the radius search re-used the user's "Max tickets to map" value (typically 50) for the OData `top` and added no geographic narrowing on the server, so SAP returned the 50 most-recent open tickets **globally**. Most of those weren't anywhere near the click point, so the haversine post-filter dropped almost all of them. Two-part fix: (1) when radius > 0, **bump `top` to max(user's value, 200)** so the candidate pool is genuinely wide; (2) when the radius center is known directly (the "Find nearby" path sets `mapState.manualCenter` with lat/lon from the clicked marker), **add a bounding-box OData clause** (`Latitude ge X and Latitude le Y and Longitude ge A and Longitude le B`) — computed from the radius in km using 111.32 km/°, slightly wider than the actual circle so the haversine post-filter still does the precise final trim. Net effect: server-side geographic narrowing → much higher hit rate, and `top=200` ensures the catchment is generous. **City + radius** (where coords aren't known until after geocoding) still benefits from the `top=200` bump even though it can't use the bbox. Status row now reports the candidate-pool size when in radius mode: *"15 tickets at 6 locations · from 87 candidates in bbox · 22 outside 100 mi radius"* so the operator can see SAP actually returned more candidates than just what's visible after filtering. **Caveat**: the bbox filter assumes the tenant's `ServiceRequest` entity exposes `Latitude` + `Longitude` fields (your screenshots confirmed it does — values like 36.0996925 / -78.4098932). On tenants that use different field names (`LatitudeMeasure`, `ServiceLocationLatitude`, etc.), the shim's property-not-found retry strips the offending clauses; the search degrades to the city-style fallback (top=200, post-filter only). If that ever becomes an issue, we'd add multi-field fallback for the bbox.
- **v0.26.8** — **`/map` — address-based radius search + km/mi unit toggle + "Find tickets nearby" on every marker.** Three related upgrades that turn the radius filter into a service-location proximity search instead of just a city-around lookup. (1) **City filter relabeled to "City or service-location address"** — Nominatim already accepts full addresses (`"1809 FENIL-CORSIER, CH"`, `"77 Perrys Chapel Church Rd, Franklinton NC"`), we just weren't telling users that. Placeholder + hint text updated to reflect both modes. (2) **km/mi unit toggle** sits next to the radius dropdown — radio-style pill with "km" / "mi" — service-ops in the US thinks in miles, EU in km, and the toggle lets either pick the natural unit. Internally still km (haversine math + circle rendering stay single-unit) but the user-facing input + status text honor the chosen unit. Status now says "*9 outside 50 mi radius*" instead of forcing-back to km. (3) **"Find other tickets nearby" panel inside every marker popup** — four quick-action buttons (25 mi / 50 mi / 100 mi / 250 mi) recenter the radius filter on THIS marker's SAP-verified lat/lon (no geocoding needed — coords are already known precisely), clear the city + serial filters, set radius dropdown + mile-unit toggle, and re-run the search. New state on mapState: `manualCenter = { lat, lon, label }` wins over the city-geocoded center when present, so a marker-click-driven radius search bypasses Nominatim entirely. Auto-clears on Apply-filters (so changing the city + clicking Apply doesn't stay stuck on the prior marker's coordinates) and on the list-view button. **Solves**: the reporter's "Zone Industrielle B / 1809 FENIL-CORSIER / CH" use case — click that ticket's marker → click `100 mi` → see every ticket within 100 miles of that exact service location, regardless of customer or city. The radius circle + center pin redraw correctly with the unit + tooltip showing miles when picked.
- **v0.26.7** — **Fix: list-view scrape failing on SAP Service Cloud V2 (Industry Cloud for Service / IfS).** The reporter's screenshot showed an "All Tickets (3,661,325)" list view rendering rows with IDs 4431041, 4431040, … but the scrape still returned ~zero IDs. Root cause: SAP V2 renders the list inside web components with **Shadow DOM** roots. `document.querySelectorAll` doesn't pierce shadow roots by default, so v0.26.6's selectors silently matched zero elements inside the actual list grid. Fix: rewrote the injected scraper as a **recursive shadow-aware walker** — `walkAllElements(root)` yields every element AND descends into `element.shadowRoot` when present. The walker visits the document once and applies all selector tests per element (faster than re-running querySelectorAll per selector). **Expanded the cell selectors** for V2: `[role="gridcell"]`, `[role="cell"]`, `[data-test-id]`, `[data-id]`, plus the existing UI5 classes (`.sapMText`, `.sapMLnk`, `.sapMLabel`, `.sapMTitle`) and generic `td / th / span / a`. Tier-tracked counts get returned in the diagnostic payload so we can see which tier matched. **Improved diagnostic surfacing**: the empty-result error in the sidebar now shows inline `Scanned N frames (M chars total). Open DevTools → Console + look for [c4s] scrapeTicketIdsFromListView for full details.` The console log includes per-frame `{ url, title, idCount, bodyTextLength, selectorCounts }` so the next tenant variance has a clear breadcrumb. Also added a `textContent` fallback (alongside `innerText`) for cases where SAP virtualizes the list and IDs render in offscreen rows that `innerText` doesn't see. Will land the actual data the next time the reporter clicks 📋 Use list-view tickets.
- **v0.26.6** — **Fix: "Use list-view tickets" always showed only 3 markers.** Root cause: `scrapeTicketIdsFromListView` called `chrome.scripting.executeScript({ allFrames: true })` — which returns an **array of results, one per frame** — but the code destructured `const [result] = ...` and read only the first frame. SAP C4C renders the actual ticket list inside a nested iframe; the top frame returned 0-3 stray numerics from breadcrumb / chrome elements while the real list data lived in a child frame whose result was thrown away. The map ended up with whatever 3-or-fewer IDs the top frame happened to contain (usually unrelated to the real list view). Fix: changed to `frameResults.flatMap(r => r?.result?.ids || [])` so IDs aggregate across every frame; the deduplicated union is what gets passed to `fetchTicketsByIds`. Each frame's injected function now also returns `{ frameUrl, frameOrigin, docTitle }` alongside the IDs, and the orchestrator logs the "best frame" (the one that contributed the most IDs) at `console.debug` so diagnostic visibility is in place for the next tenant variance. Separate UX clarification: the meta + status text now says **"X tickets at Y locations"** instead of "Y mapped" — many tickets cluster at the same site (common when `/map`-by-customer or `/map`-by-serial pulls a long history at one location), so a count like "47 tickets at 3 locations" reads correctly instead of looking like "only 3 tickets made it". The total-tickets-on-map number is summed from each marker's tickets array — counts every ticket including the clustered ones.
- **v0.26.5** — **`/map` — City + Radius filters added to the sidebar.** Two new filter inputs and one new visual overlay. **City** (text input, placeholder `e.g. Frankfurt`) — applies a server-side `substringof('<city>', ServiceLocationCity)` OData clause so the SAP query returns only tickets whose service location is in that city. Substring match catches "Frankfurt" → "Frankfurt am Main", "Frankfurt (Oder)", etc. **Radius around city** (dropdown: `No radius filter` / 10 / 25 / 50 / 100 / 250 / 500 / 1000 km) — after geocoding the markers, the city is also geocoded to a center point and any marker further than the chosen radius is dropped. New helper `haversineKm(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2)` for great-circle distance (~0.5% accurate at city scale, fine for ops use). New visual elements on the map: a **blue center pin** at the city's geocoded location + a **dashed blue circle** showing the radius boundary (pixel radius computed from km via 111.32 km/° latitude, scaled by zoom). The auto-fit bounding box now includes the center pin + the four cardinal extents of the radius circle, so the auto-zoom shows the full search area even when zero tickets fall within (so the operator sees *where* they searched, not just an empty map). Status row reports the new state: e.g., *"4 locations mapped · 9 outside radius · 1 not geocodable"*. Edge cases handled: city set but radius=0 → server-side city filter only (no client-side trim, no center marker); radius set but no city → ignored with a status nudge to enter a city; city fails to geocode (rare) → status flags it and the radius filter is skipped. The 📋 Use list-view tickets button now also clears the city + radius filters (those tickets may span any geography). All state lives on `mapState.center_pin` so the renderer can re-render correctly during pan/zoom interactions.
- **v0.26.4** — **`/map` — serial number replaces customer name as the scoping filter.** Better fit for service ops: one piece of equipment generates many tickets over its lifecycle (sometimes at multiple sites if it gets moved), so the natural "what does this equipment's service map look like?" question keys on the serial, not the customer. Changes: (1) filter sidebar's "Customer name contains" input replaced with **Serial number** input (placeholder `e.g. 40875702`, hint text *"Maps all tickets for this equipment serial across customers + sites."*); (2) OData filter clause changed from `substringof('<name>', BuyerPartyName)` to `SerialID eq '<serial>'` (exact match — more selective + faster); (3) `/map` slash handler auto-derives the serial from the current ticket via `pickSerialId(rawEntity) || t.serial` (same field-fallback chain `/warranty` uses — sniffs SerialID / EquipmentID / WarrantyID / InstallationPointID); (4) inline arg semantics flipped: `/map 40875702` scopes to that serial (was: customer name); (5) scope chip in the toolbar now reads **🎯 SERIAL: 40875702 [×]** (auto-locked from context) or **🔧 SERIAL: 40875702 [×]** (manually set); serial rendered in monospace for ID legibility; (6) the **📋 Use list-view tickets** button now clears the serial filter (list-view tickets often span multiple serials); (7) clicking × on the chip clears the serial + re-applies. The customer-name filter is gone — if someone wants "all CureVac tickets," they should use the list-view button on a list view already filtered to that customer. Serial-driven is the right primary scope for service-ops use cases.
- **v0.26.3** — **`/map` — auto-lock scope to the current ticket's customer + visible scope chip with one-click unlock.** When `/map` opens from a ticket / account / contract screen with no inline arg, it now looks up the active ticket and pre-fills the customer filter with that ticket's `BuyerPartyName` (with the usual wide-fallback chain — `AccountPartyName`, `ProductRecipientPartyName`, `ServiceLocationName1`, etc.). New visible affordance: the map toolbar shows a **🎯 Locked to: \<customer\>** chip when auto-derived from context, or **📍 Locked to: \<customer\>** when set explicitly (e.g., the operator typed `/map CureVac`). Click the chip's **×** button to clear the lock — instantly re-applies the search across all customers. This replaces the implicit behavior where the customer filter sat hidden in the sidebar — the operator now sees, at a glance, "the map is scoped to NOVOZYMES NORTH AMERICA INC right now." When the operator clicks the **📋 Use list-view tickets** button (v0.26.2), the scope chip auto-clears too (list-view tickets often span multiple customers, so it'd be wrong to constrain the rendered markers). New helper `updateMapScopeChip({lockedCustomer, lockedFromContext})` — single source of truth for the chip's render state. `/map` slash handler refactored to accept ticketHint + look up the ticket entity for context derivation. Returns the panel result with `lockedCustomer` + `lockedFromContext` flags; `renderResult` plumbs both through to `openMapPanel`. CSS for `.map-scope-chip` + `.map-scope-chip-icon` + `.map-scope-chip-label` + `.map-scope-chip-clear` — gradient-toolbar-friendly translucent chip with a rounded × pill. **Solves**: *"map view is locked for customer"* — yes, now the map respects the customer context you're already on, AND visibly shows it AND lets you unlock with one click.
- **v0.26.2** — **`/map` — "Use list-view tickets" button: scrape & map the tickets currently visible on SAP.** Closes the gap where the map ran a fresh `search_tickets` query independent of the SAP ticket list the user was actually looking at (reporter's screenshot showed 50 ticket markers when they were on a 9-row filtered list view). New button **📋 Use list-view tickets** in the map filter sidebar (below the existing Apply filters). Click → injects a content script into the active SAP tab via `chrome.scripting.executeScript({ allFrames: true })` → scrapes ticket IDs from the rendered list rows. **Scraping strategy** is two-tier: (1) targeted SAP UI5 selectors — `.sapMListItem`, `[role="row"]`, `.sapMLIBContent`, `tr` — drilling into cells via `td / .sapMText / .sapMLnk / .sapMLabel / span` and matching pure-digit content of 6-10 chars (the C4C ticket ID shape); (2) regex fallback over `document.body.innerText` when the UI5 markup doesn't match (catches tenants with custom list-view layouts). Deduped across iframes. After scraping, fires N parallel `search_tickets` calls (one filter per ID — dodges SAP's OR-chain limit), aggregates the entity results, then feeds them into the existing `renderMapMarkersFromRows()` helper — so the same lat/lon precedence, structured-address geocoding, source-badge UI, and bounding-box auto-zoom work identically. Live status bar reports: *"Scraping…"* → *"Found 9 ticket IDs · fetching entities…"* → *"Fetched 7/9 ticket entities…"* → final mapped count. Refactor: extracted `renderMapMarkersFromRows(rows, ...)` from the inline `applyMapFilters` body so list-view import and filter-based search share the rendering path verbatim. New helper `scrapeTicketIdsFromListView()` (~50 lines) for the cross-frame DOM dig. New helper `fetchTicketsByIds(ids, endpoint, headers, onProgress)` for the parallel-lookup pattern. Button auto-disables during fetch + re-enables on completion. **Result**: stand on any SAP list view (All Tickets, Open Tickets, custom query, MyArea, etc.) → click 📋 Use list-view tickets → those specific tickets get mapped, regardless of what global filters were applied. Bypasses the local filter sidebar entirely for this path.
- **v0.26.1** — **`/map` now uses SAP's actual service-location address + lat/lon.** Tickets in SAP C4C carry a populated "Service Location" block — house number, street, city, state, postal code, country, **and pre-computed Latitude / Longitude when SAP has geocoded the address**. v0.26.0 ignored all of that and only used the customer name as the geocode query. v0.26.1 fixes this with a tiered strategy: (1) **If the ticket already has `Latitude` + `Longitude`** (which it does most of the time once the address is verified in SAP) → use directly, **ZERO Nominatim calls**. (2) **Else if structured address fields are present** → use Nominatim's `/search?street=…&city=…&postalcode=…&country=…` parameters — much more accurate than free-text name lookup. (3) **Else** → fall back to free-text customer name (the v0.26.0 behavior). New helper `extractTicketLocation(ticket)` sniffs ~24 field-name variants per piece (Latitude / LatitudeMeasure / ServiceLocationLatitude / IncidentLocationLatitude / GeoLatitude / …) to handle SAP tenant variance. Marker **grouping changed from "by customer name" to "by physical location"** — tickets at the same lat/lon (rounded to 5 decimal places, ≈ 1 m precision) cluster onto one marker regardless of whose `BuyerPartyName` is set. The popup now shows the **street address as the primary label** (the actually-useful identifier for a service location) with the customer as italic subtitle, plus a colored source badge: green "✓ SAP coords" when SAP gave us the precise lat/lon, blue "geocoded" when Nominatim resolved the structured address, amber "name lookup" when we fell back to name-only search. Status row reports the breakdown: e.g., *"47 tickets · 12 locations · 9 pre-geocoded by SAP"* — so the operator knows how many of the markers are precision-pinpoints vs approximations. Cache key now distinguishes `addr:` vs `name:` lookups so re-running with structured data doesn't hit a stale name-cache miss. **Result**: a map at NOVOZYMES NORTH AMERICA INC's site (the reporter's example) now pin-drops on `77 PERRYS CHAPEL CHURCH RD, FRANKLINTON, NC 27525` with lat 36.0996925 / lon -78.4098932 — directly from the ticket's own fields, zero geocoding latency.
- **v0.26.0** — **`/map` — interactive service-location map with filter sidebar.** Full-panel OSM tile map of customer ticket locations, completely self-contained — no Leaflet or external map library dependency. Pure HTML/CSS/JS map renderer (~400 lines): web-Mercator tile-coord math, tile-grid layout from `tile.openstreetmap.org` (3 subdomain spread for load balancing), mouse-drag pan, wheel + button zoom (levels 2-18), red drop-pin markers with count badges, click-to-popup. Markers cluster tickets by customer name (one pin per customer, showing N-ticket count badge); click → popup lists up to 8 tickets at that location with "click to /triage" handoff. **Filter sidebar** (200px, left side): status checkboxes (open / closed), priority dropdown (any / 1 / 2 / 3 / 7), date-range select (last 7 / 30 / 90 / 365 days / no limit), customer-name substring search, max-tickets-to-map (25 / 50 / 100). "Apply filters" rebuilds the OData query, re-fetches tickets, re-geocodes unique customers, re-renders markers. **Geocoding via Nominatim** (nominatim.openstreetmap.org — in manifest host_permissions), rate-limited to 1 req/sec via a serialized queue (Nominatim's published policy), cache-backed in `chrome.storage.local` with 30-day TTL keyed by lowercased customer name — geocodes a customer once, reuses across all map sessions. Cache misses also recorded (avoids re-querying obviously-bad names every visit). Live status row reports "Geocoded N/M (K not found)" during the run. Bounding-box auto-zoom: after geocoding, map auto-fits to the spread of markers (zoom level chosen by lat/lon range heuristic). Manifest: added `nominatim.openstreetmap.org/*` + `*.tile.openstreetmap.org/*` to `host_permissions`. New built-in template **🗺 Map view** (ticket / account / list / all screens). Slash chip order + `/help` + options-list updated. Inline arg `/map CureVac` pre-fills the customer-name filter. The map opens as a fixed-position full-bleed overlay (under the popup header), so the regular reply box / pulse card / etc. stay intact and the map can be dismissed to return to them. Solves "I'd love to see all our open service tickets on a map" — a manager / dispatch use case that hasn't been addressable with the chat-only UX.
- **v0.25.2** — **Fix: hyphenated slash commands (`/kb-resolve`, `/parts-check`, `/pm-kit-check`, `/order-parts`, `/close-out`, `/kb-ticket`) parsed incorrectly when invoked with an argument.** Root cause: `parseSlash()` used `/^\s*(\/\w+)\b\s*(.*)$/` — `\w` matches `[A-Za-z0-9_]` but NOT `-`, so `/kb-resolve 4430359` got split as `command="/kb"`, `rest="-resolve 4430359"`. The KB-search route then fired with the literal query string `"-resolve 4430359"` and predictably found nothing — produced the misleading *"No KB articles found (direct mode) · No matches for '-resolve 4430359'"* (visible in the reporter's screenshot). The bug only fired when the user passed an explicit ticket ID arg (e.g., `/kb-resolve 4430359`) instead of relying on auto-detection; bare `/kb-resolve` (the chip-click case) worked because there was no whitespace + suffix for the regex to misparse. Fix: changed the character class from `\w` to `[\w-]` so hyphens are part of the command name. Verified against 9 test cases including `/kb pump leak` (no hyphen, still works), `/kb-resolve 4430359`, `/kb-ticket`, `/parts-check`, `/pm-kit-check`, `/order-parts`, `/close-out`, `/visits 10`, and `/skills John Cochran`. Affected all hyphenated commands but only when invoked with an inline argument — chip / palette / template path was unaffected.
- **v0.25.1** — **Fix: "KB resolutions unavailable · No ticket context to derive queries" on tickets that clearly have a product + serial.** Root cause: `runDeepKbResolution` read `t.title / t.product / t.serial` from `normalizeTicket()`, which only sniffs a narrow set of canonical field names. On tenants where the product / serial sit in non-canonical fields (e.g. `Material`, `EquipmentID`, `InstallationPointID`), normalizeTicket returns empty strings, so the deep helper bailed out — even though the same `/knowledge` panel's external-KB-search section showed the values just fine (it uses `pickSerialId()` + a broader rawEntity lookup). Fix: pushed the broader fallback into the helper itself. `runDeepKbResolution` now accepts an optional `rawEntity` parameter; when present, it falls back to `pickSerialId(rawEntity)` plus a widened `firstString` over `[ProductID, ProductId, ProductName, ProductDescription, Material, MaterialID]` for product, `[SerialID, WarrantyID, InstallationPointID, EquipmentID]` for serial, and `[Name, SubjectName, Subject, Description, ProcessingTypeName, ServiceRequestSubject]` for title. Both call sites (`/kb-resolve` standalone and `/knowledge` section) now pass `rawEntity` through. Error message also widened — when ALL angles are still empty, it now hints "field names may be tenant-specific; share the rawEntity keys" instead of just "no context", so debugging the next tenant variance is one log line away.
- **v0.25.0** — **Universal panel-refresh button — manual rerun affordance for auto-actions.** A small `↻` icon now sits inside the header of every refreshable structured panel: **Customer Pulse card**, **`/kb-resolve` panel**, and the inline **KB RESOLUTIONS section** within `/knowledge`. Click → rotates 60° on hover, then spins 360° while the underlying slash command re-runs via `ask()`. The button is event-delegated globally at `document` level — a single click handler in `init()` catches `.panel-refresh` clicks regardless of which panel rendered them, with the command stored in `data-refresh-cmd`. New helper `panelRefreshButton(command, options)` returns the button HTML — used uniformly across renderers so future panels just `+ panelRefreshButton("/whatever")` to get the affordance. Two sizes: standard (22px, used in the kb-resolutions panel header) and `small` (18px, used inside section titles + the pulse-card header where space is tight). CSS animations: 200ms rotate-60° hover transform, 700ms continuous-spin during the in-flight ask(), 90% scale on click. **The UX gap this closes**: after auto-kb-resolve fires on ticket open, the operator has no fast way to re-run if the KB indexed a new article or if they tried a fix and want a fresh suggestion. Before v0.25.0: type `/kb-resolve` manually. After: one click. Same for pulse (e.g., after sending a customer email and wanting to see if sentiment-from-history changed) and the inline knowledge resolution section (when you scroll past the section and want fresh data without re-running all of `/knowledge`). Doesn't bypass result caches (e.g., pulse's 2-hour per-customer cache still wins) — that's a design call; cache-bust is a follow-up if needed.
- **v0.24.0** — **Auto-KB-resolve + `/knowledge` shows extracted resolutions inline.** Two integrations that promote v0.23.0's `/kb-resolve` from "a command you run" to "an answer that's just there." (1) **Auto-action sequencer extended**: `c4s.auto-kb-resolve` setting (off / once / always) joins auto-triage, auto-kb-ticket, and auto-pulse. Precedence: **auto-triage > auto-kb-resolve > auto-kb-ticket** (all three compete for the reply box; first to claim wins). Auto-pulse still runs independently (separate `#pulse-card` area). Auto-kb-resolve is silently skipped when KB direct mode isn't configured — no point firing a command that'd just info-fail. Same `once`-per-hour session dedup as the other auto-actions. New Options ▸ **Auto-fetch KB resolutions (deep)** radio group with the same off/once/always pattern. Banner reads "Deep-searching KB for resolutions…" while it runs. (2) **`/knowledge` now includes the resolutions inline**: when direct mode is set, the knowledge panel adds a new `🧠 KB RESOLUTIONS — FIX STEPS EXTRACTED` section between past-tickets-on-serial and the legacy KB-articles list. Per-article cards (same visual as `/kb-resolve` standalone — relevance badge, hit-on chips, summary, match reason, bulleted fix steps, "Open article ↗" link). Top-of-section summary banner. High-match-count callout in the section meta (`5 extracted · 2 high matches`). The legacy "KNOWLEDGE BASE ARTICLES" section is **suppressed when KB resolutions populated** (avoids showing the same articles twice — once as titles, once with full fix steps). Refactored: extracted `runDeepKbResolution(t, endpoint, headers)` as a reusable helper used by both `/kb-resolve` (returns kb-resolutions-panel) and `/knowledge` (returns inline section); extracted `renderKbResolutionCards(articles)` as a reusable HTML generator. **The flow now**: open a ticket → auto-kb-resolve fires (~10s) → reply box already shows the answer with fix steps; OR open a ticket → run `/knowledge` → see past tickets PLUS the extracted fix steps in one panel. No clicking through KB articles needed.
- **v0.23.0** — **`/kb-resolve` — multi-angle KB search + body fetch + LLM-extracted resolutions.** Where `/kb-ticket` returns *"here are 5 article titles,"* `/kb-resolve` returns *"here are the actual fix steps from the 3 most relevant articles."* **The flow**: (1) builds **5 query angles** from the ticket — title, product, serial, symptom keywords (title with serial numbers stripped), product+title combo; (2) fans out parallel `search_kb_articles` calls via the v0.22.0 KB direct mode (browser cookies, no Azure AD); (3) **merges + scores articles by multi-hit count** — articles that turned up on 3+ angles rank higher than single-angle hits; (4) for the top 5 ranked articles, fetches the full body via `<base>/@api/deki/pages/<pageId>/contents` (MindTouch) with HTML fallback to scrape `<article>`/`<main>` from the user-facing URL; (5) **single LLM call** at temperature 0.2 with all 5 article bodies (≤2500 chars each) + strict JSON output spec for `{ articles: [{ index, relevance, oneLineSummary, resolution, matchReason }] }`; (6) renders a new `kb-resolutions-panel` with per-article cards. **Cards show**: relevance badge (🟢 high / 🟡 medium / ⚪ low — colored border-left), hit-on chips (which query angles found this article), one-line article summary, *match reason* (why the LLM picked it for THIS ticket), `RESOLUTION / FIX STEPS` as a bulleted list (LLM extracts 3-8 actionable steps), and an *Open article ↗* link. When the body fetch fails (CORS / tenant-specific endpoint not exposed), shows a `⚠ Couldn't fetch body — extracted from snippet only` chip so the operator knows the answer is shallower. **Top-of-panel summary banner** says which articles are most relevant + why. **Queries-run chip row** below shows transparency about how we found these (e.g. `title: Pump head leaking`, `product: QC-HPLC-51`, `serial: 19439286`). Plain-text plan-text generator for Copy / Draft actions outputs the full structured snapshot. New built-in template **🧠 KB resolution** (ticket-screen only) mirrored in options.js. `/kb-resolve` added to slash chip order + `/help` text + isSlowSlash regex (gets the progress strip). **Requires KB direct mode configured** (v0.22.0) — falls back with a clear "configure direct mode in Options" nudge if not. **The differentiator**: turns the KB from a search engine into an *answer engine* — closes the "wait, what does that article actually SAY about fixing this?" loop without making the agent click through every link.
- **v0.22.0** — **KB direct mode — call the knowledge base from Chrome without Azure AD.** When the shim doesn't have Azure credentials configured (or you don't have Azure admin access to register an app), `/kb` and `/kb-ticket` can now call the external MindTouch KB **directly from the extension** using your existing browser session cookies. Set the KB base URL once in Options ▸ *KB direct mode* (default placeholder: `https://knowledge1.thermofisher.com`); the extension then routes KB queries to `<base>/@api/deki/site/search?format=json` with `credentials: "include"` so Chrome attaches the SSO cookies you already have from logging into the KB in another tab. Built-in MindTouch response parser handles both JSON (preferred) and XML (fallback) — extracts title / uri / snippet / score / id. Friendly 401 / 403 handler shows *"Not signed in to ${url} — open it in a tab, sign in via SSO, then re-run"* instead of a raw HTTP error. `/kb-ticket` does **client-side query expansion** (title + product + serial as separate parallel queries, deduped by URL) — same effect as the shim's smart-query path, but without ever touching Azure AD. New manifest fields: `host_permissions` pre-grants `knowledge1.thermofisher.com` + `*.thermofisher.com`; `optional_host_permissions: ["https://*/*"]` lets you set any KB URL via Options (`chrome.permissions.request` pops the per-host grant dialog at save time). New Options UI: KB URL field with a **Test direct call** button that fires a `?q=test&limit=1` probe and reports green ✅ / yellow ⚠ / red ❌ inline. The shim path is still the default (when `c4s.kb-direct-url` is blank); direct mode is opt-in. **Solves the "I don't have Azure access" blocker** — get KB search working in 30 seconds without any IT involvement, just by pasting your KB URL.
