- **v0.21.0** — **`/order-parts` — search catalogue, build cart, batch-commit to SAP ticket line items.** Closes the **grey-stock loop**: the existing `log_service_item` MCP tool (POSTs a `ServiceRequestItem` with `processingTypeCode: SRP1` — Part Consumption) was wired-but-unexercised; this release plugs it into the Chrome extension via a new interactive panel. **Two entry points**: (a) standalone `/order-parts` slash command (or **📦 Order parts** template chip on any ticket screen) opens a blank cart + search panel; (b) **📦 Order these parts (N)** CTA at the bottom of every `/parts-check` result pre-seeds the cart with all in-catalogue inferred parts so the dispatcher commits in two clicks (review → confirm). **Panel layout**: ticket header · `🔍 Search parts catalogue` (debounced 300ms `search_parts` calls, results render with stock badges + click-to-add) · `📋 Cart` (line items with ± quantity steppers + × remove + colored stock-vs-cart-qty warnings — `🔴 out — will back-order`, `🟡 only 2 on hand`, `✅ 12 on hand`) · `Order parts (N) / Cancel` action row. **Commit flow**: synchronous `window.confirm()` dialog with full line-item summary acknowledges the SAP write before firing. Then parallel `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` calls route each cart entry through `background.js` (with the tool allowlist gating `log_service_item` — added in this release). Each call returns ok/error; status row reports **all-success** (green), **partial** (yellow with per-part error details), or **all-failed** (red). Successful entries auto-remove from the cart so partial-failure recovery is one click. Module-scope `orderPartsState` carries the cart across re-renders; cleared on Cancel or full success. Excluded from reply-history strip (it's interactive state, not a snapshot). New built-in template **📦 Order parts** (ticket-screen only) mirrored in `options.js`. `/order-parts` added to slash chip order + `/help` text. **Solves the grey-stock + missing-service-history pain point at the moment of consumption** — when the tech uses parts on a visit, they get logged to the ticket as proper line items so SAP downstream (stock decrement, billing, contract entitlement matching) all flows correctly. Previously this required either manual SAP entry (often skipped) or extracting from the `/close-out` note text (text-only, doesn't trigger downstream automation).
- **v0.20.0** — **Draft email reply → Outlook (one click).** Each active thread inside the `/emails` summary panel now carries a gradient `✉ Draft reply` button in its header row. Click → an LLM-drafted reply pre-fills the existing inline reply editor (textarea + 🤝 / 💼 / ⚡ tone dial + Copy & open mail). Subject is auto-prefixed as `Re: <thread subject>` (de-dupes existing `Re:`/`Fwd:`/`AW:`/`SV:` chains). Recipient is best-effort scraped from the page-context block (first email-shaped string) — Outlook gets it pre-filled when present. Tone defaults are thread-aware: `frustrated` thread → empathetic, `urgent` → concise, `formal` → formal, else empathetic. The reply prompt is dedicated (separate from the customer-update prompt) — it references the thread's open ask + last 2 body previews so the LLM grounds the reply in actual conversation history instead of templating. **`Copy & open mail` now opens Outlook reliably**: switched from `window.open(mailto:)` (some Chrome profiles classify it as a popup and block) to a hidden anchor `<a href="mailto:…">` click, which Chrome routes to the OS-registered mail handler. Body still copied to clipboard first as a safety net for the 1800-char `mailto:` length cap that Outlook hits on long bodies (URL gets subject-only, body lands in clipboard for paste). Resolved threads have no button (nothing to action). Stale threads still get the button (someone might want to ping). Status toast names the recipient on success. Tone-dial re-rolls in email mode use the dedicated email-reply prompt so tone changes are coherent. Resolves: "How do I draft a reply to this email without leaving the panel?" — one click, Outlook opens, customer reply pre-filled.
- **v0.19.0** — **Email summary (`/emails`).** New slash command + screen type that turns the customer's `EMailCollection` into a structured one-screen digest: thread structure, open asks, sentiment, headline. Backend `searchEmails` extended with `includeBody: true` — fans out to SAP's `EMailItem` child entity per email to pull the actual body text (not just metadata), bounded by `top`. The slash handler walks every party-ID candidate on the active ticket/account, finds the customer's emails, normalises subjects (strips `Re:`/`Fwd:`/`AW:`/`SV:` etc.), groups into threads by subject root, sorts by most-recent activity, and feeds thread blocks + body previews (≤600 chars each) to the LLM with a strict JSON output spec for `{ summary, headline, sentiment, threads[], openAsks[] }`. New renderer `renderEmailSummaryPanel` lays out: (1) a sentiment-coloured headline banner (😊 positive / 😐 neutral / 😶 mixed / 😟 negative), (2) an **OPEN ASKS** section with severity-bordered cards showing who's waiting on whom + ask + thread context, (3) a **THREADS** stack with per-thread status badge (🟢 Active / ✓ Resolved / 💤 Stale), tone chip (💚 cordial · ⚠ frustrated · 🔴 urgent · etc.), parties, 2-3 sentence summary, optional open-ask callout, and last-activity line. New `email` screen type joins ticket/account/contract/etc. with `SCREEN_STRONG_KEYWORDS` patterns matching email-tab labels + `Re:`/`Fwd:` subject prefixes + email-from/to headers; `SCREEN_FIELD_HINTS` covers Email Subject / Sender / To / Body / Transmission Status. New built-in template **✉ Email summary**. Mirrored in options.js. Slash chip order + `/help` text updated. Activity strip integrated — runs `Searching emails at <customer>` → `Grouping N emails into threads` → `Summarising threads with LLM`.
- **v0.18.0** — **Reply history strip — never lose a past result again.** Every structured slash-command result + free-text reply now logs onto a horizontal chip strip above the reply box (`📌 Recent | 🗂 Knowledge view × | 🔧 Parts check × | 📍 Nearby PMs × | Clear`). The chip for the currently-displayed result is highlighted with the gradient brand colors; clicking any other chip instantly swaps the reply box content back to that result (cards re-render with all their action buttons re-wired — same as the original render). × on each chip drops just that entry; if you remove the active one, the strip auto-promotes the most-recent remaining. Cap: 5 items (oldest evicts). Resets when the operator navigates to a different ticket (via refreshContext) or hits the Clear button — so each ticket starts fresh. Pulse / Close-out / error / info kinds are NOT pushed onto history: pulse has its own always-visible `#pulse-card`, close-out is an interactive editor (restoring would discard edits), and error/info are transient. Implementation refactored the inline rendering switch in `ask()` into a reusable `renderResult(result)` helper — same rendering for live + restore. Solves the "I ran /knowledge, then /parts-check, and now I want to flip back to the knowledge view but I'd have to re-run the command and wait again" frustration.
- **v0.17.0** — **Live progress feedback · empty state · micro-animations.** Three UX polish drops in one release. (1) **Activity strip** — slow slash commands (`/parts-check`, `/pm-kit-check`, `/pulse`, `/knowledge`, `/close-out`, `/nearby`, `/visits`, `/warranty`, `/similar`) now stream a stack of step rows above the reply box as they run, each row going from ⌛ spinner → ✓ green check as it completes. The operator sees "Finding similar tickets → Reading resolutions (5 tickets) → Inferring parts with LLM → Checking warehouse stock (4 parts)" instead of a static "Asking…". Auto-fades 600ms after completion. (2) **Empty-state card** — when the popup opens without a ticket / pulse / reply on screen, a centered card with the ✨ AI sparkle mark surfaces three first-action chips: `⌘K Search commands & templates`, `📚 Search the knowledge base`, `❓ Explain this page`, plus the top 3 recent prompts as one-click re-fire buttons. Replaces the "blank textarea, what do I do?" first-open moment. Re-appears after clicking Clear when no ticket is detected. (3) **Micro-animations** — `fadeInUp` for pulse card / reply box / reply actions (with 80ms stagger on the action buttons), `slideDown` for the check-in bar appearing, a blinking caret (`▊`) appended to streaming reply text that disappears when the SSE stream finishes, hover lift + subtle 97% scale on press for primary buttons, hover glow on the gradient Ask button. Pure CSS — no animation libraries. Combined effect: the extension goes from "feels hand-built" to "feels designed."
- **v0.16.0** — **⌘K command palette.** Linear/Raycast-style fuzzy launcher overlaid on the popup. Press `Ctrl+K` / `Cmd+K` from anywhere in the popup (or click the gradient `⌘K Search` button in the screen indicator strip) to open a centered modal with: (a) every slash command filtered by current screen + sub-tab, (b) every built-in + user template, (c) the last 5 prompts from history — all in one filterable list. Real-time substring + fuzzy match scoring (label-prefix > label-contains > sub-contains > fuzzy in-order), arrow-key navigation, Enter to fire, Esc to close. Selecting a slash command auto-runs it; selecting a free-text template fills the textarea and waits for the operator to edit; selecting a recent prompt lands in textarea. Matched query letters highlighted yellow inside each row. Empty state shows a friendly "no matches" card with a hint. Backdrop is a blurred dark overlay with 120ms fade-in + 140ms card scale-in animations. The screen indicator strip is now always visible (was hidden on non-SAP tabs) so the gradient palette trigger is always discoverable. Replaces "scroll through 20+ chips" with "type two letters, hit Enter." No backend changes — all data flows from `SLASH_COMMANDS`, `BUILT_IN_TEMPLATES`/`getTemplates()`, and `loadHistory()`.
- **v0.15.0** — **Pre-dispatch parts check + PM kit gap detector.** Two new slash commands close the loop on the user's "grey stock + surprise-billable items" pain points. **`/parts-check`** is the dispatcher's pre-flight: it infers the parts the tech will likely need from the resolutions of similar past tickets (vector search via `find_similar_tickets` → resolution notes via `get_ticket_notes`, parallel fan-out to the top 5 similar cases), asks the LLM to extract a structured `{ inferredParts[], summary }` ranked by recurrence (appeared in ≥3 of 5 = high confidence, 2 = medium, 1 = low), then cross-checks each inferred part against warehouse stock via `search_parts`. Renders as a card stack with a confidence badge + a colored stock chip (✅ in stock with qty/UOM, 🟡 low: <3, 🔴 out of stock, ⚪ not in catalog) and a footer nudge that focuses the dispatcher on the actionable subset ("3 out of stock — order or substitute before dispatch" vs. "✅ All parts in stock — safe to dispatch"). **`/pm-kit-check`** is the surprise-billable detector — addresses the user's "Needle + Seat" example where the PM instructions call for a part that isn't in the standard PM kit. Pulls KB articles + the most recent past PM resolutions for this product (in parallel), feeds them to the LLM with a strict JSON spec requesting `{ required[], inKit[], gaps[], summary }` where each gap carries `severity` (high/med/low) + `billableImpact` + `evidence`. Renders three labelled sections — **GAPS — KIT DOESN'T COVER** first (severity-colored cards, with billable impact + evidence quoted), then **REQUIRED BY PM INSTRUCTIONS**, then **IN STANDARD PM KIT**. High-severity gap count surfaces in a footer nudge that pushes the agent toward SVA-check or upfront-customer-warning. Two new built-in templates: **🔧 Parts check** and **🛡 PM kit gaps** (ticket-screen only). Mirrored in `options.js` BUILT_IN_TEMPLATES_OPTIONS. `/help` text + slash chip order + textarea placeholder updated. Both panels also feed plain-text variants into the Copy / Draft action row so the dispatcher can paste the result into a comment if they want a permanent record.
- **v0.14.1** — **`/skills` now groups skills by category + ranks them by transaction type**. The formatter was previously truncating to the top 5 matching skills per tech (missing the long tail) and didn't distinguish skill *types* — a tech with 12 REPAIR skills and 3 PM skills looked the same as one with the inverse. New `SCREEN_SKILL_PRIORITY` table defines the action-category order per screen: **Ticket** → REPAIR / QUAL / INSTALL / PM / OTHER; **Contract** → PM / QUAL / INSTALL / REPAIR / OTHER; **Opportunity** → INSTALL / QUAL / PM / REPAIR / OTHER; **Account** → balanced. Each tech is rendered with one line per action category, top 2 categories **bolded**, up to 8 skills per category (was 5), with "+N more" if longer. Action emojis added (🛡 PM / 🔧 REPAIR / 📦 INSTALL / ✅ QUAL / ⭐ OTHER) for visual scanning. The header now shows which categories are being emphasised ("ranked for **Contract (PMs)** context — top: 🛡 PM + ✅ QUAL"). `/skills` is now also available on **Contract** and **Account** screens (was ticket-only); on Contract screens the hint string injects "PM preventive maintenance" so the LLM matcher leans toward PM-tagged skills.
- **v0.14.0** — **On-site check-in workflow.** New stateful check-in bar at the top of the panel — visible only on ticket screens. Idle state: *"📍 Check in to &lt;customer&gt; site — starts time-on-site + shows nearby PMs"*. Click → auto-fires `/nearby` (so bundling opportunities surface the moment the tech walks in the door) AND starts a per-ticket time-on-site timer. Active state: *"📍 On-site at &lt;customer&gt; — 1h 12m elapsed"* with live counter (refreshes every 30s) and a **Check out** button. State persisted in `chrome.storage.session` keyed by ticket ID (`c4s.check-in.<id>`) so it survives panel focus changes / refreshes. On check-out, the elapsed wall-clock duration is stashed as `c4s.last-visit-duration` and the `/close-out` workflow auto-picks it up — its "Time on ticket" line reads `"1h 12m (recorded on-site via Check-in)"` instead of the SAP lifecycle-date computation. Three benefits in one button: bundling opportunities appear automatically, schedule actuals get captured (addressing the user's "low confidence in planned durations" pain point), and the close-out report's time field is real wall-clock measurement, not a derived guess.
- **v0.13.0** — **`/nearby` bundling opportunities.** New slash command that lists all OTHER open tickets at the same customer as the one currently open, with PMs (Preventive Maintenance) ranked first as the highest-leverage bundling candidates. Reuses the multi-clause-OR sequential `search_tickets` pattern from `/visits` (one filter per API call to dodge SAP's OData OR-chain limit), with each filter adding `ServiceRequestUserLifeCycleStatusCode ne '4'` to scope to open work. PM heuristics — a ticket counts as PM-shaped when `ProcessingTypeCode` starts with `ZPM`/`ZPRV`/`ZQUL`/`PM`, its `ProcessingTypeCodeText` mentions "preventive/maintenance/planned", or its title contains "PM", "Preventive", "Maintenance", "Calibration", "Inspection", "Qualification", or "Service Plan" / "Scheduled Service". Each card renders with a color-coded due-date badge (🔴 overdue, 🟡 due in ≤14d, 🟢 due in ≤60d, ⚪ no due date) based on `CompleteDuedatetimeContent` or `RequestedFulfillmentPeriodEndDateTime`. Footer nudge summarises the bundling opportunity ("3 overdue PMs + 2 due in next 30 days — talk to dispatcher about adding to today's visit"). New built-in template **📍 Nearby PMs**.
- **v0.12.0** — **Close-out workflow** — addresses the "grey stock + missing service history + KB-quality gap" trifecta in one new slash command. `/close-out` reads the ticket's existing notes (work descriptions, internal comments, case description), computes time-on-ticket from the OData `RequestInitialReceiptdatetime`/`RequestCloseddatetime` fields, and asks the LLM to synthesise a structured Service Response Report with sections **PROBLEM**, **DIAGNOSTIC STEPS**, **PARTS USED**, **ROOT CAUSE**, **RESOLUTION**, **FOLLOW-UP**, **TIME ON TICKET**. The result renders in the reply box as an editable monospace textarea with 4 action buttons: **💾 Save to ticket** (writes back as TypeCode 10022 via `add_service_request_text` with `dryRun:false, acknowledgeProduction:true`), **📋 Copy as KB seed** (clipboard), **🔄 Regenerate** (re-runs the LLM), **✕ Close** (dismiss). New `✅ Close-out` built-in template. New `parseOdataDateToMs()` + `formatHoursDuration()` helpers (handles `/Date(123…)/` and ISO formats, formats as `"1d 4h 12m"`). Designed so the tech doesn't retype anything — they verify, edit, save. Captures parts inference (text form) into the same note that goes back to SAP, so grey stock surfaces in the service record even if the standalone `log_service_item` writeback isn't wired up yet.
- **v0.11.0** — **Customer Pulse.** New `/pulse` slash command + `💚 Customer Pulse` built-in template that surfaces a one-screen customer health snapshot for the agent BEFORE they open the conversation. Pulls the customer's recent ticket history (same multi-clause OR pattern as `/visits` to dodge SAP's OData OR-chain limit) and notes for the 3 most recent closed tickets (sentiment signal), composes a structured LLM prompt with strict JSON output spec, parses `{ score, label, trend, drivers[], suggestions[] }`, and renders to a dedicated `#pulse-card` at the top of the panel — separate from the reply box, so it co-exists with `/triage` and `/knowledge` replies. Color-coded by score (🟢 Healthy / 🟡 At Risk / 🔴 Unhappy / ⚪ Cold), trend arrow (↑ improving / → stable / ↓ declining), 3-5 driver bullets ("Why"), and 3-4 personalised handling suggestions ("Handle this ticket better"). Per-customer cache in `chrome.storage.session` (2-hour TTL) so the LLM call doesn't fire on every ticket re-open. New `c4s.auto-pulse` setting (Off / Once-per-ticket / Always) mirrors auto-triage + auto-KB; pulse runs INDEPENDENTLY of those (different render target) so all three can auto-fire on ticket open. Cleared automatically on context-change (different customer/ticket) or manual Clear button.
- **v0.10.0** — **Contract-screen detection + templates.** Fixed the bug where a SAP `ServiceContract` page (e.g. "Contract 281527 — 1YR EXT WARR PROFLEX 384-WD") was misclassified as a ticket because its title matched the `<digits> - <subject>` pattern. New `SCREEN_STRONG_KEYWORDS` table is checked BEFORE the digit-dash fallback — patterns like `^\s*contract\s+\d{4,10}\s*[-—:]/i` and `^\s*service\s*contract\b/i` catch contracts; similar entries exist for opportunities, leads, and activities. Screen indicator now shows "Screen: 📋 Contract" with the right emoji. Init flow reordered: screen detection runs BEFORE entity-ID detection, so the prefill labels are screen-aware ("Summarize contract 281527" vs. "Summarize ticket 3553044"). `dataset.detectedTicket` is only stamped on real ticket screens — contracts/opportunities/leads get `dataset.detectedEntity` + `detectedEntityKind` instead, so auto-triage / `/triage` / Apply-buttons don't fire against non-ticket IDs. Added 4 contract-screen templates: **📋 Coverage analysis**, **📅 Renewal check**, **✉️ Draft renewal outreach**, **🔗 Linked tickets** — all plain-text LLM prompts that work from the scraped page context (no new shim tools needed). Also added contract field hints (`Begins On`, `Ends On`, `External Reference`, `Coverage`) to the existing `SCREEN_FIELD_HINTS` table as a secondary signal.
- **v0.9.9** — **New AI sparkle icon.** Replaced the V mark with a 4-point sparkle ✨ design in `icons/generate.mjs`. Same dark rounded-square tile and cyan→pink gradient palette; the gradient now flows diagonally across the sparkle so the top-left tip reads cyan and the bottom-right tip reads pink. Two smaller satellite sparkles (cyan in top-left corner, pink in bottom-right) sit at 48px+ for visual rhythm; suppressed at 16px to keep the toolbar icon crisp. The sparkle geometry uses a polar-coordinate test (max permitted distance interpolates from R on each cardinal axis to a thin waist at 45°) which gives the classic sharp-tipped ✨ silhouette rather than a rounded 4-petal flower.
- **v0.9.8** — **Fix `Annotate failed: Cannot access contents of url … Extension manifest must request permission to access this host`** on SAP C4C tenants. The manifest had `*.crm.ondemand.com` in `content_scripts.matches` (so `content.js` auto-injects), but `chrome.scripting.executeScript()` (used by the 🪄 Annotate button + the all-frames scrape) needs the host in `host_permissions` OR an `activeTab` user-gesture grant. The side panel doesn't grant `activeTab` (only toolbar-icon clicks, keyboard shortcuts, and context-menu items do), so injecting from the panel failed on any SAP host. Added `*.crm.ondemand.com`, `*.crm.sap.com`, `*.c4c.sap.com`, `*.cloudfoundry.io` to `host_permissions`. **On reload, Chrome will disable the extension and prompt the user to re-enable it with the new host permissions** — that's the standard MV3 flow for permission bumps; just click "Enable" at `chrome://extensions/`.
- **v0.9.7** — **Unified labelled-button row.** The duplicate `/triage /knowledge /visits ...` slash-chip row is now hidden via CSS (`#slash-chips { display: none }`). Every slash command now has a corresponding labelled-button template (🔧 Parts lookup, 📜 Service history, 📝 Notes, ❓ All commands added), so all command discovery happens through the bigger template chips. Power users can still type `/triage` etc. in the textarea — the slash parser is unchanged. Also attempts to suppress the V favicon in the detached popup's title bar via a transparent SVG `<link rel="icon">` (works on some Chrome builds; the title-bar icon is otherwise unmovable on detached popup windows because Chrome falls back to the extension's `default_icon`).
- **v0.9.6** — `/skills` now uses **the same tool path as the Skills panel**. v0.9.4's inline-arg branch (`/skills <name>`) called `search_employees` which builds an OData filter on the employee Name field — SAP C4C's OData v2 parser then rejects the request with *"Operands of logical operator '' are not valid"* (same OR-chain limit we hit on `/visits`). Both branches now call `get_employee_skills` with a hint string — for `/skills` (no arg) the hint is `[ticket.title, ticket.product, ticket.serial].join(" ")` mirroring SkillsPanel's `hint`, and for `/skills <name>` the hint is the name itself. The result is filtered client-side by name when the user provided one. Also fixed `extractEmployeeList()` to read the real `result.technicians[]` shape (was looking at `employees[]`/`results[]`/`matches[]` which never matched), and rewrote `formatEmployeeProfiles()` for the real shape: `{ id, name, salesOrg, skills[{skillId, action, equipment}], matchingSkills[], actionCounts{PM, REPAIR, INSTALL, QUAL} }`. New `skillToString()` helper renders rich skill entries as `"REPAIR: Helios NanoLab"`. When the LLM matcher returned `keywordsUsed`, those are surfaced above the results as a "Matched on keywords" prefix.
- **v0.9.5** — **Cleaner detached-popup header.** The popup window opened by the in-page floating "✨ Ask C4S" button (which has Chrome's OS-level title bar above with the extension icon + "Ask C4S") now hides the duplicate "Ask C4S" text in our internal gradient header — the OS title bar carries it. Adds two new in-header buttons in detached-popup mode: **↗** opens the same panel content in Chrome's side panel (preserves the user gesture via the same path as the existing "Side panel" footer link), and **✕** closes the detached window via `window.close()`. The OS title bar (with V icon, pin, X) is a Chrome popup-window constraint — extensions can't replace it without using the deprecated `chrome.app.window` API. The buttons are CSS-hidden in side-panel and toolbar-popup modes. Document title is also shortened to "C4S" or "C4S — &lt;ticketId&gt;" when a ticket is detected, so multiple open windows are distinguishable in the title bar.
- **v0.9.4** — **Technician skill matching** via the existing shim tools `get_employee_skills` (which queries ZSDK_EMPLOYEESKILLS or whatever skills entity your tenant uses) and `search_employees`. New `/skills` slash command with two modes: **no arg** + on a ticket → finds the top 8 technicians whose ZSDK_EMPLOYEESKILLS profile matches the ticket's `ProductID`; **with an arg** (`/skills Scott Cochran`) → looks up that specific employee and dumps their skill profile. Renders as a ranked markdown list with match score (when present), region/territory, recent-resolution count, and the top 8 skills. New built-in templates **👤 Match technician** (replaces the older Assign-Technician-only prompt that only had on-screen visibility) and **👤 Skill match** (universal ticket-screen template). Helpers: `extractEmployeeList()` (tolerates `employees[]` / `results[]` / `matches[]` / `d.results[]` shapes), `formatEmployeeProfiles()` (handles string-or-object skill entries and synthesises a `✓ Has skill matching product "X"` flag).
- **v0.9.3** — `/knowledge` now shows **inline resolutions** within each ticket card — Service Response Report (TypeCode 10022), Case Description (10004), and Internal Comments (10003) — matching the web panel's layout. After fetching serial/product histories, the handler picks the top 3 closed tickets from each section and parallel-fetches their notes via `get_ticket_notes`. Each note renders as a left-bordered sub-card under its ticket; the **Service Response Report** note gets an amber accent (💡 icon + gradient background) so the resolution stands out. The "RESOLUTION AVAILABLE" badge is now driven by the actual presence of a 10022 note rather than just `Closed` status (more accurate). Notes are sorted by TypeCode priority (resolution first, then descriptions, then comments) and capped at 4 per card. New helpers: `fetchNotesForTickets()`, `renderNoteCard()`, `selectNotesForCard()`.
- **v0.9.2** — **Full knowledge view** — new `/knowledge` slash command that mirrors the web panel's rich "past tickets on this serial + same-product closures + KB articles + external KB search shortcuts" layout. Fetches three data sources in parallel: `get_serial_history` (top 6 on this equipment serial), `search_tickets` filtered by `ProductID + status=Closed` (top 8 same-product closures by other engineers), and `search_kb_for_ticket` (internal KB articles). Renders as structured HTML cards with status/resolution badges instead of plain markdown — separate `kind: "knowledge-panel"` result type bypasses the markdown renderer and builds the multi-section view directly (all text HTML-escaped at render time). Five **External KB search** shortcuts (Symptom keywords / Symptom+Product / Ticket title / Product only / Serial number) render as clickable cards that open a customer-configurable URL template in a new tab. New Options field "External KB search → URL template" — set to your portal URL with `{query}` placeholder; blank hides the section. New built-in template **🗂 Full knowledge view** maps to `/knowledge`. Helpers added: `extractTicketList()`, `extractKbResult()`, `extractSymptomKeywords()`, `renderKnowledgePanel()`, `renderTicketCard()`, `renderKbCard()`, `renderExternalSearchCard()`, `isTicketClosed()`.
- **v0.9.1** — **Auto-fetch KB articles on ticket open**. New Options setting `c4s.auto-kb` (Off / Once-per-ticket / Always) mirrors the auto-triage UX — when a ticket is detected, the panel automatically runs `/kb-ticket` and surfaces related KB articles without any operator typing. Mutually exclusive with auto-triage: if both are enabled, **auto-triage wins** and auto-KB is skipped (so they don't fight for the reply box). Same session-storage-based de-dup as auto-triage (`c4s.auto-kb-seen`) — won't re-fire for tickets you've already KB-fetched this hour. Reuses the existing auto-triage banner with a dynamic label that switches to "Fetching related KB articles…" during the call. The `init()` sequencer first reads the auto-triage setting + session-de-dup to predict whether triage will fire, then conditionally calls auto-KB after.
- **v0.9.0** — **Knowledge base integration**. Two new slash commands surface the shim's existing `search_kb_articles` and `search_kb_for_ticket` tools: `/kb <query>` for free-text search across the KB (universal — works on any screen), and `/kb-ticket` which uses the current ticket's product/symptom/title as the query (ticket-screen only). Results render as a markdown list — each entry is `[title](url)` (clickable if the KB provides a URL), excerpt, and KB article ID. Two new built-in templates (**📚 KB for this ticket**, **📚 Search KB**) wire to those commands. New right-click context menu *"Search C4S knowledge base for `<selection>`"* — select any text on any web page, right-click, the panel opens with `/kb <selection>` pre-filled. The slash chip row, `/help`, and textarea placeholder are all updated. New `extractKbArticles()` helper handles three response shapes the KB tools return (`articles[]`, `perQuery[].articles[]`, OData `d.results[]`), with dedup by article ID across query buckets.
- **v0.8.0** — **SLA watcher with desktop notifications + toolbar badge**. Background service worker uses `chrome.alarms` to poll the shim's `get_sla_at_risk` tool on a schedule (default every 15 min, user-configurable 5-60 min). Per cycle: toolbar icon shows a red badge with the count of at-risk tickets; new tickets that crossed the threshold get a native desktop notification ("⚠ Ticket 3553044 — SLA at risk") with the title, customer, and priority. Click the notification → side panel opens with `/triage <ticketId>` pre-filled. De-duped via `chrome.storage.local` — same ticket never gets re-notified, and notifications auto-clear when the ticket de-risks or closes. New permissions `notifications` + `alarms`. New Options section "SLA Watcher" with: enable toggle (default off), hours-ahead horizon (1-168h, default 24), polling interval (5-60 min, default 15). Settings changes hot-reload the alarm via `chrome.storage.onChanged`. Notifications use the existing 128px brand icon; click handler tolerates `sidePanel.open()` gesture restrictions by falling back to opening a popup window when needed.

## Troubleshooting

### *"Executing inline script violates the following Content Security Policy directive 'script-src 'self' ...'"* on `options.html`

This was a v0.3.0 bug. The placeholder OAuth client_id (`REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`) caused `chrome.identity.getAuthToken` to bounce through a Chrome-internal OAuth page that contains an inline script — and Chrome attributes the violation to whichever extension page invoked it. Fixed in v0.3.1: the Sign-in button is now disabled until you wire up a real client ID per the *"Setting up Google SSO"* section above. If you still see this on v0.3.1+, double-check that you've reloaded the extension at `chrome://extensions/` after editing `manifest.json`.

### *"Uncaught Error: Extension context invalidated."*

You reloaded the extension at `chrome://extensions/` while the popup or options page was still open. The page is holding stale `chrome.*` references that no longer exist. Close and reopen the page (or hit the *"reload this page"* link in the in-page banner) — that re-binds to the freshly-loaded extension. This is normal Chrome behaviour during extension dev; nothing in the extension itself is broken.

### *"Ticket ID or ObjectID:"* prompt keeps firing even though the ticket is visible on the page

Up to v0.4.2 this could happen on the modern SAP C4C HTML5 client because the URL encodes the ticket in a base64 fragment (`#Nav/1/eyJ0...`) and the page's first `<h1>` is often "Home", not the ticket title. v0.4.3 fixes both: it base64-decodes the URL fragment AND scrapes every likely SAP heading element, not just the first H1. If you still see the prompt:

1. **Reload the extension** at `chrome://extensions/` (the `Reload` button), then **close and reopen the side panel** — content scripts are only re-injected on the next page load, so you may also need to refresh the SAP tab.
2. **Open DevTools on the side panel** (right-click in the panel → Inspect) and check the Console. v0.4.3 logs `[askc4s] resolveTicketId: ...` at each detection step. The log shows the scraped titles, body-text length, and which (if any) extractor matched. Common failure modes:
   - *"content script may not be injected on this URL"* → the SAP page was loaded before the extension was installed; refresh the SAP tab.
   - *"extraction found nothing"* + `titlesSample: ["Home", "..."]` → none of the scraped titles had a ticket-shaped number. Copy the `titlesSample` log into a bug report — we can add the missing selector.
3. **Type the ticket ID into the textarea** explicitly (e.g. `/triage 3553044`) — the slash-handler always honours an explicit arg.
